## 1. Question

The maintained quantitative paid-row producer selects one supported source
clock.  Its stopping-law atom can tend to zero even when the row gain and
joint reach have fixed lower bounds.  In the later-receiving orientation this
prevents the selected row from carrying fixed actual absorption.

Can one retain the full source stopping law, use the paid orientation before
selecting an atom, and obtain a source-uniform causal passport?

The answer is yes at the level of complete terminal laws.  The key is to
split the **total near-cap gain**, not a chosen first disagreement.

## 2. Aggregate orientation lemma

Fix a finite quitting game with `n` players and rewards bounded by `M>0`.
Fix an actual behavioral profile `sigma` and player `i`.  Write

\[
 C=B_i(\sigma),\qquad U=U_i(\sigma),\qquad d=C-U>0.
\]

Let `mu` be player `i`'s actual stopping law and put

\[
 v(q)=U_i(\sigma[i\leftarrow q])
 \qquad(q\in\mathbb N\cup\{\infty\}).
\]

Thus `U=E_mu v` and `C=sup_q v(q)`.  Choose `0<eta<d` and one pure clock `r`
with

\[
 v(r)>C-\eta.
\tag{2.1}
\]

Let `P=sigma[i<-r]`.  Then at least one of the following holds.

1. **Prescribed earlier-source mass.**  The complete terminal law of `sigma`
   has a finite coalition `S` containing `i` with

   \[
   \mu_\sigma(S)>
   {d-\eta\over 2^{n+1}M}.
   \tag{2.2}
   \]

2. **Near-cap earlier-receiving mass.**  The clock `r` is finite, the literal
   response profile `P` has a terminal coalition `S` containing `i` whose
   stage mass at `r`, and hence complete terminal-law mass, satisfies

   \[
   \Pr_P(S\text{ at }r)>
   {d-\eta\over 2^{n+1}M},
   \tag{2.3}
   \]

   and

   \[
   d_i(P)<\eta.
   \tag{2.4}
   \]

For Fin4 the denominator in (2.2)--(2.3) is `32 M`.

### Proof

Self-cap invariance gives `B_i(P)=B_i(sigma)=C`, so (2.1) immediately gives
(2.4).  Also

\[
 v(r)-\mathbb E_\mu v>d-\eta.
\tag{2.5}
\]

Delete the nonpositive summands in (2.5) and split the positive summands
according to the temporal orientation of `q` and `r`:

\[
 G_<:=\sum_{q<r}\mu(q)(v(r)-v(q))_+,
 \qquad
 G_>:=\sum_{r<q}\mu(q)(v(r)-v(q))_+.
\tag{2.6}
\]

The equal-clock summand is zero, and every two distinct optional pure times
have exactly one orientation.  Hence

\[
G_<+G_>>d-\eta.
\tag{2.7}
\]

At least one term is larger than `(d-eta)/2`.

For `q<r`, the source clock Quits first.  The exact first-disagreement
identity and reward boundedness give

\[
 (v(r)-v(q))_+\le 2M L_q,
\tag{2.8}
\]

where `L_q` is the opponents' survival to `q`.  Therefore

\[
G_<\le 2M\sum_{q<r}\mu(q)L_q.
\tag{2.9}
\]

The terms on the right are disjoint actual absorption events of `sigma`: the
observer's prescribed clock is the finite time `q`, all opponents survive
strictly before `q`, and the terminal coalition at `q` contains `i`.  Thus if
the `G_<` arm is larger than `(d-eta)/2`, their total mass is larger than
`(d-eta)/(4M)`.  There are `2^(n-1)` coalitions containing `i`, so one fixed
coalition satisfies (2.2).

For `r<q`, the receiving clock Quits first.  Its opponent survival is the
single number `L_r`, independent of `q`, and

\[
 (v(r)-v(q))_+\le2ML_r.
\tag{2.10}
\]

Consequently `G_> <= 2 M L_r`.  If this is the large arm, then

\[
L_r>{d-\eta\over4M}.
\tag{2.11}
\]

This arm is empty when `r=Never`, so `r` is finite.  In `P` the observer Quits
surely at `r`; its total stage absorption at that row is exactly `L_r`.
Pigeonholing the same `2^(n-1)` observer-containing coalitions proves (2.3).
The checked stage-to-terminal-law inequality then retains the bound in the
complete law.  This proves the dichotomy. `QED`

The point of (2.6) is that no lower bound on an individual prescribed
stopping-law atom is needed.  A diffuse family of later-receiving source rows
contributes through the sum in (2.9).

## 3. Uniform Fin4 killed-coordinate/atom consequence

Let `sigma_k` be actual Fin4 profiles with

\[
d_i(\sigma_k)\ge D_*/4,
\qquad D_*>0,
\tag{3.1}
\]

and let `eta_k` decrease to zero, eventually with `eta_k<=D_*/8`.  Apply
Section 2 using an `eta_k`-near-cap pure response `r_k`, and write
`P_k=sigma_k[i<-r_k]`.  After passing to one of the two arms and selecting
one of the eight coalition labels, there is a fixed finite coalition `S`
containing `i` such that either

\[
\mu_{\sigma_k}(S)>{D_*\over256M}
\tag{3.2}
\]

cofinally, or

\[
\mu_{P_k}(S)>{D_*\over256M},
\qquad d_i(P_k)<\eta_k\longrightarrow0.
\tag{3.3}

Every `P_k` is a literal one-player complete behavioral replacement against
the unchanged opponents.  No cap attainment is assumed.

Now specialize `sigma_k` to the quantitative full-replacement profiles of
one tangent endpoint.  In the prescribed arm, joint semantic/law
compactification retains the endpoint mover's checked zero debt and the
positive `S` atom.  In the response arm, compactification retains zero debt
of the paid observer and the same positive `S` atom.  Thus every such family
has the exhaustive source-attached output

\[
\boxed{
\text{off/minimum cluster with a positive finite atom and at least one
killed debt coordinate}.}
\tag{3.4}
\]

The killed label is the endpoint mover in the prescribed arm and the paid
observer in the response arm.  They need not coincide, and the response may
reactivate the mover's debt.  Accordingly (3.4) is not yet a support-rank
decrease.

## 4. Debt-normalized atom retention under the exact cap lift

There is a compatible nonresetting passport for the finite atom.

Let `X` be an actual profile with total semantic debt `D(X)>0`, terminal law
`mu_X`, and let `X^N` be any finite exact cap-prefix profile built over `X`.
Write `s_N` for the joint probability of reaching the literal suffix `X`.
For every finite terminal coalition `S`, literal prefix decomposition gives

\[
\mu_{X^N}(S)\ge s_N\mu_X(S).
\tag{4.1}
\]

The exact cap-prefix debt identity gives

\[
D(X^N)=s_ND(X).
\tag{4.2}
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
{\mu_{X^N}(S)\over D(X^N)}
\ge {\mu_X(S)\over D(X)}.}
\tag{4.3}
\]

Let a subsequence of the complete semantic/law profiles converge to `(Z,nu)`.
Since the global minimum is positive, `D(Z)>=D_*>0`; passing to the limit in
(4.1)--(4.2) yields

\[
\nu(S)\ge {D(Z)\over D(X)}\mu_X(S).
\tag{4.4}

Suppose now that each limit is causalized as the complete next source without
changing its semantic pair or law.  Iterating (4.4) through any finite chain
of exact cap-lift transitions telescopes:

\[
\mu_N(S)
\ge \mu_0(S)\prod_{m<N}{D_{m+1}\over D_m}
=\mu_0(S){D_N\over D_0}
\ge\mu_0(S){D_*\over D_0}.
\tag{4.5}

This survives quantitative debt descent, arbitrarily small Zeno descent, and
the inert mode (where the ratio is one).  It is invariant under
source-faithful causalization because the complete endpoint law is part of
the regenerated source.  It is not invariant under unrelated complement
Nashification or a newly selected horizontal response; those operations are
outside the hypothesis of (4.5).

Thus finite-atom mass no longer resets along the maintained exact cap-lift
transition.  What remains is to consume a positive-atom source whose debt
stays uniformly off the minimum fibre, or prove that the descending chain
lands at `D_*`.  In the latter case (4.5) gives exactly the positive finite
law coordinate required by the existing minimum-atom causalization and
producer atlas.

## 5. Killed-face saturation and the exact active-face wall

The output of Section 3 supplies exactly the downstream premise that was
missing from the killed-face saturation in
`CODEX_SOURCE_GATE__OFFMINIMUM_POSITIVE_ROOT_ZENO_FACE_SATURATION.md`.
Fix its killed label `p`, origin `z_0=(X_0,mu_0)`, and retained atom `S`, and
intersect the closed law-tight `p`-face cap--Nash saturation hull with

\[
 D(X_0)\mu(S)\ge D(X)\mu_0(S).
\tag{5.1}
\]

This intersection is still nonempty, compact, closed under every exact
cap--Nash semantic/law prefix, and downward same-law closed inside `d_p=0`.
At a debt-minimizer `(X_H,mu_H)` it gives

\[
 d_p(X_H)=0,
 \qquad
 \mu_H(S)\ge {D_*\over D(X_0)}\mu_0(S)>0,
\tag{5.2}
\]

and exactly one of the following numerical outcomes.

1. `D(X_H)=D_*`.  This is a same-point minimum-law regeneration with the
   killed coordinate and positive finite atom retained.
2. `D_*<D(X_H)`.  All Continue is the unique exact cap--Nash root at `X_H`,
   and its semantic/law prefix fixes the point.

Thus the aggregate paid orientation plus killed-face saturation consumes all
finite, Zeno, and limit/restart exact-prefix descendants.  It reduces the
strict residual to the neutral minimum face; it does not itself consume that
face.

There is a useful arm-sensitive strengthening in Fin4.  In the prescribed
arm the killed label is the endpoint mover `p`, while `S` contains the paid
observer `i` and the checked full-replacement packet gives `i != p`.
Therefore

\[
 \operatorname{OpponentIncidence}_{p,i}(\mu_H)
 \ge \mu_H(S)>0.
\tag{5.3}
\]

The full-debt-support and solo/Never chambers are impossible.  At a strict
hull minimum, the checked fixed-law reset dispatch applies to `(p,i)`.
Law-tightness and hull minimality force its returned point to have the same
law and the same total debt `D(X_H)`; the positive-absorption dynamic exit is
impossible because it would be another hull point with smaller debt.  Hence
the prescribed arm necessarily lands in the **same-law reset-rigid** branch.

In the near-cap response arm the killed label is `p=i`, and the retained
atom contains `i`.  If `S` contains another player, (5.3) holds with that
player.  More generally, either some finite positive-law atom contains an
opponent of `i`, in which case the same reset-rigid conclusion follows, or
the whole finite part of the law is supported only on `{i}` (with possible
Never mass).  Hence the strict response arm has exactly the two forms

\[
 \boxed{\text{same-law reset-rigid}\quad\text{or}\quad
        \{i\}\text{/Never}.}
\tag{5.4}
\]

This also answers the proposed active-face-rank check.  On the reset-rigid
branch the dispatch preserves the complete law, preserves `D`, and preserves
the killed owner.  Consequently

\[
 {\mu_{\rm returned}(S)\over D_{\rm returned}}
 = {\mu_H(S)\over D(X_H)},
\tag{5.5}
\]

with the same labels `(p,S)`.  The supported strict toggle is a static table
inequality on a positive atom; it is not a chronological transition and may
select the same labels again.  Therefore no rank built only from the killed
label, the retained coalition, its atom mass or normalized atom density, and
the all-Continue cap correspondence is forced to decrease.  The checked
`FinFourEventualAllContinueLocalRegression` shows these fields can coexist
with a literally constant maximal cap-prefix orbit (although that regression
has zero global minimum and is not a counterexample).

A member-leave toggle does transport a coalition under the corresponding
literal Never replacement, and an outsider-join toggle changes its terminal
reward.  But the reset packet gives neither a sign for the *whole behavioral
replacement payoff* nor retention of the killed coordinate under that
replacement.  This is the precise missing consumer: a source-attached
late-deviation theorem converting the supported toggle into a law-changing
chronological edge while charging all other terminal events, or a new
monotone field changed by that edge.

## 6. Exact two-event charge for a killed member-leave toggle

There is one nontrivial chronological consequence when the static toggle is
already aligned with the killed label.  It also exposes why arbitrary
supported toggles are insufficient.

Let `sigma` be an actual profile, let `j` be a player, and assume all terminal
rewards are in `[-M,M]`.  Couple `sigma` with the literal replacement
`sigma[j<-Never]` by first sampling `j`'s prescribed pure stopping clock.  For
finite `t` and `A` contained in the opponents of `j`, let `w_t(A)` be the
probability that

- `j`'s sampled clock is `t`;
- all opponents survive strictly before `t`; and
- exactly the opponent coalition `A` Quits at `t`.

For nonempty `A`, the two coupled terminal coalitions on this event are
`A insert j` and `A`.  For `A=empty`, write `h_t` for `j`'s conditional
continuation payoff after everybody has survived through `t` in the Never
replacement.  The stopping-law mixture identity and first-disagreement
partition give the exact ledger

\[
\begin{split}
 U_j(\sigma[j\leftarrow\mathrm{Never}])-U_j(\sigma)
  ={}&\sum_t w_t(\varnothing)
       \bigl(h_t-r_j(\{j\})\bigr)\\
   &+\sum_{\substack{T\ni j\\|T|\ge2}}
       \mu_\sigma(T)
       \bigl(r_j(T\setminus\{j\})-r_j(T)\bigr).
\end{split}
\tag{6.1}
\]

Here `sum_t w_t(empty)=mu_sigma({j})`.  Every displayed difference lies in
`[-2M,2M]`.  Let `d_j=B_j(sigma)-U_j(sigma)`.  Since the Never payoff is at
most the cap, (6.1) is at most `d_j`.

Now suppose a nonsingleton positive-law coalition `S` contains `j` and has
the strict member-leave margin

\[
 \delta=r_j(S\setminus\{j\})-r_j(S)>0.
\tag{6.2}
\]

Let `N` be the sum of the negative parts of every term of (6.1) except the
positive `S` term.  Then

\[
 \boxed{N\ge \delta\mu_\sigma(S)-d_j.}
\tag{6.3}
\]

Consequently, if `delta mu_sigma(S)>d_j`, there is a distinct coalition
`T` containing `j` with

\[
 \mu_\sigma(T)\ge
 {\delta\mu_\sigma(S)-d_j
  \over 2M(2^{n-1}-1)},
\tag{6.4}
\]

and one of two compensating meanings:

- if `|T|>=2`, then
  `r_j(T)>r_j(T erase j)`, the reverse strict membership orientation;
- if `T={j}`, a positive submass of singleton-`j` dates has
  `h_t<r_j({j})`, and its weighted deficit supplies the corresponding part
  of `N`.

Indeed, (6.3) follows by retaining the positive `S` summand in (6.1),
replacing every other summand by the negative of its negative part, and using
the cap upper bound.  There are at most `2^(n-1)-1` other coalition labels
containing `j`; each category contributes at most `2M mu_sigma(T)` to `N`.
Pigeonholing proves (6.4).  In particular, when `j` is killed,

\[
 \mu_\sigma(T)\ge
 {\delta\mu_\sigma(S)
  \over 2M(2^{n-1}-1)}.
\tag{6.5}
\]

For Fin4 the denominator is `14M`.  This is a genuine two-event collision
charge: a killed player's profitable leave on one positive atom cannot stand
alone.

The hypotheses are essential.  If the member toggler is not killed, its
positive debt can pay the entire amount in (6.3).  If `S={j}`, the static
comparison with the empty coalition uses the normalized empty-set reward,
whereas the literal Never replacement receives the actual future
continuation `h_t`; the claimed positive `S` summand need not exist.  For an
outsider join, the joining player would have to synchronize its Quit with a
terminal date generated by the opponents.  A complete terminal-law atom can
be spread over arbitrarily late dates and supplies no implementable pure
clock with a uniform collision mass.

Thus (6.3) does not yet rank the reset-rigid face.  The checked reset dispatch
does not say that its selected strict toggler equals its killed owner, and
the all-Continue fixed semantic/law prefix permits arbitrary chronological
delay without changing any of the static passport fields.  The exact next
lemma would have to force killed-toggle alignment or add a source-attached
synchronizer for outsider joins.

## 7. Exact reactivation ledger and a source-attached local regression

Even perfect chronological synchronization does not preserve the old killed
coordinate under a different player's response.  There is a universal but
nonvanishing leakage bound.  Suppose profiles `sigma` and `tau` differ only
in player `j`, and couple their stopping-clock laws with mismatch probability
`rho`.  For every other player `p`, every fixed response of `p` changes its
expected payoff by at most `2M rho`.  Taking suprema over all responses gives

\[
 |B_p(\tau)-B_p(\sigma)|\le2M\rho,
 \qquad
 |U_p(\tau)-U_p(\sigma)|\le2M\rho,
\]

and hence

\[
 \boxed{|d_p(\tau)-d_p(\sigma)|\le4M\rho.}
\tag{7.1}
\]

The responding player's cap is invariant under its own replacement.  If its
payoff gain is `g`, summing (7.1) therefore gives only

\[
 D(\tau)-D(\sigma)\le -g+4M(n-1)\rho.                   \tag{7.2}
\]

This cannot force descent from a positive gain alone.

The failure already occurs in a literal Fin4 pure-clock rectangle.  Fix
`K>g>0`.  Set every reward coordinate not listed below to zero, set every
reward of players 2 and 3 to zero, and define

\[
\begin{array}{c|rrrr}
 A & r_0(A)&r_1(A)&r_2(A)&r_3(A)\\ \hline
 \{1,2,3\}   &0&0&0&0\\
 \{0,1,2,3\}&0&0&0&0\\
 \{2,3\}     &-K&g&0&0\\
 \{0,2,3\}  &0&0&0&0.
\end{array}                                                   \tag{7.3}
\]

Let `sigma` be the profile in which the nonsingleton coalition
`S={1,2,3}` Quits surely at date zero and everyone is Never thereafter.
The exact pure-nonsingleton semantic formula

\[
 B_j=\max\{r_j(S\mathbin\cup\{j\}),r_j(S\setminus\{j\})\}
\tag{7.4}
\]

gives

\[
 U(\sigma)=(0,0,0,0),qquad d(\sigma)=(0,g,0,0),qquad
 D(\sigma)=g.                                             \tag{7.5}
\]

Thus player 0 is killed, the source law has the unit atom `S` carrying the
distinct positive-debt observer 1, and player 1 has the strict nonsingleton
member-leave margin

\[
 r_1(\{2,3\})-r_1(\{1,2,3\})=g.                         \tag{7.6}
\]

Replace player 1 literally by Never.  The response profile `tau` absorbs at
date zero on `T={2,3}`.  Again by (7.4),

\[
 U(\tau)=(-K,g,0,0),qquad d(\tau)=(K,0,0,0),qquad
 D(\tau)=K.                                               \tag{7.7}
\]

So the source-attached leave response gains exactly `g` and kills its own
coordinate, at a uniformly bounded date and without cap nonattainment, while
reactivating the old killed coordinate by `K`.  Taking `K>g` makes total debt
increase.  The source atom is transported pathwise to `T`, but the normalized
`S` passport is lost.

This regression has global minimum zero (the all-Never profile is diagonal),
so it is not a positive-minimum counterexample and does not refute a theorem
using additional hard-residual structure.  It does refute the strongest
local late-deviation claim from exactly the fields currently retained:
positive source atom, distinct killed label, strict supported leave toggle,
literal source chronology, exact cap response, and a newly killed responding
label do not imply old-face preservation or total-debt descent.  A valid
positive-minimum consumer must charge the reactivation term in (7.1) to an
additional hard-residual invariant; chronological synchronization alone is
insufficient.

The regression can be strengthened to pass four further candidate local
invariants simultaneously.  Fix also `a>0`, put `L=g+a`, retain `K>g`, and
define the table by the following membership rules.  Write again
`S={1,2,3}` and `T={2,3}`.

- Player 0 has singleton payoff `-L`.  For nonempty
  `A subset {1,2,3}`, set
  `r_0(S)=0`, `r_0(T)=-K`, set `r_0(A)=-L` for
  `A in {{1},{3},{1,3}}`, and set the other bases to zero.  Set the
  corresponding member values to `r_0(S insert 0)=0`,
  `r_0(T insert 0)=0`, and `r_0(A insert 0)=r_0(A)-1` on those three
  coalitions; the remaining member values may be zero.
- Player 1 has singleton payoff `-a`.  For every nonempty
  `A subset {0,2,3}`, put `b_1(T)=g` and `b_1(A)=-a` otherwise, set
  `r_1(A)=b_1(A)`, and set
  `r_1(A insert 1)=b_1(A)-h(A)`, where `h(T)=g` and `h(A)=1`
  otherwise.
- Player 2 has singleton payoff `-L`.  For nonempty
  `A subset {0,1,3}`, put `b_2({0})=-L` and `b_2(A)=0` otherwise,
  then set `r_2(A)=b_2(A)` and `r_2(A insert 2)=b_2(A)-a`.
- Player 3 has singleton payoff `-L`, has both
  `r_3({0})=r_3({0,3})=-L`, and has every other coordinate zero.

At the same pure source `S`, the payoff, cap, and debt vectors are

\[
 U=(0,0,-a,0),\qquad B=(0,g,0,0),\qquad d=(0,g,a,0),
 \qquad D=L.                                               \tag{7.8}
\]

The member-Never response still replaces `S` by `T`, gains `g` for player 1,
and gives

\[
 U'=(-K,g,-a,0),\qquad B'=(0,g,0,0),\qquad
 d'=(K,0,a,0),\qquad D'=K+a>D.                            \tag{7.9}
\]

This strengthened source has all of the following exact properties.

1. **Unit atom density:** its terminal law is `delta_S`.
2. **Origin-debt singleton moat:** for every player `i`,
   `B_i-r_i({i})>=L`, with equality for all four displayed choices.
3. **Punishment normality:** player 0 is punished by player 1 Quitting
   surely; players 1, 2, and 3 are punished by player 0 Quitting surely.
   In each case both the join and Continue payoffs are at most the punished
   player's own singleton payoff.
4. **Unique all-Continue cap root:** player 2 has strictly negative Quit
   endpoint at every root, so it Continues.  Player 1 likewise has strictly
   negative membership increments.  Once players 1 and 2 Continue, every
   possible opponent coalition seen by player 0 has a strictly negative endpoint;
   after players 0, 1, and 2 Continue, player 3's negative singleton defect
   forces it to Continue.
5. **Law-tight hull minimum:** among joint-carrier points with law `delta_S`,
   player 1's Never deviation forces debt at least `g` and player 2's Never
   deviation forces debt at least `a`; all other debts are nonnegative.
   Thus (7.8) is the unique same-law semantic point of debt at most `L`.
   Together with unique all-Continue exact prefixing, its law-tight
   cap--Nash saturation hull is the singleton containing this point.

The edge is literal and finite, so there is no carrier-seam cancellation to
charge.  Nevertheless the old killed coordinate reactivates by `K` and the
source atom disappears.  Hence singleton moat, punishment normality, positive
atom density, law-tight minimum, and exact seam provenance do not control
(7.1), even in combination.

The strengthened table still has global minimum zero: all Never is a
terminal Nash profile because every own singleton payoff is negative.  The
genuinely unused hypothesis is therefore the **positive global minimum/no-
uniform-payoff hard residual**, not any of the listed local passports.
The example does not instantiate every full-support or hard-residual field;
it isolates only the listed local invariants and must not be read as a
hard-residual regression.
Embedding (7.8)--(7.9) into that class would amount to producing a new
positive-minimum candidate and is not asserted.  A successful reactivation
charge must use the positive global lower bound in a way that is absent from
the current singleton moat and law-tight minimum statements.

There is one exact way in which the positive global minimum enters.  Let
`D_*` be the global carrier minimum, let `sigma` be actual with

\[
 \varepsilon=D(\sigma)-D_*\ge0,
\]

and let `tau` differ only in player `j`, whose payoff gain is
`g=U_j(tau)-U_j(sigma)`.  Self-cap invariance gives

\[
 d_j(\tau)-d_j(\sigma)=-g.
\]

Since `D(tau)>=D_*`, the other coordinates obey the **minimum reactivation
identity and floor**

\[
\begin{split}
 \sum_{p\ne j}\bigl(d_p(\tau)-d_p(\sigma)\bigr)
   &=D(\tau)-D(\sigma)+g\\
   &\ge g-\varepsilon.
\end{split}                                                \tag{7.10}
\]

Thus if \(g>\varepsilon\), some distinct player has

\[
 d_p(\tau)-d_p(\sigma)
 \ge {g-\varepsilon\over n-1}.                            \tag{7.11}
\]

At an exact minimum the whole responding gain must be paid by net debt
reactivation outside the responder.  Combining (7.10) with (7.1) also gives

\[
 g-\varepsilon\le4M(n-1)\rho.                            \tag{7.12}
\]

This is the strongest unconditional positive-minimum consequence of the
late replacement.  It turns leakage from an error into a compulsory transfer,
but still does not identify the recipient with the previously killed label.
The recipient can already have positive debt, and later minimum regeneration
can erase the increase.  Hence (7.10) supplies a quantitative directed
debt-transfer edge, not a nonresetting rank or terminal consumer.

### Renewable weighted-flow consequence

The exact content of repeated transfer edges can be stated without treating
profile siblings as chronology.  For a family of sources `sigma_n`, literal
one-player replacements `tau_n`, movers `j_n`, gains `g_n`, and source
excesses

\[
 \varepsilon_n=D(\sigma_n)-D_*,
 \qquad w_n=(g_n-\varepsilon_n)_+,
\]

put

\[
 \Delta_{n,p}=d_p(\tau_n)-d_p(\sigma_n),
 \qquad R_{n,p}=(\Delta_{n,p})_+.
\]

Equation (7.10) gives

\[
 \sum_{p\ne j_n}R_{n,p}\ge w_n.                          \tag{7.13}
\]

Thus

\[
 \sum_n w_n
 \le\sum_{j\ne p}\sum_{n:j_n=j}R_{n,p}.                 \tag{7.14}
\]

In Fin4, if the left side diverges, one fixed ordered mover/recipient pair
`(j,p)` has

\[
 \sum_{n:j_n=j}R_{n,p}=\infty.                           \tag{7.15}
\]

If instead every live edge has `w_n>=w_*>0`, one may choose a recipient with
`R_(n,p_n)>=w_*/3`, and some fixed ordered pair recurs infinitely often with
that same floor.  This is the maximal conclusion available from label
finiteness alone.

Suppose, additionally, that actual transition data really connect each
response to the next source.  Define its coordinate seam by

\[
 s_{n,p}=d_p(\sigma_{n+1})-d_p(\tau_n).
\]

Since every debt coordinate lies in `[0,2M]`, telescoping gives, for the fixed
recipient in (7.15),

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{\substack{n<N\\j_n=j}}R_{n,p}
 \le 2M+\sum_{n<N}(-\Delta_{n,p})_+
          +\sum_{n<N}(-s_{n,p})_+.}                     \tag{7.16}
\]

Therefore recurrent incoming paid transfer can persist only if player `p`
has infinite compensating debt decrease either on other literal response
edges or across the connecting seams.  This is a sharp reset-budget
dichotomy.  It is not yet a contradiction:

- a negative response term need not occur when `p` is the mover, so it need
  not be another paid edge with the opposite label;
- a regeneration seam selected only by carrier compactness has no public
  chronological cost; and
- when `sigma_(n+1)` and `tau_n` are independently selected siblings,
  `s_(n,p)` is only an algebraic comparison and (7.16) must not be advertised
  as an executable path ledger.

Even with genuine seams, the debt-flow equations alone allow exact rotation.
At the abstract ledger level, take four minimum sources with debt vectors

\[
 D_*e_0,\ D_*e_1,\ D_*e_2,\ D_*e_3
\]

cyclically.  At state `D_*e_j`, let mover `j` gain `D_*`, lose its whole debt,
and transfer exactly `D_*` to `j+1`; take the next source to be that response.
Then every source and response has total debt `D_*`, every excess is zero,
every transfer inequality is equality, seams vanish, and the directed
recipient graph is the four-cycle.  This is an exact algebraic regression
for the maintained debt identities, not a claimed semantic carrier or
quitting-game example.

Consequently a recurrent recipient cycle does not make cumulative gain
survive by itself.  A completion needs either a theorem charging the negative
terms in (7.16) to source-retained law/mark data, or an additional
game-specific potential that rules out the rotating ledger.  Constructing an
actual positive-minimum game realizing the rotation would be conjecture-level
new information and is not asserted here.

### Exact linked-sequence/compiler dichotomy

There is a clean conditional theorem which separates genuine compact
recurrence from the missing semantic adapter.  Let

\[
 R=\mathsf{quittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation}(r)
\]

and suppose there are floor-admissible states `z_n` and *genuinely linked*
finite paths

\[
 P_n:R.\mathsf{Path}(z_n,z_{n+1}).
\]

Put

\[
 x_n=z_n.1.1.1,
 \qquad a_n=P_n.\mathsf{chargeSum}.
\tag{7.17}
\]

Thus `a_n` is the sum of literal one-stage root absorption masses, not a
unilateral payoff gain, a debt transfer, or a stopping-law total-variation
distance.  Suppose also that `w_n,b_n>=0`, `lambda>0`, and

\[
 \lambda w_n\le a_n+b_n.                              \tag{7.18}
\]

If `sum_n w_n=infinity`, then the useful exhaustive alternative is

\[
 \sum_n a_n=\infty
 \quad\hbox{or}\quad
 \sum_n b_n=\infty.                                   \tag{7.19}
\]

Indeed, summability of both right-hand sequences would make `w_n` summable
by (7.18).  More quantitatively, with prefix sums `A_N,B_N,W_N`,

\[
 A_N\ge\lambda W_N-B_N.                               \tag{7.20}
\]

Consequently `sum b_n<infinity` suffices for `sum a_n=infinity`.  So does the
strict relative estimate

\[
 \limsup_N {B_N\over W_N}<\lambda                    \tag{7.21}
\]

along the eventually positive denominators.  This includes `B_N=o(W_N)`.

In the first arm of (7.19), the paths produce the exact checked near-return
consumer.  The only compact packet coordinate needed is the payoff projection

\[
 x_n\in
 \mathsf{quittingPunishmentFloorForwardCarrier}(r)
 =[-\mathsf{quittingRewardBound}(r),
     \mathsf{quittingRewardBound}(r)]^{\operatorname{Fin}4}.
\tag{7.22}
\]

Equip this finite product with
`d_infinity(x,y)=max_i |x_i-y_i|`.  It is compact.  For any endpoint tolerance
`delta>0`, compact recurrence applied to the divergent prefix clock
`A_N=sum_(n<N)a_n` gives `m<k` with

\[
 d_\infty(x_m,x_k)<\delta,
 \qquad 1\le A_k-A_m.                                 \tag{7.23}
\]

Concatenating `P_m,...,P_(k-1)` gives an exact `R.Path(z_m,z_k)` whose
`chargeSum` is `A_k-A_m`; (7.23) gives
`|x_m(i)-x_k(i)|<=delta` for every player.  Varying `delta` therefore defines
`QuittingPositiveCumulativeAdmissiblePayoffNearReturnFamily` with
`chargeFloor=1`.  Its checked consumer
`quittingGame_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_cumulativePayoffNearReturns`
produces a uniform-equilibrium payoff.  One could also use the checked
unbounded finite-prefix consumer directly.  No topology on behavioral
profiles, stopping laws, roots, killed labels, or marked passports is used or
needed for this recurrence step.

This theorem also pinpoints why literal response linkage is not yet the
missing result.  Even if `tau_n=sigma_(n+1)`, the semantic replacement
`sigma_n -> tau_n` has not been shown to be a path in `R`, and its moving-player
gain `g_n` (or transfer weight `w_n`) has not been shown to lower-bound the
literal absorption charge `a_n`.  Equality of response and next-source
profiles removes the algebraic seam `s_(n,p)`; it supplies neither of these
two adapter facts.  Thus compactness of the payoff cube cannot promote
divergent paid gain by itself.

The debt-side reprojection obstruction is exact as well.  For the recurrent
ordered pair in (7.15), write

\[
 Q_N^{\rm resp}=\sum_{n<N}(-\Delta_{n,p})_+,
 \qquad
 Q_N^{\rm seam}=\sum_{n<N}(-s_{n,p})_+,
 \qquad
 I_N=\sum_{\substack{n<N\\j_n=j}}R_{n,p}.
\]

Then (7.16) is equivalently the quantitative lower bound

\[
 Q_N^{\rm resp}\ge I_N-2M-Q_N^{\rm seam}.             \tag{7.24}
\]

Hence `I_N -> infinity` forces a nonsummable response-reset or source-
reprojection seam budget.  If `Q_N^seam=o(I_N)`, the response-reset budget
itself diverges and in fact

\[
 \liminf_N {Q_N^{\rm resp}\over I_N}\ge1.             \tag{7.25}
\]

This still is not admissible absorption charge.  To enter the compiler one
must prove an additional comparison of the form (7.18), with `a_n` the charge
of actual linked admissible paths and `b_n` precisely the uncharged
reset/reprojection loss.  Failure of that comparison, or divergence of its
`b_n` arm, is the isolated obstruction.  The rotating ledger above realizes
the debt-side reset arm with zero seams, so summable seams alone cannot close
the argument.

### Exact literal-spine adapter and a zero-charge paid regression

The weakest direct adapter from one behavioral response profile can be stated
exactly.  Let `tau` be a profile, let

\[
 V_t=U(\mathsf{allContinueSpine}(\tau,t)),
 \qquad q_t=\mathsf{liveRoot}(\tau,t),
\]

and fix `a<b`.  The semantic prefix identity already gives

\[
 V_t=F(q_t,V_{t+1}),                                  \tag{7.26}
\]

and every `V_t` is in the canonical reward box.  Reverse the finite segment:

\[
 \mathsf{value}(k)=V_{b-k},
 \qquad
 \mathsf{root}(k)=q_{b-k-1}\qquad(0\le k<b-a).
\tag{7.27}
\]

Then (7.26) is precisely the forward policy field of
`QuittingPunishmentFloorFinitePrefix`.  The only additional strategic fields
needed are

\[
 \mathsf{punishmentValue}_i\le V_b(i)\qquad\hbox{for every }i,
\tag{7.28}
\]

and

\[
 q_t\text{ is exact Nash against }V_{t+1}
 \quad(a\le t<b).                                    \tag{7.29}
\]

Under (7.28)--(7.29), (7.27) is an exact punishment-floor prefix and the
checked `quittingFinitePrefixAdmissiblePath` construction turns it into an
`R.Path`.  Its charge is exactly

\[
 \sum_{t=a}^{b-1}\mathsf{rootAbsorptionMass}(q_t).    \tag{7.30}
\]

Thus a usable paid-row adapter needs, in addition, a comparison between the
paid weight and (7.30).  These are genuinely missing fields.  A paid
first-disagreement row compares two complete pure-time deviations.  It does
not say that the receiving root is exact Nash against its own next suffix,
even in the observer coordinate, and it gives no floor for the prescribed
suffix payoff.  The checked cap-lift repairs exact Nash only against the
behavioral **cap** vector; that vector is not the common prescribed payoff in
(7.26).

If `tau`'s observer coordinate is literally the receiving pure-time witness,
that witness is finite, and its sure-Quit date is included in `[a,b)`, then
(7.30) is at least one.  (A paid-row witness by itself is counterfactual data
and does not assert this profile-coordinate equality.)  These extra facts
settle the charge field, but not (7.28)--(7.29).  The nonattained/Never branch
has no such consequence.  The following exact Fin4 regression shows that this
is not a technical omission.

Take

\[
 r_i(S)=-1
\]

for every nonempty coalition and every player.  Let `tau` be all Never and
let `sigma` differ only in player `j`, who Quits at date zero.  Then

\[
 U_j(\sigma)=-1,
 \qquad U_j(\tau)=0,
 \qquad B_j(\sigma)=B_j(\tau)=0.                     \tag{7.31}
\]

Thus `tau` is a literal source-attached exact best response of gain one, and
the two clocks `0` and `Never` give a paid first-disagreement row with
`liveMass=reachedGain=gain=1`.  On the response spine every live root is all
Continue.  Let `z` be the boxed state with payoff zero and stored
all-Continue root.  It is floor-admissible because

\[
 \mathsf{quittingPunishmentValue}_i
 \le\max\{r_i(\{i\}),0\}=0.
\]

Against tail payoff zero, Quit pays `-1` and Continue pays `0`, so the
all-Continue root is exact Nash and `0=F(q,0)`.  Hence `z -> z` is an exact
floor-admissible edge matching the literal response row, but

\[
 \mathsf{edgeCharge}
 =\mathsf{rootAbsorptionMass}(q)=0<1=\mathsf{gain}.
\tag{7.32}
\]

No positive constant can therefore compare arbitrary source-attached paid
gain with the absorption charge of its literal admissible response edge.
This regression has global minimum debt zero (indeed `tau` is terminal Nash),
so it is not advertised as a positive-minimum counterexample.

**Named formalization handoff.**  A narrow duplicate search found adjacent
but different packets: the constant-`-1` endpoint-bridge no-go in
`CODEX_STRENGTHEN__ENDPOINT_MATCHED_EXACT_REGENERATION.md`, the abstract
positive-actual-row embedding obstruction in
`TerminalSemanticLiteralSourceReturnNoGo.lean`, and the three-player diffuse
cap-defect example in `TerminalSemanticFixedTableCapDefectRegression.lean`.
None states the literal gain-one/zero-admissible-charge conjunction above.
The proposed internal Lean declaration name is

```text
FinFourPaidGainZeroAdmissibleChargeRegression
```

with the following exact payload: for the constant-`-1` Fin4 reward, construct
`sigma` (only `j` Quits at date zero), `tau` (all Never), a
`QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow reward tau j 1` whose witnesses are `0` and
`Never` and whose `liveMass` and `reachedGain` both equal one, and the zero
payoff/all-Continue carrier state `z`; prove the gain and equal-cap identities
in (7.31), `z R z`, and that the charged relation assigns this self-edge charge
zero.  The immediate corollary should say that no `lambda>0` uniformly bounds
`lambda * row.gain` by the charge of a literal response-spine admissible edge.
The statement must retain the hypothesis/conclusion that the example's global
minimum debt is zero.  This is a formalization handoff only, not an export
claim.

There is nevertheless an exact hard-residual compatibility statement already
checked.  For a `QuittingPaidCapLiftedSource`, whose data include a positive
global minimum, the `InertStall` arm of
`QuittingPaidCapLiftedSource.exactTrichotomy` has

\[
 \mathsf{totalAbsorption}=0
\]

while retaining at every prefix a losslessly shifted paid row with the full
original positive gain.  This is a conditional arm, not an instantiated game
(instantiating the positive-minimum hypothesis would decide the conjecture in
the negative).  It proves that the current hard-residual packet itself does
not eliminate the gain-with-zero-charge alternative.

Finally, replacing absorption charge by payoff gain in the near-return
consumer is not a formal weakening of the checked proof.  The lasso compiler
uses

\[
 c\le\sum q_t
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 {c\over1+c}\le1-\prod(1-q_t)                         \tag{7.33}
\]

to obtain the positive absorption denominator which pays for the closing
payoff seam.  Equation (7.32) rules out deriving (7.33) from paid gain.  A
`gainFloor` near-return theorem would therefore require a new semantic
argument, or an explicit gain-to-absorption alternative; it cannot be obtained
by changing the charge field of
`QuittingPositiveCumulativeAdmissiblePayoffNearReturnFamily`.  On the paid-row
branch such a new unrestricted uniform-payoff argument is exactly the missing
consumer, so treating it as an available generalized compiler would be
circular.

### Exact cap--Nash to prescribed-payoff residual

There is an exact rowwise calculation behind the preceding type mismatch.
Fix a semantic pair `(U,B)`, write

\[
 d_i=B_i-U_i\ge0,
\]

and let `q` be an exact Nash root against `B`.  For player `i`, set

\[
 x_i=q_i(\mathsf{Quit}),\qquad
 r_i=q_i(\mathsf{Continue}),\qquad
 c_i=\prod_{k\ne i}q_k(\mathsf{Continue}),\qquad
 s=r_i c_i,
\tag{7.34}
\]

where `s` is the joint Continue mass.  Let `Q_i` be the pure-Quit endpoint,
let `C_i^B` be the pure-Continue endpoint against `B`, and define the
Continue slack

\[
 \ell_i=C_i^B-Q_i.
\tag{7.35}
\]

Changing the tail from `B` to `U=B-d` does not change `Q_i`, while it changes
the Continue endpoint by exactly the opponents-only Continue mass:

\[
 Q_i^U=Q_i,\qquad C_i^U=C_i^B-c_i d_i,\qquad
 (Q_i^U-C_i^U)=c_i d_i-\ell_i.                 \tag{7.36}
\]

Exact cap Nash implies \(\ell_i\ge0\) whenever \(r_i>0\); if also
\(x_i>0\), then \(\ell_i=0\).  Therefore the least nonnegative support-local
tolerance needed
for coordinate `i` after reinterpreting the same root against `U` is

\[
 e_i=
 \begin{cases}
  (c_i d_i-\ell_i)_+,&r_i>0,\\
  0,&r_i=0.
 \end{cases}                                           \tag{7.37}
\]

The Quit-support inequality needs no new error: its cap endpoint difference
was nonnegative and (7.36) only increases it.  The ordinary probability-
weighted coordinate Nash defect is exactly

\[
 \delta_i(U,q)=r_i e_i\le r_i c_i d_i=s d_i.           \tag{7.38}
\]

Thus the least one-row tolerance for
`IsQuittingRootSupportApproxNash reward U epsilon q` is
\(\max_i e_i\), whereas the ordinary total Nash defect is
\(\sum_i r_i e_i\).  Neither expression contains the absorption factor
\(1-s\).  Notice especially
that if both actions of a positive-debt player are supported, then
\(\ell_i=0\) and (7.38) is an equality:

\[
 e_i=c_i d_i,qquad \delta_i=s d_i.                    \tag{7.39}
\]

Now apply this to the cap-lift ray.  Put `(U_n,B_n)` for the semantic pair at
depth `n`, `q_n` for its exact cap root, `s_n` for its joint Continue mass,
and `a_n=1-s_n` for its absorption charge.  Exact debt transport gives

\[
 d_{n+1,i}=s_n d_{n,i},
 \qquad
 d_{n,i}=P_n d_{0,i},
 \qquad
 P_n=\prod_{t<n}s_t.                                  \tag{7.40}
\]

At a positive global minimum, the checked cap-lift port has
\(\sum_n a_n<\infty\) and \(P_n\ge\mathsf{reachFloor}>0\).  Hence
\(s_n\to1\); also \(r_{n,i}\to1\) and \(c_{n,i}\to1\).  For every
coordinate with \(d_{0,i}>0\),

\[
 d_{n,i}\longrightarrow d_{\infty,i}>0,
 \qquad
 c_{n,i}d_{n,i}\longrightarrow d_{\infty,i}.          \tag{7.41}
\]

Consequently summability of either the support residuals \(e_{n,i}\) or the
ordinary defects \(\delta_{n,i}\) forces the precise **slack wall**

\[
 \liminf_n \ell_{n,i}\ge d_{\infty,i}.                 \tag{7.42}
\]

Conversely, if \(\limsup_n\ell_{n,i}<d_{\infty,i}\), then both residuals have
an eventual positive lower bound and their sums diverge.  Thus the cap-lift
summability of \(a_n\) does not make the cap-to-payoff Nash residual summable;
it says that a summable residual is possible only if the cap roots develop
Continue slack at least as large as the surviving debt.

The zero-charge arm makes the obstruction exact.  In an `InertStall`, every
`q_n` is all Continue and `(U_n,B_n)=(U,B)`.  Hence

\[
 r_i=c_i=s=1,\qquad
 Q_i=\operatorname{reward}_i(\{i\}),\qquad C_i^B=B_i,\qquad
 e_i=\delta_i=(\operatorname{reward}_i(\{i\})-U_i)_+. \tag{7.43}
\]

Therefore exactly one of the following occurs.

1. Some singleton reward exceeds the prescribed suffix payoff.  Then the
   same positive support/Nash defect occurs at every prefix, its cumulative
   sum grows linearly, and the cumulative absorption charge is identically
   zero.  This disproves every unconditional charge-relative or
   charge-summable cap-to-payoff residual bound.
2. Every singleton reward lies below the prescribed suffix payoff.  Then all
   residuals in (7.43) vanish: the outer all-Continue roots are already exact
   Nash against the prescribed suffix payoff.  But the semantic pair and the
   positive paid row are transported unchanged, so summing zero residuals
   still produces no absorption and no terminal consumer.

This is sharper than the bare cap/payoff type warning.  The bridge is fully
classified in the hard zero-charge branch: it either fails by a permanent
quantitative residual, or succeeds exactly while leaving the paid-port
consumer problem untouched.

### Passive padding and rare pulses do not produce branch S.3

The checked well-supported form of AGKRS branch S.3 asks, for every
`delta>0`, for one **infinite** root sequence which is completely absorbing
and whose support-local endpoint error is at most `delta` at **every** date,
against that same sequence's actual continuation payoff.  The quantifier over
dates makes silent padding ineffective.

Let `p` be the live-root sequence of a supplied profile with prescribed
payoff `U`, and let `Pad_N(p)` prepend `N` all-Continue rows.  If

\[
 \operatorname{reward}_i(\{i\})\le U_i\qquad(i\in I),                 \tag{7.44}
\]

then every new padding row is support-exact against `U`.  After date `N`, the
root and continuation vector are literally those of `p`, shifted by `N`.
Consequently

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathsf{CompleteAbsorption}(\mathsf{Pad}_N(p))
   &\Longleftrightarrow \mathsf{CompleteAbsorption}(p),\\
 \sup_t\mathsf{supportError}(\mathsf{Pad}_N(p),t)
   &=\sup_t\mathsf{supportError}(p,t).
 \end{aligned}                                                     \tag{7.45}
\]

Thus padding preserves whichever S.3 field was already missing.  A
pointwise limit in which the bad or absorbing row moves to temporal infinity
does not repair this: every finite member still has that row, while the limit
is the all-Continue sequence and is not completely absorbing.

Rare hazard pulses fail for a second, quantitative reason.  Assume rewards
are bounded by `M`, and suppose the strict prescribed singleton isolation at
the intended self-tail is

\[
 U_i-\operatorname{reward}_i(\{i\})\ge\kappa>0
 \qquad(i\in I).                                                  \tag{7.46}
\]

Consider any candidate pulse root `q` against a continuation vector `V` with
`|V_i-U_i|<=eta`.  Let `c_i` be the probability that every opponent of `i`
Continues at the pulse.  Bounding all outcomes on the complementary event by
`M` gives the exact one-sided estimates

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 Q_i(q)&\le c_i\operatorname{reward}_i(\{i\})+(1-c_i)M,\\
 C_i(V,q)&\ge c_iV_i-(1-c_i)M,
 \end{aligned}
\]

and hence

\[
 Q_i(q)-C_i(V,q)
 \le-c_i(\kappa-\eta)+2M(1-c_i).                    \tag{7.47}
\]

If player `i` assigns positive probability to Quit and the row is
support-`delta` Nash, its used-Quit inequality and (7.47) force, whenever
`delta<kappa-eta`,

\[
 1-c_i\ge
 {\kappa-\eta-\delta\over \kappa-\eta+2M}
 =:\theta>0.                                         \tag{7.48}
\]

In particular every non-all-Continue supported pulse in the self-tail tube
has joint absorption at least `theta`.  A solo pulse is impossible.  If
exactly two players are active, each one's Quit probability is at least
`theta`, because it is the other's entire opponent hazard.  Therefore a
two-scale row with one vanishing hazard cannot satisfy the support condition;
making an action rare does not dilute its support-local endpoint error.

Equations (7.45) and (7.48) leave only macroscopic phase rows.  Such rows can
indeed produce S.3 when a separate collision or cyclic certificate makes
their used actions nearly indifferent, but the paid-port packet supplies no
such certificate.  If their continuation vectors stay near `U`, (7.48)
requires fixed opponent activity; if the vectors leave that tube, the
construction has entered the already isolated noncarrier annotation-seam
branch.  The positive terminal atom supplies absorption mass, not the
endpoint inequalities for every player at the pulse.

The renewal calculation displays the same missing field.  For a finite root
word whose absorbed reward contribution is `w` and whose joint survival is
`c`, its payoff map is

\[
 F(V)=w+cV.                                                        \tag{7.49}
\]

Repeating the word is completely absorbing when `c<1`, but its phase target
is the fixed point `V_*=w/(1-c)`.  It preserves the intended self-tail `U`
only under the vector balance

\[
 w=(1-c)U.                                                        \tag{7.50}
\]

A retained atom gives `1-c>0` for a suitable cut; it does not give (7.50),
nor support-local Nash inequalities against `V_*`.  A paid comparison of two
pure clocks gives neither vector identity.  Two-scale scheduling is therefore
not a formal consequence of the atom/clock passport: it needs new phase
targets and a simultaneous endpoint-balance theorem.

The constant-`-1` Fin4 regression above makes the quantifier failure literal.
The all-Never response sequence is support-exact and nonabsorbing.  The sibling
source in which only `j` Quits at date zero is completely absorbing and has a
unit terminal atom, but its active row has support error exactly one.  Every
finite all-Continue padding preserves these two facts.  Replacing the sure
Quit by any probability `0<h<1` still has error one.  If two players pulse
with probabilities `h,b in (0,1)`, their respective support errors are
`1-b` and `1-h`; support error at most `delta` forces both
`h,b>=1-delta`, so dilution collapses to a near-sure collision.  The table
does have S.3 through an unrelated sure collision, which is exactly the
point: the S.3 witness is not produced by the retained atom/paid-clock
self-tail data.  As before, this interface regression has global minimum
zero and is not a positive-minimum counterexample.

**Producer/consumer dependency audit.**  No S.3 consumer is used in this
argument.  The producer-side inputs are only: the literal live-root sequence
of the actual source; the `InertStall` equal-pair/all-Continue/losslessly
shifted-row fields; the retained atom/paid-clock passport; the reward bound;
and, for (7.46)--(7.48), a separately supplied prescribed-payoff singleton
isolation.  The verification interfaces are the definitions
`IsCompletelyAbsorbing`, `IsQuittingRootSupportApproxNash`,
`IsQuittingRootSequenceSupportApproxNash`, and
`QuittingWellSupportedAbsorbingSequenceExistence`, together with the literal
root-sequence shift identities.  Equations (7.45)--(7.50) are ordinary
algebraic verifiers, not checked producers.  In particular, this section does
**not** invoke the well-supported-to-S.3 adapter, the projective-lasso
producer, the normal-S.3 uniform-payoff compiler, or any theorem whose premise
already assumes S.3/well-supported data.  The outcome is failure of the three
tested producers (passive padding, vanishing pulses, and atom-only renewal),
not an S.3 witness.

### An anchored relative-timing bubble defeats compact continuity

Quotienting common delays removes the absolute clock escape in the inert cap
lift, but it does not bound the **relative diameter** between the retained
atom and its paid response.  The following exact Fin4 family already has its
translation anchor at date zero.

Let player `0` receive terminal reward one exactly when players `0` and `1`
belong to the quitting coalition together, and zero otherwise.  Give every
other payoff coordinate value zero.  For `n>=1`, let `sigma_n` be the profile
in which

\[
 T_0=0,\qquad
 T_1=\begin{cases}n,&\text{with probability }1/2,\\
                   \infty,&\text{with probability }1/2,
      \end{cases}
 \qquad T_2=T_3=\infty.                               \tag{7.51}
\]

The literal terminal chronology has the fixed atom

\[
 \Pr_{\sigma_n}\{\text{first terminal date }0,
                    \text{ coalition }\{0\}\}=1,     \tag{7.52}
\]

so the source payoff is zero.  Player `0`'s source-supported clock `0` and
receiving clock `n` form a later-receiving paid row of gain `1/2`: at time
`n` it ties player `1` with probability `1/2`, and otherwise Quits alone for
zero.  More strongly, for every finite pure response time `ell`,

\[
 P_\ell(\sigma_n):=
 U_0(\sigma_n[0\leftarrow\ell])
 =\begin{cases}1/2,&\ell=n,\\0,&\ell\ne n,
  \end{cases}
 \qquad
 P_\infty(\sigma_n)=0.                                \tag{7.53}
\]

This is a genuine one-player counterfactual payoff, not an abstract suffix
probe.  The profile is already normalized by the positive atom (7.52), so no
further common translation changes (7.53).

In the one-point compactification of stopping laws,

\[
 \tfrac12\delta_n+\tfrac12\delta_\infty
 \Longrightarrow\delta_\infty,
 \qquad
 \sigma_n\Longrightarrow\sigma_\infty,
                                                               \tag{7.54}
\]

where only player `0` Quits at date zero in `sigma_infinity`.  Every fixed
finite probe in (7.53) converges to zero, which is its payoff at the limit.
But the moving response satisfies

\[
 P_n(\sigma_n)=1/2,
 \qquad
 \sup_{\ell\in\mathbb N\cup\{\infty\}}P_\ell(\sigma_n)=1/2,
 \qquad
 \sup_\ell P_\ell(\sigma_\infty)=0.                  \tag{7.55}
\]

Thus the best-response value and the paid mark are not upper semicontinuous
even after anchoring and quotienting all common delays.  The discontinuity is
the diagonal collision

\[
 (T_0,T_1)=(n,n)\longrightarrow(\infty,\infty):       \tag{7.56}
\]

every finite pair is a terminal tie, while the limiting pair is joint Never
and has no terminal outcome.

There is also a topology-independent common-modulus obstruction.  Distinct
sources are separated by their own response probes:

\[
 |P_n(\sigma_n)-P_n(\sigma_m)|=1/2\qquad(n\ne m).     \tag{7.57}
\]

Hence no sequentially compact metric carrier can encode every `sigma_n` and
make all pure-time payoff probes `P_ell` continuous with one common modulus.
Indeed (7.57) is exactly the separated-probe hypothesis consumed by
`not_isSeqCompact_of_commonProbeModulus`; it is the actual-profile,
one-player version of the checked Fin4 suffix-spike no-go.  In particular,
there is no compact state/action model on which the payoff
`(ell,state) -> P_ell(state)` is jointly continuous: joint continuity on a
compact product would supply precisely such a uniform modulus.

Choosing the paid response rather than the atom as translation origin only
reverses the escape: the receiving clock is fixed at zero and the atom moves
to relative time `-n`.  A one-anchor quotient cannot keep both endpoints
finite.  A blow-up boundary which distinguishes a finite tie escaping to
infinity from joint Never could retain (7.55), but it is no longer the compact
space of actual stopping laws.  It would need a decoder for the new ghost
boundary and compatibility with every unilateral replacement; the checked
all-depth suffix-spike theorem shows that these counterfactual probes cannot
all have one compact uniform modulus.

This family has global minimum debt zero and does not refute a theorem using
the positive-minimum hard residual.  It proves the requested architecture
boundary: a retained positive atom and paid clock do not make Fin4 relative
stopping-time laws compact enough for a direct continuous Nash/fixed-point
argument.  A successful compactification must resolve the tie-at-infinity
bubble, not merely quotient common delays.

### 7.12 Punishment normality does not splice the early join to the late tie

Punishment normality does strengthen a singleton cell.  Applied to a
terminal exploitability witness with owner `o`, the checked atomic-collision
theorem supplies an outsider `j != o` such that

\[
 r_j(\{o,j\})-r_j(\{o\})\geq\gamma.                 \tag{7.58}
\]

This is a **conditional cell inequality**, not yet the payoff of one
behavioral deviation against the actual source.  If the singleton cell
`{o}` has absolute mass `alpha`, making `j` Quit at that date gains at least
`alpha gamma` on that cell, but can lose as much as `2M(1-alpha)` on its
complement.  Thus the unconditional bound supplied by (7.58) and boundedness
alone is only

\[
 \Delta_j^{\rm join}\geq
 \alpha\gamma-2M(1-\alpha).                         \tag{7.59}
\]

The player cannot condition its simultaneous root action on whether `o`
also chooses Quit.  In particular, the retained positive atom floor need not
make the right side of (7.59) positive.

Here is an exact Fin4 regression showing that this is not merely slack in the
estimate.  Let every unlisted reward coordinate be zero, and specify

\[
 r(\{0\})=(0,2,1,1),\qquad
 r(\{0,1\})=(2,2,1,1),\qquad
 r_2(\{0,2\})=2.                                    \tag{7.60}
\]

In particular every own-singleton reward is zero.  Hence every player is
punishment-normal: against all opponents playing Never, every finite own Quit
and Never itself pay zero, so the punishment cap is zero.

For `n>=1`, let player `0` Quit at time `0` or time `n`, each with probability
`1/2`; let player `1` Quit at `n` or Never, each with probability `1/2`; and
let players `2,3` play Never.  The terminal law is

\[
 \Pr(\{0\})=3/4,\qquad \Pr(\{0,1\})=1/4,
\]

and its prescribed payoff is

\[
 U=(1/2,2,1,1).                                     \tag{7.61}
\]

Thus this actual source has the uniform prescribed singleton-isolation

\[
 \min_i\bigl(U_i-r_i(\{i\})\bigr)=1/2>0.           \tag{7.62}
\]

It also has a fixed early singleton atom: `{0}` occurs at date zero with
mass `1/2`.  Player `0`'s supported clock zero and receiving clock `n` form a
paid later-receiving row of gain one.  Indeed, against player `1`, pure time
`n` gives the tie payoff `2` with probability `1/2` and the singleton payoff
zero otherwise, while every other pure time, including Never, pays zero.

At the early singleton, player `2` has the strict punishment-normal join

\[
 r_2(\{0,2\})-r_2(\{0\})=1.                        \tag{7.63}
\]

Nevertheless its unconditional time-zero join deviation pays

\[
 \tfrac12 r_2(\{0,2\})+\tfrac12 r_2(\{2\})=1=U_2. \tag{7.64}
\]

The gain on the early atom is cancelled exactly when player `0` selects its
late clock: then player `2` Quits alone.  Thus the guaranteed strict join is
not even a positive behavioral-gain row at the actual source.

The same example gives an exact two-scale support calculation.  At date zero
let `a` be player `0`'s Quit probability and `h` player `2`'s Quit
probability, and retain after joint Continue the delayed time-`n` tail above.
The root endpoint differences (Quit minus Continue) are

\[
 E_0=-(1-h),\qquad E_2=2a-1.                        \tag{7.65}
\]

The first identity uses `r_0({0})=r_0({0,2})=r_0({2})=0` and delayed-tail
value one.  The second uses (7.60) and the fact that player `2` receives one
at either delayed terminal coalition.

Consequently, in a support-local `delta`-Nash row, positive support on
player `0`'s early Quit forces

\[
 1-h\leq\delta.                                     \tag{7.66}
\]

But both the reach probability of the late branch and player `0`'s late paid
gain are multiplied by `1-h`; in particular the paid gain is exactly
`1-h`.  Therefore any vanishing-error row retaining the early source clock
forces the late gain to vanish.  At exact error zero the dichotomy is sharp:

* if the delayed suffix is reached with positive probability, then
  `a=h=0`, so there is no early singleton clock and no joiner clock;
* if `a>0`, then `h=1`, so absorption at the early row is certain and the
  late paid clock is never reached.

This is the requested separation of the two clocks.  Punishment normality
can produce a finite collision block only by consuming the late source; it
does not produce two persistent labels or an S.3 sequence on one executable
chronology.  Repeating the static singleton cell is not an adapter: a second
persistent marginal would require nonsummable repeated `h`-mass, while
(7.66) makes the retained late gain/reach vanish on every small-error block.

The regression has an all-Never equilibrium and hence global minimum debt
zero.  It does not instantiate a positive-minimum hard residual or a global
terminal-gap witness.  What it proves is the precise local insufficiency:
punishment normality, positive prescribed singleton isolation at the actual
source, a fixed positive singleton atom, a strict outsider join on that atom,
and a source-supported late paid response still do not imply the checked
two-persistent/S.3 input.  Any positive-minimum theorem must use an additional
global field to prevent the off-atom cancellation (7.64) or to reimburse the
preempted suffix in (7.66).

**Checked dependency and status.**  The implication (7.58) is exactly
`QuittingTerminalExploitabilityWitness.exists_atomicCollision_gain_of_normal`
in `PunishmentNormalAtomicCollision.lean`, and its hard-residual wrapper is
`FinFourQuantitativeFullSupportHardResidual.exists_terminalGap_collision_at_singleton`
in `PunishmentNormalAtomicCollisionHandoff.lean`.  The target notion ruled
out here is `HasTwoPersistentQuittingMarginals` in
`PersistentDeletedClockTwoLabel.lean`.  Equations (7.59)--(7.66) are ordinary
mathematics; no two-persistent theorem, S.3 producer, or compiler is assumed.

### 7.13 Global exact-response compactness has a cycle arm

There is a useful global normal form, but it is a trichotomy rather than the
tempting "tight limit or clock escape" dichotomy.  A tight exact-best-response
orbit need not converge to a Nash fixed point; it can be a strict horizontal
cycle.

Write `P_i(t;sigma_-i)` for player `i`'s payoff from the deterministic clock
`t in N union {infinity}`, and let `B_i(sigma)` be the unrestricted behavioral
cap.  The checked pure-time extremality identity is

\[
 B_i(\sigma)=\sup_t P_i(t;\sigma_{-i}).             \tag{7.67}
\]

Assume the actual-profile global barrier

\[
 D(\sigma)\geq D_*>0                               \tag{7.68}
\]

and select at every profile a maximum-debt mover.  Its debt is at least
`D_*/4`.  Whenever (7.67) has a deterministic attainer, use a fixed tie rule
(prefer Never, then the least finite time) and replace only that mover by the
selected attainer.

If every selected finite time belongs to one fixed finite alphabet, then
the orbit is finite-state.  Indeed each coordinate is either its unchanged
initial stopping law or one of the selected pure clocks.  The deterministic
orbit therefore enters a literal nontrivial cycle.  Every edge has mover gain
at least `D_*/4` and kills that mover's target debt.  No cycle vertex is Nash.
This is the arbitrary-initial-law analogue of the checked inherited-alphabet
pure-clock orbit; coordinates which never move simply remain frozen.

This already disproves the proposed tight-implies-fixed-point step.  In fact,
if `M` bounds the rewards, coupling the old and new own stopping laws gives

\[
 \|\sigma_i^{n+1}-\sigma_i^n\|_{\rm TV}
 \geq {D_*\over 8M}.                               \tag{7.69}
\]

The payoff change is at most `2M` times that total-variation distance.
Thus compact recurrence supplies constant subsequences at cycle vertices,
not asymptotic regularity of a source with its successor.  A potential or a
separate no-cycle theorem would be needed to turn tightness into Nash.

There are two exact escape arms.  First, if the supremum in (7.67) is not
attained at some selected source, Never is strictly below the cap and every
cap-approximating sequence of finite times has a subsequence tending to
infinity.  This is already a relative clock-escape certificate, so arbitrary
cap attainment is not needed for the alternative.

Second, suppose every visited cap is attained but the selected finite times
are unbounded.  Stabilize the Fin4 mover label `i` and take `t_n -> infinity`.
Put

\[
 \eta_n=P_i(t_n;\sigma^n_{-i})
          -P_i(\infty;\sigma^n_{-i})\geq0,
\]

and let

\[
 q_n=\Pr_{\sigma^n_{-i}}
       \{T_j\geq t_n\text{ for every }j\ne i\}.
\]

The finite response and Never induce the same terminal outcome unless every
opponent survives to `t_n`.  Hence the exact inequality is

\[
 0\leq\eta_n\leq2M q_n.                             \tag{7.70}
\]

After a subsequence `eta_n -> eta`, this gives the following exhaustive
classification.

1. **Neutral escape (`eta=0`).**  Never is an `eta_n`-best response.  Its
   gain over the current profile is at least `D_*/4-eta_n`, and the updated
   mover debt is at most `eta_n`.  The escaping finite clock can therefore
   be replaced by a near-exact Never reset.  This is still a horizontal
   response, not a Nash--Bellman edge.

2. **Robust relative escape (`eta>0`).**  Eventually

   \[
    q_n\geq c:={\eta\over4M}>0.                     \tag{7.71}
   \]

   Each of the three opponent stopping laws has mass at least `c` at or
   beyond `t_n`.  Along a weak stopping-law subsequence, every opponent limit
   therefore has Never mass at least `c`, while the selected mover
   `delta_{t_n}` converges to Never.  The weak limiting profile has joint
   Never mass at least `c^3`.

The last sentence must not be read as convergence of terminal outcome laws.
Every updated finite profile has mover `i` stopping surely, hence literal
joint-Never outcome mass zero.  Its weak stopping-law limit has joint-Never
mass at least `c^3`.  Thus (7.71) is exactly a positive tie/order bubble at
the joint-infinity diagonal, not a terminal Nash limit.  The terminal laws
themselves have compact subsequences, but those limits are not the terminal
law of the weak profile limit.  Interpreting the defect requires separately
retaining which finite coalition absorbed on the moving late event.

The nonattained-cap arm is automatically robust in the same sense.  At its
fixed source, if `B_i-P_i(infinity)=2 eta>0`, sufficiently good finite
approximants satisfy `P_i(t)-P_i(infinity)>=eta`; (7.70) gives the fixed tail
floor `q>=eta/(2M)`, and the approximating times must escape every finite
calendar.

This proves the honest global alternative:

\[
 \boxed{\text{terminal Nash}\;|\;\text{strict tight response cycle}
   \;|\;\text{neutral Never escape}\;|\;
   \text{robust joint-infinity bubble}.}            \tag{7.72}
\]

Under (7.68) the first arm is impossible.  On a pure-clock source the second
arm is exactly the already checked finite inherited-alphabet response-cycle
theorem.  For arbitrary sources, cap nonattainment and unbounded selected
clocks are assigned to the last two arms rather than silently discarded.

There is a precise retained-atom refinement.  Suppose along the escaping
subsequence the sources retain a singleton `{o}` at one fixed bounded date
with mass at least `alpha>0`.  For large `n`, `t_n` is later than that date.

* If `i != o`, the late replacement Continues through the singleton date,
  so the target singleton mass is at least `alpha`.  In the robust arm the
  same target sequence carries both this fixed early atom and the positive
  joint-infinity bubble (7.71).  Punishment normality attaches the checked
  strict outsider collision inequality to the early singleton, but Section
  7.12 shows that this conditional port does not by itself become a second
  persistent clock.
* If `i=o`, the late replacement deletes the early owner atom.  The response
  orbit has left the retained-atom passport before compactness is used.

Thus arbitrary better-response iteration does not preserve the tangent-frontier
passport.  Its escape is classified as either an owner-erasing branch or a
two-component early-atom/joint-infinity bubble.  The latter is a sharper
boundary object, but neither component supplies a terminal consumer without
an additional source-preserving rule.

**Status and checked dependencies.**  The pure-time supremum (7.67) is
`quittingContinuationBestResponseValue_eq_sSup_pureTimeDeviationPayoff`.
The finite-alphabet exact cycle, gain floor, and killed-mover identity are
checked for pure-clock sources in `PureTimeSelectedExactResponseOrbit.lean`;
the frozen-arbitrary-coordinate extension above is ordinary mathematics.  The
stopping-law and terminal-payoff canonicalization is checked in
`StoppingLawCanonicalization.lean`.  Equations (7.69)--(7.72), including the
tail coupling (7.70), are ordinary mathematics.  They do not assume
best-response convergence, cap attainment for arbitrary opponents, or
continuity at joint infinity.

### 7.14 Positive global debt forces failure of better-reply security

Mixed Nashification of each finite clock alphabet removes the cycle arm, but
it does not produce a compact-game equilibrium.  It proves the opposite: if
the positive global debt barrier exists, the ordinary compact stopping-law
game necessarily fails the closure-payoff security condition required by a
Reny-type discontinuous-game theorem.

Let

\[
 A_n=\{0,1,\ldots,n,\infty\}.
\]

Choose a mixed Nash equilibrium `mu^n` of the finite normal-form game in
which every player selects a clock in `A_n`, and realize those four mixed
clock laws behaviorally.  Finite-game Nash existence is the only fixed-point
input here.  Pass to a subsequence such that the four laws converge weakly
to `mu` and the prescribed payoff vectors converge to `u`.  Then `(mu,u)`
belongs to the closure of the payoff graph of the compact stopping-law game.

For every player `i` and every **fixed complete stopping law** `lambda_i`,
finite-game Nash and the one-player mixture identity give

\[
 U_i(\lambda_i,\mu^n_{-i})
 \leq U_i(\mu^n)+
       2M\lambda_i(\{n+1,n+2,\ldots\}).            \tag{7.73}
\]

Indeed every clock in `A_n` is bounded by the equilibrium payoff.  The
remaining finite-clock mass is at most the displayed tail, and replacing its
payoff by the reward upper bound costs at most `2M` per unit mass.  For fixed
`lambda_i` that tail tends to zero, so

\[
 \limsup_n U_i(\lambda_i,\mu^n_{-i})\leq u_i       \tag{7.74}
\]

simultaneously for every player and every fixed behavioral deviation.

Under the global barrier (7.68), the actual weak-limit profile `mu` is not a
terminal Nash profile.  But (7.74) says that no player has one fixed stopping
law which secures a payoff strictly above `u_i` against all opponent profiles
near `mu_{-i}`: the sequence `mu^n_{-i}` itself defeats such security.  Thus
the following closure-payoff implication is false:

> at every non-Nash `(mu,u)` in the closure of the payoff graph, some player
> has a fixed strategy and a neighborhood of `mu_{-i}` on which that strategy
> pays uniformly more than `u_i`.

This is exactly the better-reply-security premise the proposed compact
discontinuous-game existence route would need.  No external equilibrium
theorem is invoked: (7.73)--(7.74) directly falsify its premise.

The same construction identifies the lost response quantitatively.  At
`mu^n`, select a maximum-debt player `i_n`.  Pure-time extremality gives a
finite clock `t_n=n+1` which attains the unrestricted cap: every earlier
clock is in the finite Nash menu, while all clocks later than `n` have the
same payoff against opponents supported on `A_n`.  Hence

\[
 P_{i_n}(t_n;\mu^n_{-i_n})-U_{i_n}(\mu^n)\geq D_*/4.\tag{7.75}
\]

Since Never belongs to `A_n`, it pays at most the finite-game equilibrium
payoff.  Opponents have no
finite clock after `n`, and therefore late Quit and Never differ **only** on
the event that every opponent selected Never.  Consequently

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 &P_{i_n}(t_n;\mu^n_{-i_n})
       -P_{i_n}(\infty;\mu^n_{-i_n})\\
 &\qquad=
 s_{i_n}\prod_{j\ne i_n}\mu^n_j(\{\infty\})
 \geq D_*/4,                                       \tag{7.76}
 \end{aligned}

where `s_i=r_i({i})`.  Stabilizing the Fin4 mover label gives `s_i>0` and

\[
 \prod_{j\ne i}\mu^n_j(\{\infty\})
 \geq{D_*\over4s_i}\geq {D_*\over4M}.             \tag{7.77}
\]

Thus the obstruction is not a neutral clock: it is a robust late release of
positive joint-opponent-Never mass into the singleton `{i}`.  The selected
clocks escape to infinity precisely because every fixed clock is eventually
inside the finite Nash menu and hence cannot improve.

Punishment normality together with the hard-residual terminal-gap witness does
not secure this boundary.  It attaches an outsider `j` with a strict
collision gain to the moving singleton `{i}`, but realizing that collision
requires `j` to match the moving date `t_n`.  This is another escaping
response, not one fixed strategy securing the closure payoff.
Equation (7.74) rules out **every** fixed mixed stopping law, so the static
collision inequality cannot repair better-reply security.  Section 7.12 is
the complementary same-row calculation: even when a singleton atom is fixed,
the conditional join may be exactly cancelled off that atom.

The original retained tangent-frontier atom also does not pass through this
argument.  Each `mu^n` is a newly solved finite-clock equilibrium; only the
reward table and global debt barrier are retained.  Imposing a fixed atom as
a joint constraint would no longer be the product mixed-strategy simplex to
which ordinary finite Nash existence applies.  Therefore the global theorem
is a sharp architecture no-go:

\[
 D_*>0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \begin{cases}
 \text{robust positive-solo clock escape in every finite-menu Nash family},\\
 \text{failure of better-reply security of the ordinary compact law game}.
 \end{cases}                                       \tag{7.78}
\]

**Status and dependencies.**  The finite-deadline profile interface and its
exact late-Quit/Never escape charge are checked in
`TerminalSemanticFiniteDeadlineNashEscalation.lean`; in particular
`QuittingFiniteDeadlineNashProfile.semanticDebt_le_escapeCharge` and the
global-floor survival theorem give the checked version of (7.76)--(7.77).
Existence of the finite mixed Nash profiles and the closure-security argument
(7.73)--(7.74) are ordinary finite-game mathematics.  This section strengthens
the local falsifier in `CODEX_CEDAR__STOPPING_TIME_COMPACT_GAME.md`: under the
hypothetical positive global barrier, failure of better-reply security is
forced rather than exceptional.

### 7.15 Cross-audit: the robust release is the adjacent boundary field

The positive-solo/opponent-Never part of Section 7.14 is not a new boundary
mechanism.  It is the old-profile half of
`QuittingAdjacentDeadlineGapSource`.  Let `G_B` be that source's selected old
observer gain from the newly exposed boundary action and `G_N` its gain from
Never, both evaluated after literal inclusion in the successor timing game.
The source and old Nash law give

\[
 G_B\geq\gamma,\qquad G_N\leq0.                    \tag{7.79}
\]

The checked late-Quit/Never identity gives, without any support hypothesis,

\[
 G_B-G_N=
 \left(\prod_{j\ne i}\mu_j(\{\infty\})\right)s_i. \tag{7.80}
\]

Therefore every adjacent-gap source already satisfies

\[
 s_i>0,\qquad
 \prod_{j\ne i}\mu_j(\{\infty\})
   \geq {\gamma\over s_i}\geq {\gamma\over M}.     \tag{7.81}
\]

The small strengthening over the named declaration is only one of theorem
surface.  The existing
`quittingAdjacentDeadlineOldOpponentNeverProduct_ge_div_of_support` assumes
positive old observer Never support so that `G_N=0`; (7.79)--(7.80) need only
the Nash inequality `G_N<=0`.  Thus (7.81) also classifies the zero-own-Never
case.

It does **not** eliminate an arm of
`quittingAdjacentDeadline_singletonSeparatedTail_dispatch`:

* zero observer Never is already the lossless old-boundary response, whose
  actual grafted gain is the stored `G_B>=gamma`;
* a macroscopic failure to pass is already the paid pass response;
* small pass plus small censor error is already the paid reverse participant;
* the raw censor-error branch remains unchanged.

Nor does (7.81) control the successor law.  The selected-effect theorem still
returns exactly

\[
 \text{large selected/full operational effect}\quad\text{or}\quad
 \text{paid reverse participant}.                  \tag{7.82}
\]

The large-effect arm compares the included old law with the censored new Nash
law; an old opponent-Never cylinder floor supplies no upper bound on that
displacement.  Hence Section 7.14 merely rederives the checked large-boundary
input at each deadline.  Its additional conclusion is global and topological:
assembling all finite-menu Nash laws into one payoff-graph sequence proves
failure of better-reply security.  No adjacent-deadline consumer is improved,
and no one of the four operational arms is removed.

**Exact checked bridge.**  `QuittingAdjacentDeadlineGapSource.of_terminalExploitabilityGap`
supplies (7.79).  Equation (7.80) is the timing-profile specialization of
`quittingRootSequencePureTimeTerminalValue_late_sub_none_eq` using
`quittingFiniteDeadlineOpponentSurvival_timingProfile_eq_prod_none`; the same
calculation appears internally in `AdjacentDeadlineRetainedTailReprojection.lean`.
The support-specialized cylinder theorem and both dispatches are in
`AdjacentDeadlineSingletonSeparatedTailDispatch.lean` and
`AdjacentDeadlineSelectedBoundaryEffectDispatch.lean`.  Equation (7.81) is
an ordinary two-line corollary proposed only as a declaration-surface
strengthening, not as a new consumer.

**Checked/no-go dependency audit.**  The stopping-law normal form and fixed

### 7.16 Large adjacent operational effect produces a same-tail paid port

The checked selected-effect dispatch leaves a large selected gauge unchanged.
That residual can in fact be converted to a literal paid port.  The conversion
does not put its source endpoint on the minimum fibre, so it feeds exactly the
maintained paid-port question rather than solving it.

Fix Fin4 data from a `QuittingAdjacentDeadlineGapSource`, write `M>0` for the
reward bound and `gamma>0` for its gap, and let `tau` be the supplied actual
singleton-separated tail.  Put

\[
 u_i=U_i(\tau),\qquad
 u_i-r_i(\{i\})\geq \delta/2>0.                    \tag{7.83}
\]

Let `P` be the included old timing law and `R` the included censored successor
law, both on the successor clock.  Set

\[
 p_j=P_j(\infty),\qquad c_j=R_j(\infty),\qquad
 m(P)=\prod_jp_j,\qquad m(R)=\prod_jc_j.           \tag{7.84}
\]

Let `o` be the selected observer and `a` the newly exposed boundary action.
Write `g_P^0,g_R^0` for the two hard-zero-tail gains of response `a`.  Assume
the large selected-effect arm

\[
 {\gamma\over8M}\leq
 \max\left\{
   \max_j|p_j-c_j|,
   {|g_P^0-g_R^0|\over4M}
 \right\}.                                        \tag{7.85}
\]

Put `epsilon=gamma/(16M)`.  There are two exhaustive cases.

**Never-law probe.**  Suppose `|p_k-c_k|>=epsilon` for some `k`.  Give every
other player the deterministic `Never` prefix, give player `k` first its
`P_k` prefix law and then its `R_k` prefix law, and follow either prefix by the
same literal tail `tau`.  Against the passive prefixes, every finite stop by
`k` is the singleton `{k}`, while `Never` reaches `tau`.  Hence the two
payoffs differ exactly by

\[
 (c_k-p_k)\bigl(u_k-r_k(\{k\})\bigr).              \tag{7.86}
\]

Orient the edge toward the endpoint with the larger `Never` coefficient.
It is one unilateral complete-strategy update of player `k` and has gain

\[
 w_{\rm law}\geq
 {\gamma\over16M}{\delta\over2}
 ={\delta\gamma\over32M}.                           \tag{7.87}
\]

The mover's opponents are identical at the two endpoints.  Therefore its
unrestricted continuation cap is exactly unchanged and its debt is reduced
by exactly the displayed payoff gain.

**Boundary-gain probe.**  Otherwise `|p_j-c_j|<epsilon` for all four players.
Equation (7.85) forces

\[
 |g_P^0-g_R^0|\geq\gamma/2.                         \tag{7.88}
\]

Graft `tau` literally after both finite timing laws.  The retained-tail payoff
identity gives

\[
 g_P^\tau=g_P^0-m(P)u_o,qquad
 g_R^\tau=g_R^0-m(R)u_o.                            \tag{7.89}
\]

For four numbers in `[0,1]`, the elementary product telescope and bounded
rewards give

\[
 |m(P)-m(R)|\leq\sum_j|p_j-c_j|
   <4\epsilon={\gamma\over4M},qquad |u_o|\leq M.   \tag{7.90}
\]

Consequently

\[
 |g_P^\tau-g_R^\tau|>\gamma/4,                      \tag{7.91}
\]

so at least one of `|g_P^tau|,|g_R^tau|` is greater than `gamma/8`.  If the
selected gain is positive, update that grafted base profile to pure boundary
response `a`; if it is negative, orient the same edge from the response corner
back to the base law.  This is again one literal complete-strategy update,
now of observer `o`, with

\[
 w_{\rm gain}>\gamma/8.                             \tag{7.92}
\]

Its cap is unchanged and its debt falls by exactly `w_gain`, for the same
fixed-opponents reason.

Thus the large selected-effect arm always produces a same-tail paid port with
floor

\[
 \boxed{\min\{\delta\gamma/(32M),\ \gamma/8\}}.     \tag{7.93}
\]

The strict inequality in the second branch can be weakened by changing the
auxiliary split constant; no attempt is made to optimize constants.

**Full-distance and label audit.**  The full operational pseudometric has
three exact finite coordinate families: `Never`, prescribed hard payoff, and
hard pure-action gain.  A large prescribed-payoff coordinate alone is not a
paid unilateral edge, since a hybrid telescope may charge one player's payoff
change to another player's law.  It is unnecessary here.  The source-specific
checked proof first gives

\[
 d_{\rm eff}(P,R)\geq\theta
 \Longrightarrow
 \begin{cases}
 |p_k-c_k|\geq\theta&\text{for some }k,\quad\text{or}\\
 |U_k^0(P)-U_k^0(R)|\geq2M\theta&\text{for some }k,\quad\text{or}\\
 |G_k^0(a;P)-G_k^0(a;R)|\geq4M\theta
   &\text{for some }(k,a).
 \end{cases}                                         \tag{7.94a}
\]

This is just witness extraction from the nested finite maxima in the checked
definition; no cap-attainment assertion occurs.  The second line is the
cross-player obstruction just noted.  Across varying deadlines, `k`
stabilizes, but an arbitrary selected action `a_N` need not: after passage to
a subsequence it may be a fixed old date, a fixed lag behind the boundary, or
escape both from the origin and the boundary.  The selected source theorem
avoids this moving-action trichotomy because its only action probe is the
canonically named newly exposed boundary.

The source-specific checked proof gives

\[
 \text{large selected gauge}\quad\lor\quad
 \text{paid reverse boundary participant}.         \tag{7.94}
\]

If the full distance is large only in an unselected payoff or action
coordinate while the selected gauge is small, the second arm of (7.94) has
already paid.  If the first arm holds, (7.86)--(7.93) pay.  Hence one must
retain the selected-dispatch provenance rather than treating the weaker
