full-distance wrapper's left disjunct as a new independent obstruction.

For a sequence of adjacent sources, the player in (7.86) and the observer in
(7.89) stabilize by Fin4 pigeonhole.  The action in the second branch is
already canonically labelled *new boundary*, so no absolute-date
stabilization is needed.  There is no monotone-law conclusion: the identity

\[
 p_{j,N}-\bigl(p_{j,N+1}+b_{j,N}\bigr)              \tag{7.95}
\]

for a censored `Never` discrepancy can oscillate through independently
selected finite Nash laws.  Summing one sign only across a nonconsecutive
subsequence is invalid.  The passive law probe avoids that false rank.

**Scope.**  Every endpoint above is a literal finite-prefix graft of the same
actual tail, and every port has exact update, cap equality, and debt
subtraction.  In the law-probe branch the other prefix laws are passive
`Never`, so the port retains one adjacent source coordinate rather than the
whole old/censored product profile.  Neither source endpoint is proved to lie
on the minimum fibre merely because its suffix `tau` does.  The edges are
strategic alternatives, not consecutive `R.Path` chronology.  The theorem
therefore eliminates the large operational-effect output as a local paid-port
producer, but does not supply the missing paid-port consumer.

The exact graft identity used in (7.89) is the specialization of
`quittingTerminalPayoff_retainedTailMixedTimingProfile_eq_add_prod_none_mul`
in `AdjacentDeadlineRetainedTailReprojection.lean`.  Equations (7.86),
(7.90), and the orientation step are ordinary mathematics.  A narrow search
found no existing declaration or note proving this large-selected-effect to
same-tail paid-port alternative.  Suggested formalization interfaces are

```text
finiteDeadlinePassiveNeverProbe_payoff_sub
finFourAdjacentLargeSelectedEffect_sameTailPaidPort
finFourAdjacentSource_exists_sameTailPaidPort
```

### 7.17 Clean source theorem and exact paid-port waist

The preceding argument combines with the checked small-selected-effect branch
without retaining the full operational pseudometric as an output.

**Fin4 adjacent spectator paid-port theorem (ordinary mathematics).**  Assume
the hypotheses of
`quittingAdjacentDeadline_selectedBoundaryEffectGauge_ge_or_paidReverseParticipant_finFour`:
an adjacent gap source with `gamma>0`, reward bound `M>0`, `gamma/M<=1`, a
literal actual tail `tau` satisfying (7.83), and the checked near-pure-`Never`
observer inequality.  If `tau` realizes a positive global minimum, retain
that fact as additional provenance.  Define

\[
 W_{\rm rev}={27\delta(\gamma/M)^4\over4096},\qquad
 W_{\rm large}=\min\left\{{\delta\gamma\over32M},{\gamma\over8}\right\},
 \qquad W=\min\{W_{\rm rev},W_{\rm large}\}.          \tag{7.96}
\]

Then there are a player `k` and literal actual behavioral profiles `X,Y`
such that

\[
 Y=X[k\leftarrow Y_k],\qquad
 U_k(Y)-U_k(X)\geq W>0,                               \tag{7.97}
\]

\[
 B_k(Y)=B_k(X),qquad
 d_k(Y)=d_k(X)-\bigl(U_k(Y)-U_k(X)\bigr).             \tag{7.98}
\]

Both profiles are finite-prefix grafts of the same `tau`.  More precisely:

* in the small-selected-effect branch, `X,Y` are exactly the censored and
  participant grafts returned by the checked reverse-participant theorem;
* in the large Never-coordinate branch, they are the two passive prefix
  probes of (7.86), oriented toward larger `Never` mass;
* in the large boundary-gain branch, they are one of the two grafted bases
  and its pure-boundary response corner, oriented by the sign of the response
  gain.

The proof is a literal case split on the *selected* gauge.  The small case is
the named checked theorem; the large case is (7.85)--(7.93).  Thus the theorem
also covers the left arm of the checked full-distance wrapper, but it does so
by retaining and re-running its stronger selected-gauge proof.  A bare
hypothesis

\[
 d_{\rm eff}(P,R)\geq\gamma/(8M)                     \tag{7.99}
\]

does not imply (7.97): its only large coordinate may be a prescribed payoff
of one player caused by another player's law.  That observer payoff is not
the mover's unilateral payment.  This is why the source theorem is stated
from the adjacent dispatch data, not from (7.99) alone.

**Probability and agency audit.**  Every finite prefix law is a PMF and the
joint prefix law is the independent product used by the finite timing game.
The probabilities `p_j,c_j,m(P),m(R)` in (7.84) are unconditional prefix
probabilities.  In the passive probe, the only two exhaustive events are
`k` stops finitely, of probability `1-p_k` or `1-c_k`, and every prefix player
passes, of probability `p_k` or `c_k`; no conditional independence or public
randomization is inserted.  On the former event the terminal coalition is
literally `{k}`, and on the latter the complete behavioral profile `tau` is
run.  Equation (7.86) is therefore an actual terminal-payoff identity,
including any positive nonabsorption mass of `tau` under the project's zero
payoff convention.

In the boundary-gain probe, the pure boundary strategy absorbs before the
tail.  Only the prescribed base payoff receives the joint-pass correction
`m(P)u_o` or `m(R)u_o`, which proves (7.89).  Reversing an edge whose displayed
gain is negative changes the observer from the pure boundary strategy back
to its complete mixed-prefix-plus-tail strategy; this is still one permitted
behavioral deviation.  No finite-clock restriction is placed on later
deviations when asserting (7.98).  The cap equality follows because the two
endpoints have identical opponent behavioral strategies, and the debt
identity is then algebraic.

**Decoder into the maintained quantitative waist.**  From (7.97), the source
profile has unrestricted coordinate debt at least `W`, since `Y_k` is one of
the behavioral strategies included in its cap.  Apply the checked theorem

```text
positiveDebt_exists_actualReach_paidRow_withSupport
```

to `(X,k,Delta=W)`.  It returns a
`QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow reward X k (W/4)` with the literal
supported source witness and the quantitative floors

\[
 W\leq4M\,\Pr_X(k\hbox{ survives to the row}),
 \qquad
 W\leq8M\,\texttt{row.liveMass}.                     \tag{7.100}
\]

Here `row.liveMass` is the opponents-only probability of reaching the first
disagreement.  Independence along the all-Continue history therefore also
gives the joint-reach floor `W^2/(32M^2)`.  Thus the port has exactly the
own-survival, opponent-reach, joint-reach, literal-continuation, and
unrestricted-cap data requested by
`FIN4_QUANTITATIVE_PAID_PORT_CONSUMER.md`; its finite prefix gives literal
ancestry from `tau`.  If `z_*=Sem(tau)` is the retained positive global
minimum, these data define a `QuittingPaidCapLiftedSource` with gain `W/4`.
The checked consumer

```text
QuittingPaidCapLiftedSource.nonempty_summablePort
```

then supplies the exact cap-Nash prefix orbit, summable absorption, uniform
suffix reach, and shifted paid row at every finite depth.  Writing
`D_*=D(z_*)`, its exact reach floor is

\[
 {D_*\over D(X)}.
\tag{7.101}
\]

Since every Fin4 payoff and cap coordinate lies in `[-M,M]`, `D(X)<=8M`.
Hence every shifted row has certified gain at least

\[
 {D_*\over8M}{W\over4}={D_*W\over32M}.               \tag{7.102}
\]

The exact cap-lifted identities, rather than (7.102), remain the canonical
statement; the displayed bound only makes uniformity explicit.

**Remaining nonclaims.**  This source theorem strictly removes the large
selected/full operational-effect item from the producer atlas: every branch
now reaches the already maintained quantitative paid-port waist.  It does
not solve that waist.  In particular it does not turn strategic alternatives
`X,Y` into adjacent stages of an admissible Nash--Bellman path, put `X` on the
minimum fibre, make cap-prefix absorption nonsummable, restart the summable
all-Continue port, decrease a source-stable finite rank, or produce terminal
approximate Nash profiles.  The cap-lifted limit remains a semantic/Bellman
port rather than a behavioral profile which reaches `X` after infinitely
many prefixes.

### 7.18 A coherent deadline selection: exact rows and the noncommuting seam

The preceding source theorem can be applied without forgetting which
successor law becomes the next old law.  Fix, once and for all, one exact hard
timing Nash law

\[
 \mu^n\in NE_n\qquad(n\in\mathbb N),                 \tag{7.103}
\]

and use `(mu^n,mu^(n+1))` at the `n`-th adjacent source.  This is coherence of
the selected sequence, not censor compatibility: generally
`C_n mu^(n+1) != mu^n`.

#### 7.18.1 Projective/TV positive alternatives

Regard every marginal as a probability law on `Nat union {infinity}` by
putting zero mass beyond its deadline.  Let

\[
 A_n:=\sum_i\operatorname{TV}(L_n\mu_i^n,\mu_i^{n+1}).\tag{7.104}
\]

The checked adjacent estimate gives

\[
 d_i(\mu^n)\leq4M A_n\quad\hbox{for every }i.         \tag{7.105}
\]

Consequently `liminf A_n=0` supplies actual all-behavior terminal approximate
Nash profiles and hence a uniform-equilibrium payoff by the named checked
vanishing-adjacent-distance consumer.  More strongly, if

\[
 \sum_n A_n<\infty,                                   \tag{7.106}
\]

then the embedded laws are Cauchy in total variation.  Their marginal limits
give independent stopping laws `mu^infinity`.  Products converge in total
variation, so prescribed payoffs and every fixed pure-time or `Never`
deviation payoff converge.  Passing the finite-game Nash inequalities to the
limit and then using pure-time extremality proves that `mu^infinity` is an
exact terminal Nash profile against every behavioral deviation.

Under a hypothetical global terminal gap `gamma>0`, neither alternative can
occur: the checked source gives `A_n>=gamma/(4M)` for every `n`.  Thus the
coherent selection still lives in the separated branch.

#### 7.18.2 A lossless consecutive-time paid row at every deadline

For `n>=1`, let `o_n` be the observer selected from `mu^n`.  In the included successor
clock, its newly exposed time `n` has gain at least `gamma`.  The old finite
action `n-1` belongs to the deadline-`n` Nash menu, so its gain is nonpositive.
Therefore, against the same opponents,

\[
 V_{o_n}(n;\mu^n_{-o_n})-
 V_{o_n}(n-1;\mu^n_{-o_n})\geq\gamma.                \tag{7.107}
\]

Graft the same actual tail `tau` after the successor clock and define `X_n`
by prescribing pure time `n-1` to `o_n`, and `Y_n` by prescribing pure time
`n`; all opponents use the included `mu^n` laws.  Both displayed strategies
stop before the tail, so (7.107) is unchanged by the graft.  Hence

\[
 Y_n=X_n[o_n\leftarrow Q_n],\qquad
 U_{o_n}(Y_n)-U_{o_n}(X_n)\geq\gamma,                \tag{7.108}
\]

with exact cap equality and debt subtraction.  The first disagreement is at
`n-1`; the source player reaches it surely, and the paid-row inequality itself
forces opponents-only reach at least `gamma/(2M)`.  This is a stronger
source-attached row than one obtained by discarding the chain into an
operational-effect branch.

It still is not a chronological chain, because `Y_n` is not `X_(n+1)`.

#### 7.18.3 Constant-observer runs force a negative reprojection budget

Suppose `o_n=i` for every `n` in a consecutive interval `[a,b]`.  The target
`Y_n` and next source `X_(n+1)` prescribe the same pure time `n` to player `i`.
They differ only in replacing the opponents' selected Nash laws `mu^n_-i`
by `mu^(n+1)_-i` (and in an irrelevant empty final timing row after player
`i` already stops).  Define the exact source-reprojection seam

\[
 e_n:=U_i(X_{n+1})-U_i(Y_n).                          \tag{7.109}
\]

Writing `w_n=U_i(Y_n)-U_i(X_n)>=gamma`, the literal alternating chain of
payoff numbers telescopes:

\[
 \sum_{n=a}^bw_n+\sum_{n=a}^{b-1}e_n
   =U_i(Y_b)-U_i(X_a).                                \tag{7.110}
\]

Both endpoint payoffs lie in `[-M,M]`.  Thus, with `e_n^-:=max(0,-e_n)`,

\[
 \boxed{\sum_{n=a}^{b-1}e_n^-
   \geq(b-a+1)\gamma-2M.}                            \tag{7.111}
\]

This is the strongest honest linked-sequence conclusion.  A long
constant-observer run cannot have summable, or even sublinear relative,
source-reprojection loss.  Coupling the opponent laws also gives

\[
 |e_n|\leq2M\sum_{j\ne i}
   \operatorname{TV}(L_n\mu_j^n,\mu_j^{n+1}),        \tag{7.112}
\]

so (7.111) locates the loss inside the actual adjacent-law displacement.
But `e_n<0` is an externality caused by changing opponents; its reverse is
not a unilateral gain of the opponents who caused it.  It is therefore not
an admissible charged-relation edge.

If the observer word has arbitrarily long constant runs, (7.111) forces this
macroscopic negative seam on those runs.  If its constant runs have uniformly
bounded length, observer switches occur with positive density.  Passing to an
infinite subsequence with one stabilized Fin4 observer then skips intervening
deadlines, so `new_n=old_(n+1)` no longer supplies the connector.  Finite
label stabilization does not repair chronology.

There is also a finite-prefix version which does not choose between those two
cases qualitatively.  Partition `o_1,...,o_N` into their
maximal constant runs, and let `R_N` be the number of runs (one plus the number
of switch dates).  Summing (7.110) separately on those runs and paying at most
`2M` for the two endpoints of each run gives

\[
 \boxed{\sum_{\substack{1\leq n<N\\o_n=o_{n+1}}}e_n^-
       \geq N\gamma-2MR_N.}                         \tag{7.112a}
\]

Equivalently,

\[
 N\gamma\leq2MR_N+
  2M\sum_{\substack{1\leq n<N\\o_n=o_{n+1}}}
       \sum_{j\ne o_n}
       \operatorname{TV}(L_n\mu_j^n,\mu_j^{n+1}).  \tag{7.112b}
\]

Thus either the same-observer negative-seam bill is at least `N gamma/2`,
or `R_N>=N gamma/(4M)`.  The obstruction is quantitatively exhaustive:
linear paid weight cannot disappear merely by alternating short runs; it is
then recorded as linear observer-switch count.

#### 7.18.4 Exact censor accounting

For a fixed player `k`, write

\[
 z_{n,k}=\mu_k^n(\mathsf{none}),\qquad
 b_{n,k}=\mu_k^{n+1}(n),                              \tag{7.113}
\]

and let

\[
 E_{n,k}=\operatorname{TV}(\mu_k^n,C_n\mu_k^{n+1}).  \tag{7.114}
\]

A direct coordinate calculation gives the exact, not merely triangular,
censor identity

\[
 \boxed{
 \operatorname{TV}(L_n\mu_k^n,\mu_k^{n+1})
 =E_{n,k}+
 \min\{b_{n,k},(z_{n,k}-z_{n+1,k})_+\}.}             \tag{7.115}
\]

Indeed, if `a=z_(n,k)-z_(n+1,k)`, the difference of the two TV expressions is

\[
 \tfrac12\bigl(b_{n,k}+|a|-|a-b_{n,k}|\bigr)
 =\min\{b_{n,k},a_+\}.                               \tag{7.116}
\]

Thus newly exposed boundary mass need not add its whole mass to adjacent TV;
part of it can be absorbed by the `none` discrepancy.  The usual `E+b` bound
is valid but loses this orientation.

The same accounting exposes why the law-coordinate ports do not telescope.
Put `h_k=U_k(tau)-r_k({k})>0`, and use passive opponent prefixes as in
Section 7.16.  To avoid collision with the scalar `A_n` in (7.104), let

* `P_n` use player `k`'s law `mu_k^n`, with payoff `s_k+z_(n,k)h_k`;
* `B_n` use `C_n mu_k^(n+1)`, with payoff
  `s_k+(z_(n+1,k)+b_(n,k))h_k`;
* `C_(n+1)` use `mu_k^(n+1)`, with payoff
  `s_k+z_(n+1,k)h_k`.

Then the censor triangle is exactly

\[
\begin{aligned}
 U_k(B_n)-U_k(P_n)
   &=h_k(z_{n+1,k}+b_{n,k}-z_{n,k}),\\
 U_k(B_n)-U_k(C_{n+1})&=h_kb_{n,k},\\
 U_k(C_{n+1})-U_k(P_n)&=h_k(z_{n+1,k}-z_{n,k}).
\end{aligned}                                        \tag{7.117}
\]

The censor edge is always oriented `C_(n+1) -> B_n`.  The operational edge
between `P_n,B_n` is oriented toward whichever has the larger pass
coefficient.  If its sign reverses, it points backward in deadline index; if
it points forward, reaching `C_(n+1)` spends exactly the boundary term in
(7.117).  Every positively oriented passive-law edge increases the scalar
potential

\[
 \Phi_k(z)=r_k(\{k\})+z h_k.                          \tag{7.118}
\]

Therefore any genuinely concatenated directed path made only of these paid
law/censor edges has total gain at most `h_k<=2M`.  For arbitrary selected
ports `(S_m,T_m)` of fixed player `k`, with paid weights `w_m`, any proposed
forward connectors have signed seams

\[
 s_m=\Phi_k(S_{m+1})-\Phi_k(T_m),
\]

and the exact telescope gives

\[
 \sum_m s_m^-\geq\sum_mw_m-h_k.                      \tag{7.119}
\]

Thus divergent port weight forces divergent negative reprojection loss.  A
sign-stabilized nonconsecutive subsequence does not change (7.119); the
skipped source resets are precisely the missing negative terms.

#### 7.18.5 The response-effect square is noncommuting

For the boundary-gain branch, fix an observer `i` and response `Q_n`.  At the
two same-tail bases `P_n,R_n`, put

\[
\begin{array}{ll}
 x=U_i(P_n),&x^Q=U_i(P_n[i\leftarrow Q_n]),\\
 y=U_i(R_n),&y^Q=U_i(R_n[i\leftarrow Q_n]).
\end{array}
\]

The horizontal gains are `g_P=x^Q-x` and `g_R=y^Q-y`; the two vertical
source-reprojection seams are `s=y-x` and `t=y^Q-x^Q`.  Their exact curl is

\[
 \boxed{g_P-g_R=s-t.}                                 \tag{7.120}
\]

Hence a large operational response effect is exactly a large failure of the
response update to commute with source reprojection.  In particular

\[
 |g_P-g_R|\geq c\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \max\{|s|,|t|\}\geq c/2.                            \tag{7.121}
\]

Transporting the paid horizontal response across the adjacent source must
therefore pay one of these macroscopic vertical seams.  The identity gives no
sign or unilateral payer for that seam.  At the next deadline the response
label also changes from `Q_n` to `Q_(n+1)`; Nash supplies a new paid horizontal
row, but no equality identifies its source corner with the previous response
corner.

Equations (7.117) and (7.120) are the requested exact noncommuting diagrams.
They show why choosing `new_n=old_(n+1)` is useful but insufficient: censoring
interposes a signed scalar reset in the law branch, while source reprojection
and response installation have macroscopic curl in the gain branch.

#### 7.18.6 Switch dates: two opposite response charts and the collision residue

The switch alternative in (7.112a) has more structure than an anonymous
change of label.  Let `P_n` be the actual hard timing profile induced by
`mu^n`, and for a player `k` and pure time `t` put

\[
 G_k^t(P):=U_k(P[k\leftarrow Q_t])-U_k(P).           \tag{7.122}
\]

Suppose `o_n=i`, `o_(n+1)=j`, and `i!=j`.  The current and next adjacent
sources give

\[
 G_i^n(P_n)\geq\gamma,\qquad
 G_i^n(P_{n+1})\leq0,
 \qquad G_j^{n+1}(P_{n+1})\geq\gamma.                \tag{7.123}
\]

The middle inequality holds because time `n` is already in the
deadline-`n+1` Nash menu.  The last response is evaluated in the actual
behavioral game, so it is also legal at `P_n`, even though it is two dates
beyond the last old menu action.  Write

\[
 a_n:=G_j^{n+1}(P_n).
\]

There are two exact first alternatives.

* If `a_n>=gamma/2`, then the one literal source `P_n` has two distinct paid
  behavioral deviations: player `i` gains at least `gamma` by `Q_n`, and
  player `j` gains at least `gamma/2` by `Q_(n+1)`.
* If `a_n<gamma/2`, then the same adjacent profile pair carries two oppositely
  oriented common-pure-target operational charts:

  \[
  G_i^n(P_n)-G_i^n(P_{n+1})\geq\gamma,
  \qquad
  G_j^{n+1}(P_{n+1})-G_j^{n+1}(P_n)>\gamma/2.        \tag{7.124}
  \]

  The pure target is fixed within each displayed difference, but the
  observer's prescribed law is allowed to change from `P_n` to `P_(n+1)`;
  these are operational-gain charts, not common ordered-response-pair charts.
  In particular, putting
  `A=G_i^n(P_n)-G_j^(n+1)(P_n)` and
  `B=G_i^n(P_(n+1))-G_j^(n+1)(P_(n+1))`,

  \[
  A-B>3\gamma/2,
  \quad\text{so}\quad
  \max\{A,-B\}>3\gamma/4.                           \tag{7.125}
  \]

The last display is the literal observer-switch seam.  Its large number is
an interpersonal difference of two players' response gains, not a unilateral
gain.

Independently of the split on `a_n`, let
`I=Q_n` for player `i`, `J=Q_(n+1)` for player `j`, and use commuting update
notation `P_n^I,P_n^J,P_n^(I,J)`.  Define the gain of the second response
after installing the first by

\[
 b_n:=U_j(P_n^{I,J})-U_j(P_n^I).
\]

If `b_n>=gamma/4`, then

\[
 P_n\ \xrightarrow[i]{\ \geq\gamma\ }\ P_n^I
 \ \xrightarrow[j]{\ \geq\gamma/4\ }\ P_n^{I,J}   \tag{7.126}
\]

is a genuine source-target-matched two-edge paid chain with two distinct
movers.  If `b_n<gamma/4` and the first alternative `a_n>=gamma/2` also
holds, then instead

\[
 \bigl[U_j(P_n^J)-U_j(P_n)\bigr]
 -\bigl[U_j(P_n^{I,J})-U_j(P_n^I)\bigr]>\gamma/4.   \tag{7.127}
\]

Thus, within the two-paid-deviations-at-`P_n` arm, the only local failure of
the canonical two-edge concatenation is a
macroscopic common-`J` response collision caused by installing `I`.  This is
an actual four-profile behavioral square.  It has no sign for player `i` on
the vertical comparison other than the already paid first edge, and no sign
for player `j` on the response after `I`.

There is a simpler source-reprojection form which applies without first
splitting on `a_n`.  Put

\[
 B_n=P_n^I,\qquad C_n=P_n^{I,J},\qquad
 B_{n+1}=P_{n+1}^J.
\]

If `b_n<gamma/4`, then the very same complete response
`J=Q_(n+1)` gains at least `gamma` at `P_(n+1)`.  Hence

\[
 G_j^{n+1}(P_{n+1})-G_j^{n+1}(B_n)>3\gamma/4.       \tag{7.127a}
\]

Define the baseline and installed-response reprojection seams

\[
 s_n=U_j(P_{n+1})-U_j(B_n),\qquad
 t_n=U_j(B_{n+1})-U_j(C_n).
\]

The exact response curl gives

\[
 t_n-s_n
 =G_j^{n+1}(P_{n+1})-G_j^{n+1}(B_n)>3\gamma/4.
\]

Consequently

\[
 \boxed{\quad t_n>3\gamma/8\quad\text{or}\quad
                s_n<-3\gamma/8.\quad}               \tag{7.127b}
\]

Thus every observer switch yields either the literal two-mover charged
connector (7.126), a positive next-target reprojection seam `C_n -> B_(n+1)`,
or a comparably large loss on the actual current-target to next-source seam
`B_n -> P_(n+1)`.  The first is an admissible paid path.  The other two are
not: `C_n` is reached only by taking the possibly unprofitable second
response of gain `b_n`, and `B_n -> P_(n+1)` changes several opponents of
player `j`.  This is the exact noncommuting diagram left by positive-density
observer rotation.

The switch costs also admit one global finite-prefix identity.  For every
`n`, use the direct paid boundary-response target

\[
 B_n:=P_n[o_n\leftarrow Q_n],\qquad
 w_n:=U_{o_n}(B_n)-U_{o_n}(P_n)\geq\gamma.
\]

For the transition from `n` to `n+1`, put

\[
 s_n:=U_{o_{n+1}}(P_{n+1})-U_{o_{n+1}}(B_n),
 \qquad
 c_n:=U_{o_{n+1}}(B_n)-U_{o_n}(B_n).                \tag{7.127c}
\]

Here `s_n` is the literal current-target to next-source reprojection seam,
and `c_n` is zero on a constant-observer transition but is an interpersonal
payoff-coordinate switch otherwise.  With
`phi_n=U_(o_n)(B_n)`, there is no estimate or approximation:

\[
 \phi_{n+1}-\phi_n=s_n+w_{n+1}+c_n.
\]

Therefore every prefix of `N` transitions satisfies

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{n=0}^{N-1}s_n^-+
 \sum_{n=0}^{N-1}c_n^-
 \geq N\gamma-2M.}                                  \tag{7.127d}
\]

This is the global accumulation law missing from the runwise calculation.
It does not lose short alternating runs: if negative source reprojection is
sublinear, negative observer-switch cost is linear.  Conversely, if both
negative bills were summable relative to the paid weight, the bounded scalar
`phi_n` would contradict the divergent paid sum.

The identity is still not a charged return.  A negative `s_n` changes the
whole profile, and a negative `c_n` compares two players at one profile.
Even after stabilizing an ordered switch pair, the profiles carrying its
cost may have different payoff vectors; a directed cycle in the finite label
graph is not a scalar payoff cycle.  Thus (7.127d) isolates the remaining
global theorem exactly: one must convert a linear accumulated `s^-` or `c^-`
bill into an actual unilateral path, or prove that source coherence makes
the relevant target payoff vectors recurrent in a common chart.

There is nevertheless a genuine Fin4 localization of each no-chain switch.
Assume `b_n<gamma/4`, so (7.127b) applies, and retain
`j=o_(n+1)`.

If `t_n>3gamma/8`, the profiles `C_n` and `B_(n+1)` have the same complete
strategy `Q_(n+1)` for `j` and differ only in the other three coordinates.
Replace those coordinates one at a time.  Their three observer-payoff
increments sum to `t_n`, so one actual unilateral opponent update has mover
`k!=j` and observer payoff gain

\[
 U_j(H[k\leftarrow\rho])-U_j(H)>\gamma/8.           \tag{7.127e}
\]

If instead `s_n<-3gamma/8`, run the hybrid from `P_(n+1)` toward `B_n`.
Replace `j` first by its component in `B_n`.  Because this is a switch,
`j!=o_n`, so that component is precisely the old law `mu_j^n`, embedded in
the deadline-`n+1` action menu.  Exact Nash of `mu^(n+1)` says that this first
own-coordinate replacement has nonpositive payoff gain for `j`.  The
remaining three opponent replacements therefore have total `j`-payoff gain
strictly greater than `3gamma/8`, and again one of them satisfies (7.127e).

Thus a switch which does not give the matched two-edge paid chain always
gives a literal one-opponent externality edge of scale `gamma/8`, with the
next observer's strategy fixed along that edge.  This conclusion uses all
four behavioral profiles and exact agency; it is stronger than the
interpersonal scalar `c_n`.

It also carries a uniform complete-terminal atom.  Decompose the payoff
difference in (7.127e) over the `15` nonempty Fin4 coalitions.  Some terminal
`S` has a signed contribution greater than `gamma/120`.  Under the reward
bound, the corresponding terminal-mass difference has magnitude greater
than `gamma/(120M)`, and the endpoint with the matching sign has actual
terminal mass at least that amount.  What is not supplied is mover payment:
the selected coordinate update may benefit `j` while decreasing the mover's
own payoff.  Consequently this is a source-derived actual-law/terminal-atom
externality packet, not a selected chronological stage, a
`QuittingPaidCapPort`, or an admissible return.

For completeness, let `m=U_k(H[k<-rho])-U_k(H)` be the selected mover's own
payoff change in (7.127e).  There is an exact final trichotomy:

\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 m\geq\gamma/16 &: \text{the displayed forward update is paid};\\
 m\leq-\gamma/16 &: \text{the literal reverse update is paid};\\
 |m|<\gamma/16 &: \text{the mover is nearly neutral while `j` gains
                         more than `gamma/8`.}
 \end{array}                                          \tag{7.127f}
\]

The first two arms enter the maintained paid-port waist after choosing the
profitable orientation; in the negative-`s_n` branch the forward orientation
also points from the next-source hybrid back toward the preceding target.
The third arm is the precise unpaid switch residue.  It cannot be discarded:
the reward coordinates controlling mover payoff and observer payoff are
independent even on the terminal atom selected above.  No minimum-fibre field
has been retained by the coordinate hybrid.

The near-neutral residue admits no universal fixed weighted social/cap
potential.  This has an exact zero-minimum Fin4 regression on the same Gray
square used by the cyclic-plateau controls, but with different payoffs.  Put

\[
 S_0=\{2\},\quad S_1=\{0,2\},\quad
 S_2=\{0,1,2\},\quad S_3=\{1,2\},                   \tag{7.127g}
\]

and use the literal toggle cycle with movers `0,1,0,1`.  Give players `0,1`
payoff zero at every terminal.  Give player `2` the values `0,1,0,1` on
`S_0,S_1,S_2,S_3`, and player `3` the values `1,0,1,0`; set every unlisted
coordinate to zero and the all-Continue payoff to zero.

At the four pure stationary sure-exit profiles, every transition is one
complete-strategy update.  The mover's own payoff change is exactly zero.
Choose observers `2,3,2,3` in phase order.  Their payoff changes are exactly
one:

\[
 \Delta U_{\mathrm{mover}}=0,qquad
 \Delta U_{\mathrm{observer}}=1                    \tag{7.127h}
\]

on all four edges, and the source and target terminal atoms have mass one.
The unselected observer coordinate falls by one on the same edge.

Every displayed profile is in fact an exact all-behavior terminal Nash
profile.  Players `0,1` are payoff-indifferent everywhere; player `2` can
only leave the sure-exit coalition for payoff zero; player `3` can only join
it for payoff zero.  All Continue is also exact, so the global minimum debt
is zero.  At every displayed profile payoff equals cap coordinatewise.

For any fixed weights, a weighted sum of payoff and cap coordinates reduces
on this family to one scalar function on the four vertices.  Its increments
sum to zero around the literal closed update cycle.  More explicitly, the
two edge types change `(U_2,U_3)` by `(1,-1)` and `(-1,1)`, so no fixed
positive social weights orient both.  Hence iteration, label recurrence,
mass-one atom provenance, exact Nash, and exact mover neutrality do not
produce a positive weighted return at this interface.  A successful
positive-minimum argument must use a field excluding this zero-debt cycle;
no universal payoff/cap potential can do so.

The earlier response-collision arm is real even for two coherent adjacent sources, rather than an
artifact of abstract scalar charts.  Here is an exact rational Fin4
regression.  Players `0,1` are active and `2,3` are payoff-zero dummies.  An
active player receives `1` iff the terminal coalition contains exactly one
of `{0,1}`, and receives `0` otherwise.  Thus both active players prefer
either active singleton to their collision; all rewards have absolute value
at most `M=1`.

At deadline `n`, let each active player's timing law be uniform on

\[
 \{0,1,\ldots,n-1,\mathsf{Never}\},                 \tag{7.128}
\]

and let both dummies play `Never`.  Against this law, an active pure time
`t<n` receives `n/(n+1)`: the opponent collides with probability `1/(n+1)`
and otherwise exactly one active player belongs to the first terminal
coalition.  `Never` also receives `n/(n+1)`.  Hence these are exact mixed
Nash laws `mu^n`, with unrestricted mixed deviations reduced exactly to their
pure timing values.  The newly exposed boundary time has payoff `1`, and
therefore gain and terminal debt exactly `1/(n+1)`.

Take the two sources `(mu^0,mu^1)` and `(mu^1,mu^2)`, common scale
`gamma=1/3`, and select observers `o_0=0`, `o_1=1`.  The new boundary action
has gain zero at `mu^1` and `mu^2`, respectively, because it belongs to the
new Nash menu.  Moreover

\[
 \sum_k\operatorname{TV}(L_0\mu_k^0,\mu_k^1)=1,
 \qquad
 \sum_k\operatorname{TV}(L_1\mu_k^1,\mu_k^2)=2/3,   \tag{7.129}
\]

so both local adjacent-TV fields dominate `gamma/(4M)=1/12`, and the successor
law of the first source is literally the old law of the second.

At `P_0` all players play `Never`.  Player `0` gains `1` from time `0`, and
player `1` gains `1` from time `1`.  After either canonical response is
installed, installing the other changes the first active singleton into the
other active singleton, or leaves the earlier one first; the second mover's
payoff remains `1`.  Hence its incremental gain is exactly zero in either
order.  The response-collision loss in (7.127) is exactly `1`.
For the reactivation square (7.127a), player `1`'s response gain rises from
`0` at `B_0=P_0^{Q_0}` to `1/2` at `P_1`.  The whole rise is paid by the
negative baseline seam: `s_0=-1/2` and `t_0=0`.  Thus even literal coherence
and exact finite-game Nash laws do not orient the reactivation seam as a
positive connector.

This is an all-behavior, source-coherent falsifier of the implication
"observer switch plus two source gains gives the canonical two-edge paid
connector."  It is not a counterexample to a fixed positive-gap infinite
chain: here the boundary debt is `1/(n+1)` and tends to zero.  Consequently it
does not refute a global argument that essentially uses one uniform `gamma`;
it proves that such an argument must accumulate the uniform scale across
switch dates, rather than close a switch from one two-response square.

#### 7.18.7 Positive-minimum cap-or-debt response chase

The unpaid residue in (7.127f) has one exact interaction with the positive
global minimum.  This interaction must be stated at a genuinely near-minimum
endpoint; finite replacement ancestry by itself is not enough.

Let `sigma` be an actual profile with

\[
 D(\sigma)\leq D_*+\varepsilon,
\]

and let `tau=sigma[k<-rho]`.  Suppose `j!=k` has observer gain

\[
 e=U_j(\tau)-U_j(\sigma)>0.
\]

Put `Delta B_j=B_j(tau)-B_j(sigma)`.  There is an exact identity

\[
 \boxed{\quad e=\Delta B_j+d_j(\sigma)-d_j(\tau).\quad}       \tag{7.127i}
\]

Consequently at least one of the following occurs:

\[
 \Delta B_j\geq e/2,                                      \tag{7.127j}
\]

or

\[
 d_j(\sigma)-d_j(\tau)\geq e/2.                          \tag{7.127k}
\]

The second branch is a genuine target-attached paid-port producer.  Global
minimality and (7.127k) give

\[
 \sum_{p\ne j}\bigl(d_p(\tau)-d_p(\sigma)\bigr)
 =D(\tau)-D(\sigma)+d_j(\sigma)-d_j(\tau)
 \geq e/2-\varepsilon.                                  \tag{7.127l}
\]

In Fin4 some `p!=j` therefore satisfies

\[
 d_p(\tau)-d_p(\sigma)\geq {e/2-\varepsilon\over3}.
                                                               \tag{7.127m}
\]

Since source debts are nonnegative, the same lower bound holds for
`d_p(tau)`.  Pure-time/Never extremality of the unrestricted behavioral cap
then selects, for every `eta>0`, a literal pure-time or Never response of
player `p` at `tau` with gain at least

\[
 {e/2-\varepsilon\over3}-\eta.                         \tag{7.127n}
\]

Thus, if `e>=E`, `epsilon<=E/8`, and `eta=E/16`, this response gains at least
`E/16`.  It starts at the actual target `tau`; it is not a horizontally
reselected sibling.  This closes the debt-drop branch into the maintained
paid-port waist.

The first branch is different.  Choose an `eta`-near-cap pure-time/Never
response `r` for player `j` against the target opponents, and put

\[
 \sigma'=\sigma[j\leftarrow r],\qquad
 \tau'=\tau[j\leftarrow r].
\]

The `j`- and `k`-updates commute, while the cap at `sigma` bounds the same
response there.  Hence

\[
 U_j(\tau')-U_j(\sigma')
 \geq B_j(\tau)-B_j(\sigma)-\eta
 \geq e/2-\eta,                                      \tag{7.127o}
\]

and `d_j(tau')<=eta`.  Therefore a cap-compensated unpaid edge lifts to a
second literal externality edge, with the same mover and observer and with
the observer near its cap at the target.  If the mover's own payoff change
on this lifted edge has magnitude at least a chosen floor, one orientation
is again paid.  Otherwise the lift remains an unpaid externality.

There is no automatic iteration of (7.127l) in this cap-rise branch.  The
new source `sigma'` need not satisfy any near-minimum upper bound: replacing
`j` by a response selected against the *target* opponents can reactivate the
other three debts by an arbitrary bounded amount.  If
`D(sigma')-D_*` is macroscopic, this is precisely an off-minimum transition.
If it is small, (7.127o) is a new near-minimum unpaid edge, but selecting the
same target response again is idempotent and gives no label or rank change.
Thus the response chase has an exact fork:

\[
 \boxed{
 \text{target-attached paid response}
 \quad|\quad
 \text{macroscopic off-minimum lift}
 \quad|\quad
 \text{near-minimum cap-transport residue}.}        \tag{7.127p}
\]

The last residue has a global cap-reset ledger on a *literally linked*
sequence.  Suppose for this paragraph that
`sigma_(n+1)=tau_n`, every observer gain is at least `E`, and every edge
falls in (7.127j).  Write

\[
 K_{n,p}=B_p(\sigma_{n+1})-B_p(\sigma_n).
\]

Since the selected coordinate obeys `K_(n,j_n)>=E/2`, while every cap lies
in `[-M,M]`, every prefix of `N` edges satisfies

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{n<N}\sum_p(K_{n,p})^-
 \geq {NE\over2}-8M.}                               \tag{7.127q}
\]

Indeed the sum of all cap increments telescopes to a number at least `-8M`,
and the positive variation contains the `N` selected increases.  A negative
cap increment of player `p` can in turn be witnessed by an old-source
near-cap response: after putting that same response on both endpoints, the
reverse lifted edge raises player `p`'s payoff by the cap drop, up to its
near-cap error.  But that edge points backward in the linked chronology, and
its lifted source again need not be near the minimum.  Hence (7.127q) is a
linear **cap-reset budget**, not an admissible response cycle.

This also identifies the precise mismatch with the coherent deadline family.
The externality edges in (7.127e) are coordinate hybrids inside one adjacent
pair; their targets are not the next such hybrid sources.  The coherent law
identity `new_n=old_(n+1)` therefore does not instantiate the literal linkage
assumed in (7.127q).  Conversely, the cap-lift construction supplies
positive-minimum ancestry and near-minimum semantic states only after adding
new exact prefixes; copying one such prefix across both sides of the
macroscopic externality is not a checked operation.  Finite recurrence of
the mover/observer labels cannot repair either noncommuting square.

This is stronger than the zero-minimum Gray-cycle no-go: positive minimum
does consume the observer-debt-drop arm quantitatively.  What survives is
exactly matched cap/payoff transport, whose accumulated positive cap motion
must be repaid by reverse cap motion or by leaving the minimum neighbourhood.
No positive-`D_*` quitting-game regression is asserted; constructing one
with the full global-minimum property would amount to the unresolved
counterexample direction of the main conjecture.

#### 7.18.8 Cap rise: a reached two-cut interaction or parallel transport

The target-near-cap lift in (7.127o) can be tested at the first disagreement
with the source observer strategy, but an order-one cap rise alone does not
force the two strategy changes to interact.

Freeze the other two players.  Let `s,r` be the source and target-near-cap
strategies of player `j`, and let `a,b` be the two strategies of player `k`.
Write

\[
 F(x,y)=U_j(\text{profile with }j=x, k=y).
\]

The bottom and top `k`-externalities and their square curl are

\[
 E=F(s,b)-F(s,a),\qquad T=F(r,b)-F(r,a),
\]

\[
 \chi=T-E
 =[F(r,b)-F(s,b)]-[F(r,a)-F(s,a)].                 \tag{7.127r}
\]

If `B_j(b)-B_j(a)>=c` and `r` is `eta`-near-cap against `b`, then

\[
 T\geq c-\eta.                                      \tag{7.127s}
\]

There is a literal reached-event consequence when `chi` is large.  Represent
all four behavioral strategies by their stopping-law PMFs on
`Nat union {infinity}`.  Independently sample pure times
`t_r,t_s,u_b,u_a` from those four laws.  Multilinearity of terminal payoff
gives

\[
 \chi=\mathbb E\bigl[
 f(t_r,u_b)-f(t_r,u_a)-f(t_s,u_b)+f(t_s,u_a)\bigr], \tag{7.127t}
\]

where the expectation still includes the two fixed opponents.  Hence some
four supported pure times have pure square curl of magnitude at least
`|chi|`.  Nonzero curl forces both pure-time pairs to differ.  Put

\[
 t_j=\min\{t_r,t_s\},\qquad t_k=\min\{u_b,u_a\}.
\]

If one of the two fixed opponents stops strictly before
`max{t_j,t_k}`, one of the two coordinate changes is screened before its
first disagreement and the pointwise four-corner difference is zero.  Since
the difference of four rewards is bounded by `4M`, the fixed opponents must
reach the later first-disagreement date with probability at least

\[
 \boxed{
 \Pr(\text{fixed opponents reach }\max\{t_j,t_k\})
 \geq {|\chi|\over4M}.}                              \tag{7.127u}
\]

On every outcome contributing to the nonzero curl, one of the four pure
corners also keeps both `j` and `k` alive to that later cut.  Thus (7.127u)
is an actual reached two-cut interaction/collision event for a displayed
pure replacement corner, not a terminal-coalition pigeonhole or a cap-only
number.  It is observer-deleted only before choosing that corner; after the
choice it is literal joint reach.

Combining (7.127s) with a threshold gives the exact physical dichotomy.  For
`eta<=c/4`, either

\[
 |\chi|\geq c/4,
 \qquad
 \Pr(\text{reached two-cut interaction})\geq {c\over16M},
                                                               \tag{7.127v}
\]

or

\[
 E=T-\chi\geq c/2.                                  \tag{7.127w}
\]

The second line is **parallel cap transport**: the source observer strategy
already receives essentially the same positive `k`-externality as the
target-optimal response.  Its large number is physical on the original
edge, but it supplies no interaction with the `j`-replacement and hence no
new chronological connector.

This obstruction is exact, even with positive total debt and a sure terminal
atom.  Let `k=0`, `j=1`, fix player `2` to Quit surely at date zero, and let
player `3` Continue.  Compare `sigma`, where `k` Continues, with `tau`, where
`k` Quits at date zero.  Set

\[
 r_1(S)=\mathbf 1_{\{0,2\}\subseteq S},\qquad
 r_3(S)=\mathbf 1_{\{2,3\}\subseteq S},
\]

and give players `0,2` payoff zero everywhere.  Then

\[
 \operatorname{Law}(\sigma)=\delta_{\{2\}},\qquad
 \operatorname{Law}(\tau)=\delta_{\{0,2\}},
\]

\[
 U_1(\sigma)=B_1(\sigma)=0,qquad
 U_1(\tau)=B_1(\tau)=1,                              \tag{7.127x}
\]

while player `3` has payoff zero and cap one at both profiles.  Hence

\[
 D(\sigma)=D(\tau)=1,qquad
 \Delta U_k=0,qquad \Delta U_j=\Delta B_j=1.       \tag{7.127y}
\]

The prescribed strategy of `j` is already cap-optimal at both endpoints, so
one may take `r=s`; there is no first disagreement and `chi=0`.  Thus a sure
atom, positive debt level, neutral mover, and unit cap rise do not force a
two-strategy collision.  The table has a zero-debt profile after player `3`
takes its profitable response, so its **global** minimum is zero.  It is not
a counterexample to a theorem using `D_*>0` globally.

The global-positive-minimum hypothesis contributes exactly the debt-drop
response (7.127n) and the ever-available maximum-debt response; it does not
by itself put a lower bound on the square curl (7.127r).  Proving such a
bound from the full hard-residual/tangent passport would be a genuinely new
source-coherence theorem.  An exact game with (7.127x)--(7.127y) and positive
global minimum would instead be a conjecture-level counterexample and is not
asserted here.

#### 7.18.9 Literal adapter to the checked post-mark two-cut splice

The large-curl branch of (7.127v) does instantiate the checked
`QuittingUniformlyReachedPostMarkTwoCutBlock`, but the adapter must use one
of the four selected pure corners as an actual root-sequence profile.  The
two counterfactual response cuts themselves are not roots of one sequence.

Let the supported pure times selected in (7.127t) have nonzero pure curl,
and retain the notation

\[
 t_j=\min\{t_r,t_s\},\qquad
 t_k=\min\{u_b,u_a\},\qquad T=\max\{t_j,t_k\}.
\]

If `t_j<=t_k`, nonzero curl implies that one of `t_r,t_s` reaches `t_k`;
otherwise both `j`-strategies would terminate before the first
`k`-disagreement and the `k`-difference would be screened in both rows.
Choose that `j`-strategy and choose the `k`-strategy which stops at `t_k`.
The symmetric choice applies when `t_k<=t_j`.  Thus there is one literal
pure corner `P` such that

1. both selected players reach date `T`;
2. at least one of them Quits surely at date `T`; and
3. the two fixed opponents reach `T` with probability at least
   `|chi|/(4M)`.

Consequently the actual joint live mass of `P` at `T` has the same lower
bound.  Prefix `P` by one deterministic all-Continue row and call its live
root sequence `q`.  Define

\[
 \mathsf{markedRow}=0,
 \qquad \mathsf{entryCut}=T+1,
 \qquad \mathsf{exitCut}=T+2,                        \tag{7.127z}
\]

\[
 \mathsf{reachFloor}={|\chi|\over4M},
 \qquad \mathsf{hazardFloor}=1.                     \tag{7.127aa}
\]

The entry-reach field is exactly item 3 above.  The one-row block beginning
at the entry cut contains a sure quitter, so the sum of its four marginal
Quit probabilities is at least one.  The semantic fields are literal:

\[
 \mathsf{entryPair}=\operatorname{Sem}(P\text{ restarted at }T),
 \qquad
 \mathsf{exitPair}=\operatorname{Sem}(P\text{ restarted at }T+1).
                                                               \tag{7.127ab}
\]

Both are actual carrier points.  Supply the separately retained global
minimum pair as `minimum`; its checked carrier membership, global lower
bound, and positive debt are precisely the remaining fields of
`QuittingPositiveMinimumTwoCutBlock`.  No assertion that either entry or
exit is near the minimum is needed by the record.  The theorem itself tests
the exit's distance above the minimum.

Put

\[
 K=(1-\exp(-1))D_*.
\]

The checked Fin4 theorem
`QuittingUniformlyReachedPostMarkTwoCutBlock.finFour_offMinimum_or_exists_paidSplice`
now gives the literal alternative

\[
 D(\mathsf{exitPair})
 \geq D_*+{\exp(1)-1\over2}D_*,                      \tag{7.127ac}
\]

or one player has a unilateral suffix response whose gain at the entry is
greater than `K/16`, whose target entry debt is at most `K/16`, and whose
parent splice is a literal one-player behavioral update with gain

\[
 \boxed{
 U_p(\mathsf{splice})-U_p(\mathsf{parent})
 >{|\chi|(1-\exp(-1))D_*\over64M}.}                  \tag{7.127ad}
\]

The splice leaves every complete behavioral prescription strictly before
the entry cut unchanged.  Thus the large-curl branch is not merely an
externality label: it produces a checked positive-minimum off-minimum exit
or a physically reached paid splice in the original reward table.

The source ancestry is finite and literal at the profile level after applying
the same silent shift to the whole square.  The shifted corner `P` is obtained
from the shifted externality source by replacing the complete strategies of
`j` and `k`; the checked splice adds one further unilateral replacement.  If
the **corner family itself** is known to converge semantically to the retained
minimum, the checked positive-minimum singleton margin makes the silent row
semantically invisible, exactly as in the existing silent-padding adapter.
Near-minimum provenance of the original source alone does not imply that for
the two-replacement corner.  The record does not require it: the corner and
its exit suffix may be off-minimum, and (7.127ac) is the theorem's honest
output rather than a failed hypothesis.

There is one exact source-facing field which this adapter does **not**
provide.  The two supported pure replacements used to expose the curl need
not agree with the original mixed `j`- and `k`-strategies at dates below
`T`.  Hence an earlier retained marked atom, source law, or tangent row is not
automatically preserved from the original source to the selected corner.
The artificial row `markedRow=0` satisfies the checked record's index field,
but is not a claim that the old marked passport survives.

`positiveDebt_exists_commonPrefix_profitableStoppingLawFork` in
`fable/lean/FableCommonPrefixFork.lean` does not fill this gap.  It starts
from one player's **own positive debt** and builds one profitable stopping-law
fork agreeing with that player's source below its paid row.  Here `chi` is a
two-coordinate payoff externality.  Copying player `j`'s payoff into an
auxiliary `k`-coordinate would make the theorem applicable only in the
auxiliary table and would not make the resulting update profitable for `k`
in the original table.  Nor does the one-coordinate fork preserve both sides
of the four-corner curl simultaneously.

Therefore the exact interface verdict is:

\[
 \boxed{
 \text{large curl}
 \Longrightarrow
 \text{checked off-minimum exit or actual paid splice},
 }
\]

but preserving the **original** tangent/source mark through this direct pure
selection requires controlling the alternatives' stopping laws before that
mark.  The next paragraph gives the exact two-coordinate prefix split;
entry/exit semantics, positive-minimum data, hazard, reach, and finite
replacement ancestry already match.

#### 7.18.10 Two-coordinate common-prefix selector with an exact premark fork

Fix an original marked row `ell` and put `m=ell+1`.  For a stopping law
`mu`, let `pi_m mu` retain its atoms at dates strictly below `m` and censor
all remaining finite and Never mass to one survival symbol.  For a source
law `mu_0` and alternative law `mu_1`, set

\[
 \delta_m(\mu_0,\mu_1)
 =\operatorname{TV}(\pi_m\mu_0,\pi_m\mu_1).          \tag{7.127ae}
\]

There is a canonical source-prefix/alternative-tail splice
`Sp_m(mu_0,mu_1)`: keep every source atom below `m`, and distribute the
source survival mass at `m` according to the alternative law conditional on
survival to `m`.  If the alternative survival is zero, put the remaining
source mass at Never.  A direct atom calculation gives

\[
 \operatorname{TV}\bigl(\mu_1,
          \operatorname{Sp}_m(\mu_0,\mu_1)\bigr)
 =\delta_m(\mu_0,\mu_1).                             \tag{7.127af}
\]

The reconstructed behavioral strategy of the splice is literally equal to
the source strategy at every date below `m`.  This follows directly from
equality of the finite stopping masses and survival denominators below the
cut; it is not an application of the one-coordinate profitable-fork theorem.

Return to the square in (7.127r).  Write

\[
 r_m=\operatorname{Sp}_m(s,r),\qquad
 b_m=\operatorname{Sp}_m(a,b),
\]

\[
 \delta_j=\delta_m(s,r),\qquad
 \delta_k=\delta_m(a,b),
\]

and define the source-prefix curl

\[
 \chi_m=
 F(r_m,b_m)-F(r_m,a)-F(s,b_m)+F(s,a).                \tag{7.127ag}
\]

Changing one stopping law by total variation `delta` changes every bounded
payoff by at most `2M delta`.  Replacing first the `j`-alternative and then
the `k`-alternative in the four terms therefore gives

\[
 \boxed{
 |\chi-\chi_m|\leq4M(\delta_j+\delta_k).}            \tag{7.127ah}
\]

Consequently, if `|chi|>=C>0`, there is an exhaustive alternative:

\[
 \boxed{
 \delta_j+\delta_k\geq {C\over8M}
 \quad\text{or}\quad
 |\chi_m|\geq {C\over2}.}                           \tag{7.127ai}
\]

The first output is an exact **premark source seam**.  At least one of the
two alternative strategies changes an order-one part of its stopping law on
the finite prefix through the original marked row.  No replacement equal to
the source on that prefix can reproduce this part of the curl.  This is a
logical obstruction to a universal common-prefix selector, not a missing
constant.

In the second output, all four strategies in the square agree pairwise with
the original source below `m`.  Conditional on their common survival to
`m`, represent each suffix law as a mixture of deterministic suffix stopping
times, including Never.  Multilinearity selects four supported
pure suffixes with curl of magnitude at least `|chi_m|`.  Reattach each pure
suffix to the common original prefix.  The resulting four complete
behavioral strategies remain literally equal to the source below `m`.

Let `T>=m` be the later of the two selected suffix first-disagreement dates.
Exactly as in (7.127u), nonzero curl is screened unless the two fixed
opponents and the two common source prefixes reach `T`.  One pure-suffix
corner `P_m` has a sure quitter at `T` and

\[
 \Pr_{P_m}(\text{all players reach }T)
 \geq {|\chi_m|\over4M}
 \geq {C\over8M}.                                   \tag{7.127aj}
\]

Now use the *original* mark and no artificial silent row:

\[
 \mathsf{markedRow}=\ell,
 \qquad \mathsf{entryCut}=m,
 \qquad \mathsf{exitCut}=T+1,                       \tag{7.127ak}
\]

\[
 \mathsf{reachFloor}={C\over8M},
 \qquad \mathsf{hazardFloor}=1.                     \tag{7.127al}
\]

The root sequence is the actual live-root sequence of `P_m`.  Its entry and
exit pairs are literally

\[
 \mathsf{entryPair}=\operatorname{Sem}(P_m
       \text{ restarted at }m),
 \qquad
 \mathsf{exitPair}=\operatorname{Sem}(P_m
       \text{ restarted at }T+1).                   \tag{7.127am}
\]

They are actual carrier points.  The entry reach is bounded by (7.127aj),
and the block contains the sure-Quit row at `T`, so its total marginal hazard
is at least one.  Supplying the retained positive global minimum pair gives
a literal `QuittingUniformlyReachedPostMarkTwoCutBlock` whose complete roots
are equal to the original source roots at every date through the marked row.

With

\[
 K=(1-\exp(-1))D_*,
\]

the checked Fin4 consumer gives either

\[
 D(\mathsf{exitPair})
 \geq D_*+{\exp(1)-1\over2}D_*,                     \tag{7.127an}
\]

or a literal parent-level unilateral paid splice with payoff gain

\[
 \boxed{
 U_p(\mathsf{splice})-U_p(P_m)
 >{C(1-\exp(-1))D_*\over128M}.}                     \tag{7.127ao}
\]

The checked splice is unchanged before `entryCut=m`.  Hence it preserves the
original marked row, its reach and coalition atoms, every terminal branch
ending by that row, and the finite-replacement ancestry: `P_m` uses at most
two complete replacements of the original source, both equal to it before
`m`, and the paid splice adds one replacement with the same common prefix.
No near-minimum assertion about the two-replacement corner is smuggled in;
the consumer uses the retained global minimum separately and returns the
off-minimum alternative (7.127an) when the exit is far from it.

The premark arm in (7.127ai) is real even for a unit cap rise, a sure marked
atom, and positive debt at both displayed endpoints.  Take players
`j=0,k=1,h=2,p=3`.  In the source let `j,k,h` use pure times `0,1,2` and let
`p` Never stop.  The marked row is zero.  Compare the `j`-alternative time
`2` and the `k`-alternative time `2`, keeping `h` at time `2`.  Set

\[
 r_j(S)=\mathbf 1_{\{j,k,h\}\subseteq S},
 \qquad
 r_p(S)=\mathbf 1_{\{j,p\}\subseteq S},             \tag{7.127ap}
\]

and give `k,h` payoff zero.  Against source `k` at time `1`, player `j`'s cap
is zero.  Against target `k` at time `2`, its time-`2` response gives one, so
the cap rise is one.  The four `j`-payoffs are

\[
 F(s,a)=F(r,a)=F(s,b)=0,
 \qquad F(r,b)=1,                                   \tag{7.127aq}
\]

and therefore `chi=1`.  Player `p` has payoff zero and cap one at both the
source and the top corner, so both displayed total debts equal one.  Yet any
two replacements agreeing with the source through marked row zero retain
player `j`'s sure Quit there; player `k`'s later change is screened and the
postmark curl is exactly zero.  Here

\[
 \delta_j=1,\qquad\delta_k=0.                        \tag{7.127ar}
\]

After player `p` takes its profitable response this table has a zero-debt
profile, so its global minimum is zero.  The example is an exact obstruction
to deriving common-prefix selection from the local cap-rise, debt-level,
atom, and ancestry fields.  It does not refute an additional theorem saying
that full positive-global-minimum tangent provenance rules out the premark
arm.  Such a theorem is now the sole missing positive-minimum statement:

\[
 \boxed{
 \text{retained positive-minimum source passport}
 \Longrightarrow
 \delta_j+\delta_k<{C\over8M}
 \text{ (or directly consumes the premark seam).}}   \tag{7.127as}
\]

Subject to that one inequality, the two-coordinate common-prefix selector
and the checked paid-splice consumer are complete with the original mark and
source ancestry.

#### 7.18.10.1 The premark seam is a reached absorption block

The first arm of (7.127ai) is not only a TV obstruction.  It has a literal
source-attached physical decoder, and positive minimum prices the resulting
block.

Let the original source profile be `sigma`, and let `alpha>0` be the stage
mass with which the retained coalition `K` terminates at date `ell`.
Keep `m=ell+1`.  From

\[
 \delta_j+\delta_k\ge {C\over8M}
\]

choose `i` in `{j,k}` with

\[
 \delta_i\ge \theta:={C\over16M}>0.                 \tag{7.127at}
\]

This branch is possible only when `theta<=1`.  Write `mu_0` for `i`'s
source stopping law and `mu_1` for its alternative law.  Since their
`m`-censored laws differ, there is a first date

\[
 t<m\quad\hbox{such that}\quad \mu_0(t)\ne\mu_1(t). \tag{7.127au}
\]

They have identical finite atoms before `t`, hence identical reconstructed
behavioral hazards there.  Put

\[
 e_x=\mu_x([t,m))\qquad(x=0,1).
\]

If `P` and `N` are respectively the sums of the positive and negative parts
of `mu_1(u)-mu_0(u)` over `t<=u<m`, the survival-lump difference is `N-P`.
Consequently

\[
 \delta_i={P+N+|N-P|\over2}=\max\{P,N\}
 \le\max\{e_0,e_1\}.                               \tag{7.127av}
\]

Choose `x` attaining the last maximum and let `P_i` be the actual profile
obtained from `sigma` by using `mu_x` for player `i` and leaving all other
complete strategies unchanged.  If `x=0`, this is just `sigma`; if `x=1`,
it is one literal complete-strategy replacement.  In either case `P_i` is
identical to `sigma` at every live history strictly before `t`.

The retained marked event at `ell` is contained in the source joint-reach
event at `t`.  Thus

\[
 \Pr_{P_i}(\operatorname{reach}t)
 =\Pr_\sigma(\operatorname{reach}t)\ge\alpha.       \tag{7.127aw}
\]

Conditional on this reach, player `i` stops in `[t,m)` with probability
`e_x/s_x(t)`, where `s_x(t)<=1` is its survival to `t`.  By (7.127av) this
is at least `delta_i`.  Opponent stopping can only make absorption earlier
inside the same block.  Hence the actual root word of `P_i` on `[t,m)` has

\[
 \boxed{
 \Pr_{P_i}(\operatorname{absorb\ in\ }[t,m)\mid
                 \operatorname{reach}t)
 \ge\delta_i\ge\theta,}                            \tag{7.127ax}
\]

and its unconditional block absorption is at least `alpha*theta`.  This is
the requested chronological content of the TV seam.  It uses the old atom
only as a reach witness; it does not pretend that the replacement preserves
the atom at `ell`.

Let `X_t` and `X_m` be the literal suffixes of `P_i` beginning at `t` and
`m`, let `s` be the conditional joint survival of this word, and let

\[
 \mathcal C_{t,m}
 =\sum_{u=t}^{m-1}
   \Pr_{P_i}(\operatorname{reach}u\mid\operatorname{reach}t)
   \operatorname{RootDefect}(B(X_{u+1}),q_u)        \tag{7.127ay}
\]

be its conditional weighted cap-defect ledger.  The checked finite-word
identity
`quittingTerminalSemanticDebtSum_literalRootStack_eq_weightedLedger_add`
gives

\[
 D(X_t)=\mathcal C_{t,m}+sD(X_m),\qquad s\le1-\theta.\tag{7.127az}
\]

Since both suffixes are actual carrier points and the global minimum debt is
`D_*>0`, the raw-block calculation gives the exhaustive alternative

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal C_{t,m}\ge {\theta D_*\over2}
 \quad\hbox{or}\quad
 D(X_m)\ge D_*+{\theta D_*\over2(1-\theta)}.}       \tag{7.127ba}
\]

For `theta=1`, the first arm holds in the stronger form
`mathcal C_(t,m)>=D_*`; the second arm is omitted.  In particular the second
arm of (7.127ba) crosses the simpler fixed collar
`D(X_m)>=D_*+theta*D_*/2`.  In the first arm, multiplying by the entry reach
in (7.127aw) shows that the original actual profile carries at least

\[
 {\alpha\theta D_*\over2}                           \tag{7.127bb}
\]

of reached cap-defect charge between the first disagreement and the old
mark.  This is a checked cap-defect currency, not an admissible exact-root
path charge.

The existing option-budget identity makes the residual precise.  If rewards
are bounded by `M`, (7.127ba)'s first arm implies, conditionally from `t`,

\[
 \boxed{
 \Pr(\hbox{singleton absorption in }[t,m))
       \ge {\theta D_*\over8M}
 \quad\hbox{or}\quad
 \mathcal E_{t,m}\ge {\theta D_*\over4},}           \tag{7.127bc}
\]

where `mathcal E_(t,m)` is the reached sum of literal prescribed-payoff
endpoint defects.  Every summand of the latter is the actual gain of one
reached best-endpoint update.  Its Continue-oriented portion integrates,
after Fin4 pigeonhole, into one legal through-block behavioral replacement;
the Quit-oriented portion may diffuse over the calendar because an earlier
Quit screens every later update.  All unconditional masses and gains in
(7.127bc) inherit the factor `alpha` from (7.127aw).

The interval in (7.127ax) is *through* the old marked row.  It cannot in
general be shortened to `[t,ell)`, because the entire censored-TV discrepancy
may be a transfer between Quit at `ell` and survival past `ell`.  There is an
exact strict-premark/atom-factor refinement.  Put

\[
 q_x=\mu_x([0,\ell))\qquad(x=0,1).
\]

If `q_0+q_1>=delta_i/2`, one of the source or one-coordinate replacement
profiles has strict-premark stopping mass at least `delta_i/4`, hence its
actual profile has at least that much strict-premark absorption (with entry
at date zero).  Otherwise

\[
 \sum_{u<\ell}|\mu_1(u)-\mu_0(u)|<\delta_i/2.
\]

The defining TV equality then forces both remaining coordinates together to
contribute more than `3*delta_i/2`.  Their signed sum has magnitude less than
`delta_i/2`, so each remaining coordinate difference has magnitude greater
than `delta_i/2`.  In particular the relevant marked-event factor differs by
more than `delta_i/2`: this factor is `mu_i(ell)` when `i in K`, and survival
past `ell` when `i notin K`.  If `beta` is the
`K`-stage mass after the one-coordinate replacement, the unchanged other
three factors and the source floor give

\[
 |\beta-\alpha|>{\alpha\delta_i\over2}.              \tag{7.127bd}
\]

Thus failure of strict-premark absorption forces a fixed movement of the
named atom itself.  Its sign is not determined: a replacement can increase
the atom just as well as decrease it.  If it decreases, (7.127bd) is literal
atom expenditure; if it increases, the replacement retains a stronger atom.
Even a fixed decrease is not by itself a renewable finite rank, because a
later replacement of the same coordinate can reactivate the atom.  The
positive-minimum dispatch (7.127ba) is deliberately sign-free and therefore
does not rely on this false rank.

This result is the finite-word counterpart of the near-minimum linear seam
certificate in
`CODEX_SPINOZA__NEAR_MINIMUM_MOVING_ROW_LINEAR_SEAM_CERTIFICATE.md`.
The present argument prices the physical premark absorption by cap defect or
an exit-debt collar.  If one additionally tries to replace its arbitrary
roots by support-Nash rows while staying source-close, that certificate
prices the attempted repair by a linear payoff seam or support error.  The
two accounts are compatible, but neither turns the diffuse Quit-oriented
occupation in (7.127bc) into an exact Nash--Bellman return.

#### 7.18.10.2 The stopping-law fork contracts only to the paid-port waist

The checked scratch chain

```text
FableStoppingSelection
  -> FableDebtActualReach
  -> FableProfitableFork
  -> FableCommonPrefixFork
```

does not integrate the diffuse Quit-oriented part of (7.127bc).  Its premise
is one **complete-profile debt**

\[
 \Delta\le d_p(X),                                  \tag{7.127be}
\]

and its conclusion is one horizontal complete stopping-law replacement with
gain at least `Delta^2/(16*M)`, plus a reached first-disagreement cut and
literal live-spine equality with the canonical stopping-law realization of
`X_p` strictly before that cut.  By contrast, write

\[
 g_{n,u,p}=L_{n,u}E^Q_{n,u,p}                       \tag{7.127bf}
\]

for the actual one-date gains in a fixed player's Quit-oriented occupation.
Every individual update is a legal behavioral response to the same profile,
so

\[
 g_{n,u,p}\le d_p(X_n),                             \tag{7.127bg}
\]

but there is no reverse inequality with the sum over `u`.  Earlier Quit
updates screen later ones.  Therefore a fixed lower bound on
`sum_u g_(n,u,p)` does not instantiate (7.127be) at that scale.

This gives an exact sequence-level contraction.  After Fin4 stabilizes the
occupation player `p`, pass to one of the following two arms.

1. If `limsup d_p(X_n)>0`, fix `Delta>0` below that limsup.  The common-prefix
   theorem supplies a literal unilateral edge `X_n -> Y_n` with fixed gain

   \[
    U_p(Y_n)-U_p(X_n)
       \ge {\Delta^2\over16M}=:g_0>0,               \tag{7.127bh}
   \]

   together with its own reached common cut.  If `X_n` is near the positive
   global minimum, own-cap invariance and global minimality give

   \[
    \sum_{r\ne p}(d_r(Y_n)-d_r(X_n))
       \ge g_0-(D(X_n)-D_*).                        \tag{7.127bi}
   \]

   Thus inside the `g_0/4` minimum collar, one target spectator has debt at
   least `g_0/4`; outside that collar the source is already a fixed
   off-minimum point.  In either case the checked actual-reach selector and
   cap lift return exactly the maintained off-minimum/target paid-port waist.
   This is a useful contraction, but not a new consumer of that waist.

2. If `d_p(X_n)->0`, then (7.127bg) forces every one-date gain to vanish even
   though their sum may stay fixed.  Applying the scratch chain with
   `Delta_n<=d_p(X_n)` gives only a fork lower bound of order
   `Delta_n^2/M -> 0`.  Applying it instead to another player whose debt is
   kept positive by `D_*>0` produces an unrelated paid row and forgets the
   Quit occupation.  Hence the surviving object is exactly the normalized
   diffuse occupation, not a hidden fixed-scale fork.

The source-prefix bookkeeping in the first arm is exact and should not be
suppressed.  In the application from (7.127ba), `X_n` in (7.127be) is the
literal suffix `X_t` at the earliest premark disagreement.  Let `W^-` be the
actual root word strictly before `t`, let

\[
 \bar X=W^-\star X_t,\qquad \bar Y=W^-\star Y_t,
 \qquad R_t=\Pr_{\bar X}(\hbox{reach }t).             \tag{7.127bi-a}
\]

The endpoint selected in Section 7.18.10.1 agrees with the original source
on its live spine strictly before `t`, so this is the original source prefix,
not an independently chosen word.  The arbitrary-word cancellation theorem
in `FableForkLift` gives, for every coordinate `r`,

\[
 U_r(\bar Y)-U_r(\bar X)
   =R_t\,[U_r(Y_t)-U_r(X_t)].                         \tag{7.127bi-b}
\]

In particular `R_t>=alpha`, so the full-profile mover gain is

\[
 G:=U_p(\bar Y)-U_p(\bar X)
   \ge \alpha {\Delta^2\over16M}.                    \tag{7.127bi-c}
\]

The lift is literally unilateral and retains the original word at every date
before `t`.  The common-prefix fork inside `X_t` further preserves the
canonical source live-spine actions up to its own selected cut.  These are
live-spine/source-ancestry statements; they do not assert equality of
arbitrary off-path behavioral histories.  If `W^-` is additionally a
compatible punishment-floor certificate, the checked compatible-word fork
lift also retains the debt sandwich and the punishment floor.  Without that
extra field, only the exact payoff scaling and unilateral/source-prefix
identities are claimed.  Thus (7.127bi) applies at the full source with `G`
in place of `g_0`; inside the `G/4` minimum collar it produces a target
spectator debt at least `G/4`.  Positive `D_*` and the retained marked atom
are therefore preserved through the prefix lift, but exact target-root
typing is not.

The through-mark cap-defect block cannot be prepended to the fork as an
admissible chronological macro.  In the charged arm of (7.127ba), positivity
of `mathcal C_(t,m)` means that some actually reached root of the literal
block has positive cap Nash defect against its literal successor cap.  But
every edge of `quittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation` requires an
exact zero-defect root.  The raw block therefore is not an `R.Path`.  The
fork's common-prefix field says only that its two **behavioral profiles**
agree before its cut; it supplies none of the `value`, `policy`, `exactNash`,
or punishment-floor fields of `QuittingPunishmentFloorFinitePrefix`.

Even an independently supplied exact cap--Nash source word, constructed
against the source suffix caps, does not repair the type.
Let `b_u` be the source suffix cap seen by an old exact root `q_u`, let
`b'_u` be the cap after the forked suffix is substituted, and put

\[
 \Delta_{u,r}=b'_{u,r}-b_{u,r},\qquad
 h_{u,r}=Q_{u,r}-C_{u,r}(b_u).                      \tag{7.127bj}
\]

The checked `FableCapSeam` formula gives the target-side coordinate defect
of the retained root exactly:

\[
 \boxed{
 \operatorname{Def}_{u,r}(b'_u,q_u)
 =c_{u,r}(h_{u,r}-H_{u,r}\Delta_{u,r})_+
  +q_{u,r}(H_{u,r}\Delta_{u,r}-h_{u,r})_+.}         \tag{7.127bk}
\]

Here `c_(u,r)` and `q_(u,r)` are the old Continue and Quit probabilities,
and `H_(u,r)` is opponent Continue mass.  Formula (7.127bk) is the exact
source-reprojection seam, not a TV bound.  The old word remains exact after
the fork if and only if every displayed term vanishes.  At a mixed old
coordinate, `h_(u,r)=0`, so a cap rise costs
`q_(u,r) H_(u,r) (Delta_(u,r))_+` and a cap fall costs
`c_(u,r) H_(u,r) (-Delta_(u,r))_+`.

The common-prefix fork controls neither sign nor slack in (7.127bk).  If one
term is positive, the target vertical word is not admissible.  If every term
vanishes, the same word gives two parallel admissible vertical paths, but the
horizontal behavioral fork is still not an admissible Bellman edge: its
payoff gain is a whole-strategy comparison, not a root successor identity.
Thus the square has the exact form

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{array}{c}
 \text{positive reprojection seam: target exact path is lost},\\
 \text{zero reprojection seam: two exact paths, but no admissible connector}.
 \end{array}}                                      \tag{7.127bl}
\]

This proves cleanly that the Fable chain returns only the same paid-port
waist.  Reapplying it extracts another horizontal row and does not change
(7.127bl).  A genuine advance must instead control or consume the signed
source-reprojection seam, or supply a separate exact Bellman connector.

#### 7.18.10.3 The cap-lifted reprojection seam is summable

Although the common-prefix fork does not itself give an admissible connector,
its cap-reprojection defect cannot remain a nonsummable obstruction when the
same forked suffix is replayed under the positive-minimum cap-lift ray.

Let `X_0` be any actual profile, let `Y_0` differ from it only in one complete
strategy, and let

\[
 X_{n+1}=q_n\star X_n
\]

be the cap-lift sequence: `q_n` is exact Nash against
`b_n=B(X_n)`.  Replay the **same** roots over the forked suffix,

\[
 Y_{n+1}=q_n\star Y_n,qquad b'_n=B(Y_n).             \tag{7.127bm}
\]

Put

\[
 c_n=\Pr_{q_n}(\mathbf C),\quad
 S_0=1,\quad S_{n+1}=c_nS_n,
\]

\[
 \Delta_{n,r}=b'_{n,r}-b_{n,r},\qquad
 e_{n,r}=\operatorname{Def}_r(b'_n,q_n).            \tag{7.127bn}
\]

The cap of a literal root-prefix profile is the maximum of the two pure
endpoints.  That maximum is its prescribed root-successor payoff plus the
coordinate Nash defect.  Since `q_n` is exact at `b_n`, while root-successor
payoff differences scale by the joint Continue mass, one gets the exact
recurrence

\[
 \boxed{\Delta_{n+1,r}=c_n\Delta_{n,r}+e_{n,r}.}    \tag{7.127bo}
\]

This is the global form of the one-row signed seam (7.127bk).  It uses the
actual target caps `B(Y_n)` at every replayed depth; no semantic surrogate or
TV estimate is inserted.

Now assume the same positive global minimum used by the cap lift, and write

\[
 \sigma={D_*\over D(X_0)}>0.
\]

Exact source debt scaling and global minimality give `S_n>=sigma`, so every
`c_n` is positive.  Dividing (7.127bo) by `S_(n+1)` yields

\[
 \boxed{
 {\Delta_{n+1,r}\over S_{n+1}}
 ={\Delta_{n,r}\over S_n}+{e_{n,r}\over S_{n+1}}.} \tag{7.127bp}
\]

Thus every normalized cap displacement is nondecreasing.  Both caps lie in
the reward box, so `|Delta_(n,r)|<=2M`; also `sigma<=S_n<=1`.  Telescoping
(7.127bp) gives the uniform estimate

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{n=0}^\infty {e_{n,r}\over S_{n+1}}
 \le {4M\over\sigma},\qquad
 \sum_{n=0}^\infty e_{n,r}\le {4M\over\sigma}.}     \tag{7.127bq}
\]

For Fin4 the total replay defect `e_n=sum_r e_(n,r)` therefore satisfies

\[
 \sum_ne_n\le {16M\over\sigma},
 \qquad e_n\longrightarrow0.                       \tag{7.127br}
\]

In particular `q_n` is an `e_n`-Nash root against the actual forked cap
`b'_n`, and

\[
 b'_{n+1}=F(q_n,b'_n)+(e_{n,r})_r.                  \tag{7.127bs}
\]

The prescribed-payoff comparison has no maximum operation and hence no seam:
if `Gamma_(n,r)=U_r(Y_n)-U_r(X_n)`, then

\[
 \Gamma_{n,r}=S_n\Gamma_{0,r}.                     \tag{7.127bt}
\]

Consequently the complete normalized semantic displacement is explicit:

\[
 {\Delta_{N,r}\over S_N}
 =\Delta_{0,r}+\sum_{n<N}{e_{n,r}\over S_{n+1}},
 \qquad
 {\Gamma_{N,r}\over S_N}=\Gamma_{0,r}.             \tag{7.127bu}
\]

If `X_0 -> Y_0` is a unilateral fork by `p`, then
`Delta_(0,p)=0`.  Induction in (7.127bo), or own-coordinate cap invariance at
each common root, gives

\[
 \Delta_{n,p}=e_{n,p}=0\quad(n\ge0).                \tag{7.127bv}
\]

Thus a source gain `g>0` remains a literal horizontal gain
`S_n*g>=sigma*g` at every depth, while the target vertical roots become
asymptotically exact and their total cap-reprojection error is summable.

This rules out the adverse possibility proposed at the end of Section 10:
for one fixed forked suffix, the cap-lifted source-reprojection seam is always
summable.  It still does not produce an `R.Path`.  Each target root with
`e_n>0` is nonexact, and when `e_n=0` the horizontal fork still has no Bellman
edge type.  Moreover the source absorption tail is itself summable, so taking
late blocks makes both the target Nash error and the available exact root
charge small.  The remaining missing datum is now sharply isolated as an
**exact horizontal connector** (or a consumer of a persistent paid horizontal
edge beside an exact source track and an asymptotically exact replay track), not a
nonsummable cap seam.

Suggested Lean-sized declarations are

```text
capLiftReplay_capDisplacement_succ
capLiftReplay_normalizedCapDisplacement_mono
capLiftReplay_summable_totalNashDefect
capLiftReplay_unilateralOwnerCap_eq
```

#### 7.18.10.4 Reversed replay blocks and the exact consumer boundary

The recurrence above can be matched more tightly to the checked
summable-residual and projective consumers.  Put

\[
 E_n=\sum_r e_{n,r}.
\]

Since `e_(n,r)` is exactly the coordinate Nash defect of `q_n` against
`b'_n`, (7.127br)--(7.127bs) show that

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 |b'_{n+1,r}-F_r(q_n,b'_n)|&=e_{n,r}\le E_n,\\
 q_n&\text{ is }E_n\text{-Nash against }b'_n,\\
 \sum_nE_n&\le {16M\over\sigma}.
 \end{aligned}                                      \tag{7.127bv-a}
\]

Thus `(q_n,b'_n)` is a literal **forward** approximate Nash--Bellman chain,
with both scalar Bellman and Nash errors bounded by `E_n`.  It is important
not to call this a `QuittingSummableResidualNashBellmanSpine`: that structure
requires

\[
 v_n\simeq F(q_n,v_{n+1}),
 \qquad q_n\text{ Nash against }v_{n+1},             \tag{7.127bv-a'}
\]

whereas (7.127bv-a) says

\[
 b'_{n+1}\simeq F(q_n,b'_n),
 \qquad q_n\text{ Nash against }b'_n.
\]

There is no order-reversing cofinal map of `Nat`.  Only finite intervals can
be reversed into the chronological orientation.

Here is the finite-block orientation.  Fix `s` and `H>0`.  For a phase `z` of
the length-`H` reversed block, put

\[
 k=s+z^{\rm rev},\qquad
 \widehat q_z=q_k,\qquad \widehat v_z=b'_{k+1}.       \tag{7.127bv-b}
\]

At every nonclosing phase the chronological continuation is exactly `b'_k`.
Consequently the Bellman and root-Nash residuals are, separately,

\[
 \widehat v_{z,r}-F_r(\widehat q_z,\widehat v_{z+1})
   =e_{k,r},
 \qquad
 \widehat q_z\text{ is }E_k\text{-Nash at }\widehat v_{z+1}.
                                                               \tag{7.127bv-c}
\]

At the closing phase, whose underlying forward index is `s`, the wrapped
continuation is `b'_(s+H)`.  If

\[
 \delta_{s,H}=\|b'_{s+H}-b'_s\|_\infty,
\]

then the exact closing Bellman residual is

\[
 e_{s,r}+c_s\bigl(b'_{s,r}-b'_{s+H,r}\bigr),         \tag{7.127bv-d}
\]

and continuation stability makes the closing root
`(E_s+2*delta_(s,H))`-Nash.  Reversal changes neither the roots nor the block
absorption:

\[
 A_{s,H}=1-\prod_{k=s}^{s+H-1}c_k.                  \tag{7.127bv-e}
\]

The source hazards are summable by the positive-minimum budget.  Moreover
the root update gives

\[
 \|b'_{n+1}-b'_n\|_\infty\le 2Ma_n+E_n.
\]

Hence `b'_n` converges and both the internal-error tail and
`delta_(s,H)` tend to zero uniformly in `H` as `s` tends to infinity.
This supplies absolutely small late reversed blocks.  It does **not** supply
the projective estimate

\[
 \sum_{k=s}^{s+H-1}E_k+\delta_{s,H}
       =o(A_{s,H}).                                  \tag{7.127bv-f}
\]

Both numerator and denominator can vanish at the same rate.  Ordinary
summability permits, for example, `E_k=kappa*a_k` with fixed `kappa>0`, for
which no interval improves the relative ratio.  Thus the
`ForwardBlockSingleSeam` lasso cannot be instantiated from (7.127bv-a) alone.

There is an even sharper failure at the infinite residual-spine interface.
Positive minimum gives

\[
 \sum_na_n<\infty,
 \qquad \sum_n q_{n,r}(\mathrm{Quit})<\infty
       \quad\hbox{for every }r,
 \qquad \inf_n S_n\ge\sigma>0.                      \tag{7.127bv-g}
\]

One can choose disjoint late forward intervals, reverse each interval, and
concatenate the resulting chronological blocks.  Since `b'_n` converges, the
inter-block endpoint seams can be chosen summable; continuation stability
then makes their additional Nash errors summable too.  This does produce a
genuine `QuittingSummableResidualNashBellmanSpine`, but each original root is
used at most once.  Therefore it has **no** persistent marginal label,
whereas
`QuittingSummableResidualNashBellmanSpine.exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_twoPersistent`
requires two.  Equivalently, the `QuittingSummableSeamSource` decoder demands
joint and deleted-player survival tending to zero on every suffix, while the
joint survival here has a positive limit.  Late blocks make the residual
ledger arbitrarily small but never repair this failed survival field.

To create persistent charge one must repeat returned reversed blocks or
otherwise reuse hazards.  Repetition also repeats their internal and closing
errors.  The checked consumer can tolerate that only after a charge-relative
selection such as (7.127bv-f); ordinary summability gives no such selection.
For the support-projective lasso there is one further caution: full root Nash
error `E_k` is probability weighted and does not by itself bound the
support-local endpoint error of a very small played action.  Thus even
(7.127bv-f) must be stated for the consumer's actual Bellman and support
residuals, not merely for total Nash defect.

The persistent horizontal gain in (7.127bt) is invisible to both checked
interfaces.  It is a comparison between two tracks, not an endpoint return
inside the `b'_n` track, an absorption charge, or a persistent marginal Quit
label.  In particular it cannot replace (7.127bv-f) or (7.127bv-g).

The common-prefix fork nevertheless gives a precise first-disagreement
factorization compatible with every replay.  Suppose the original source
and target first differ on the live spine at relative date `t` in mover `p`,
with own Quit probabilities `x,y`, common opponent conditional root, pure
Quit value `Q`, opponent-absorption Continue contribution `A`, opponent
Continue mass `H_p`, and post-Continue mover values `V_X,V_Y`.  Then

\[
 U_p(Y_0)-U_p(X_0)
  =(y-x)\bigl[Q-(A+H_pV_X)\bigr]
     +(1-y)H_p(V_Y-V_X),                             \tag{7.127bv-h}
\]

multiplied by the common reach of the cut.  After replay depth `n`, the same
cut occurs at absolute date `n+t`; the outer common word multiplies the whole
identity by `S_n`, so its gain remains at least `sigma*g`, and the entire
original source prefix is retained.

The first summand in (7.127bv-h) is a one-root endpoint comparison at a fixed
tail.  The second is a different-continuation term.  Fable's law transport
does not make that term vanish: it redirects complete stopping-time mass, so
the conditional mover laws after the first cut can still differ.  This is
