the single missing typed datum.  To close a replay block one needs either an
exact floor-admissible cross-track Nash--Bellman edge, or quantitatively the
weaker projective cross seam of size `o(A_(s,H))`.  The fork supplies neither;
it supplies (7.127bv-h), a profitable behavioral comparison with two
different continuations.

The positive paid-Never cylinder from finite-deadline compactification is a
sharp concrete test of this boundary.  Fix the deadline-`n` Nash source and
payer `i`.  For every `L>=n+2`, let `rho^L` replace `i` by pure Quit at `L`.
The checked cylinder calculation gives

\[
 \operatorname{Sem}(\rho^L)=z^+\quad\hbox{for every finite }L,
 \qquad U_i(\rho^L)-U_i(\rho^\infty)\ge D_*/4,       \tag{7.127bv-i}
\]

while `rho^L` converges weakly to the `Never` profile `rho^infinity` and the
limit loses exactly the positive opponent-Never cylinder.  Thus even equality
of the full finite terminal semantic pairs does not provide chronological
composition: the `rho^L` are alternative siblings, not successive edges.

At the first disagreement date `L`, pure Quit versus `Never` does remove the
second term in (7.127bv-h).  It is a pure Quit-versus-Continue comparison
against the common `Never` tail, and the positive opponent-Never cylinder
makes that endpoint gap genuinely paid.  But the source all-Continue
marginal at `L` then has exactly this positive Nash defect.  It is therefore
a horizontal one-root deviation, not an exact Nash root.  The pure-Quit
target root need not be Nash for the other players either.  Replacing it by
an exact root against the common cap is precisely the cap lift already
replayed above and again loses the source-attached horizontal edge.  After
any common replay prefix, (7.127bv-i) is simply shifted outward and scaled by
the common survival; the noncomposable-sibling diagram is unchanged.  Never
mass is a complete-law tail event, not a finite-stage Bellman state or a
source-reentry identity.

Suggested Lean-sized declarations for the exact positive part are

```text
capLiftReplay_forwardBellmanResidual
capLiftReplay_reversedBlock_internalBellmanResidual
capLiftReplay_reversedBlock_closingBellmanResidual
capLiftReplay_disjointReversedBlocks_toSummableResidualSpine
capLiftReplay_allMarginalQuitHazards_summable
capLiftReplay_not_hasTwoPersistentQuittingMarginals
fableForkReplay_firstDisagreement_gain_factorization
```

#### 7.18.10.5 The connector split: charged non-Nash root or debt transfer

Formula (7.127bv-h) has one useful quantitative consequence, but it does not
make the horizontal fork admissible.  Include the common reach `R>0` of the
original first-disagreement cut and write

\[
 G=R(T+C)>0,
 \quad T=(y-x)[Q-(A+H_pV_X)],
 \quad C=(1-y)H_p(V_Y-V_X).                          \tag{7.127bv-j}
\]

All endpoint and continuation values lie in `[-M,M]`.  There is the following
exhaustive, sign-sensitive split.

**Root arm.**  If `R*C<G/2`, then

\[
 RT>{G\over2},qquad
 R|y-x|\ge {G\over4M}.                              \tag{7.127bv-k}
\]

At least one of the source and target roots therefore has unconditional
mover-Quit mass at this reached cut at least `G/(4M)`.  This is genuine
physical absorption, with the original source prefix.  It is not an
admissible Nash--Bellman charge.  Indeed the target marginal `y` is an
available one-root deviation from the source marginal `x` at the fixed tail
`V_X`, and its conditional gain is `T>0`.  Hence the source root's mover
coordinate Nash defect is at least `T`; the very inequality which produces
the charge proves that the source root is non-Nash.  Purifying to the better
endpoint repairs only the mover coordinate and says nothing about the other
three root coordinates.

**Connector arm.**  If `R*C>=G/2`, then `C>0` and

\[
 h:=V_Y-V_X\ge {G\over2}.                            \tag{7.127bv-l}
\]

The two conditional suffix profiles still differ only in mover `p`, so own
cap invariance gives

\[
 d_p(Y^{\rm tail})=d_p(X^{\rm tail})-h.              \tag{7.127bv-m}
\]

Let `D_* > 0` be the global minimum over actual terminal semantic pairs.  If

\[
 D(X^{\rm tail})-D_*\ge h/2,                         \tag{7.127bv-n}
\]

the source suffix is already outside the `h/2` minimum collar.  Otherwise
global minimality at the target and (7.127bv-m) imply

\[
 \sum_{r\ne p}
   \bigl(d_r(Y^{\rm tail})-d_r(X^{\rm tail})\bigr)
   \ge h/2.                                         \tag{7.127bv-o}
\]

For Fin4 some spectator consequently has target debt at least
`h/6>=G/12`.  Thus the large connector is not a neutral untyped seam: it
forces either a definite source-attached off-minimum suffix or a definite
spectator-debt target.  The checked debt-to-actual-reach/Fable fork machinery
then returns this arm to the maintained off-minimum/paid-port waist.

The split is invariant under replay.  At depth `n`, the entire identity and
the cut reach are multiplied by `S_n`, while the relative cut is shifted to
absolute date `n+t`.  Since `S_n>=sigma`, the root arm carries actual
absorption at least

\[
 {\sigma G\over4M},                                  \tag{7.127bv-p}
\]

at the shifted non-Nash row.  The connector arm retains the same conditional
suffix gain `h`, off-minimum collar, and spectator-debt conclusion behind the
longer original source prefix.

This is the strongest honest positive-minimum consequence of the connector
split.  It does not give the requested consumed chronology:

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{array}{c}
 \text{small connector}\Rightarrow
   \text{fixed actual charge at a provably non-Nash root};\\
 \text{large positive connector}\Rightarrow
   \text{off-minimum suffix or a paid spectator-debt port}.
 \end{array}}                                       \tag{7.127bv-q}
\]

Exact cap-Nashification of the first arm changes the root/source chart and
returns to the cap-lift replay.  Applying the debt selector to the second arm
returns to the Fable paid-port waist.  Neither operation supplies the missing
cross-track Bellman connector.

The finite paid-Never plateau makes the split completely concrete.  For the
anchor's own `Q_L`-versus-`Never` edge, `C=0`, so it is in the root arm and
the boundary Continue root has the macroscopic Nash defect.  At a finite
endpoint `rho^L`, any cap-attaining response of a new debtor `p!=i` can be
chosen at one of `0,...,n,n+1,L,infinity`.  A response before `L` is literal
deadline descent; a response at `L` adds a second anchor/collision; and
`infinity` erases or retains the sole anchor.  No response increases `L`.
This finite-anchor classification prevents an unseen later connector inside
one fixed endpoint, but it does not make the alternatives composable across
different `L` or prevent a later regenerated source from choosing a larger
anchor.  It is therefore a finite local rank, not a renewable global rank.

Suggested Lean-sized declarations are

```text
fableFirstDisagreement_rootOrConnector
fableFirstDisagreement_rootArm_actualAbsorption_ge
fableFirstDisagreement_rootArm_nashDefect_pos
fableFirstDisagreement_connectorArm_offMinimum_or_spectatorDebt_finFour
fableForkReplay_rootArm_actualAbsorption_ge
```

#### 7.18.10.6 Recursive exactification has an exact reset account

There is no further gain from iterating the two arms unless the regenerated
sources share a new global account.  The exact obstruction can be written in
one line.  Suppose, now genuinely linked as actual profile transformations
(not yet asserted to be one executable play), that step `m` consists of

\[
 X_m\xrightarrow[\text{one player }p_m]{\text{horizontal fork }g_m}Y_m,
 \qquad
 X_{m+1}=q_m\star Y_m,                               \tag{7.127bv-r}
\]

where `q_m` is exact Nash against `B(Y_m)`.  Put

\[
 R_m=\sum_{r\ne p_m}\bigl(d_r(Y_m)-d_r(X_m)\bigr),
 \qquad a_m=\Pr_{q_m}(\text{some Quit}).
\]

Own-cap invariance and exact cap-Nash debt scaling give

\[
 D(Y_m)-D(X_m)=R_m-g_m,
 \qquad D(X_{m+1})=(1-a_m)D(Y_m).                    \tag{7.127bv-s}
\]

Therefore every finite linked iteration satisfies the exact reset balance

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{m<N}\bigl(g_m+a_mD(Y_m)\bigr)
   =D(X_0)-D(X_N)+\sum_{m<N}R_m.}                   \tag{7.127bv-t}
\]

Positive minimum controls each exactification separately:

\[
 a_mD(Y_m)\le D(Y_m)-D_* .                          \tag{7.127bv-u}
\]

Thus a cap-Nash root of fixed absorption necessarily starts from a fixed
off-minimum excess; on a near-minimum target its exact absorption must vanish.
In particular Nashifying the fixed physical charge in the root arm need not
retain any comparable exact charge.  It can only spend the excess already
present at the new target.

The right side of (7.127bv-t) explains why iteration does not create a
bounded monotone account.  At a near-minimum fork, (7.10) says
`R_m>=g_m-(D(X_m)-D_*)`: the spectator term can replace almost the entire
mover debt just removed.  Equality permits the four-coordinate rotating-debt
ledger already recorded after (7.16), with `R_m=g_m`, zero total-debt change,
and zero cap-root charge.  When a fork instead raises total debt, that increase
can finance later exact absorption through (7.127bv-u), but the fork itself
is still a non-Nash horizontal seam.  Hence even divergent
`sum_m a_m` would be a collection of exact vertical pieces separated by
untyped resets, not one path in the admissible charged relation.

The four natural proposed accounts all fail at this exact equality:

1. mover payoff is not global, because `p_m` can rotate and each response has
   uncontrolled externalities in the other payoff coordinates;
2. total-debt excess is replenished by `R_m-g_m` and need not decrease;
3. the finite paid-Never anchor rank decreases only inside one fixed
   `rho^L`, while regeneration may choose a new, larger `L`; and
4. exact hazard capacity is spent only inside the cap segments and is not
   additive across the intervening horizontal forks.

This is not merely a lack of a chosen potential.  Equation (7.127bv-t) is
the complete scalar balance supplied by own-cap invariance, positive minimum,
and exact cap scaling.  Its sole uncontrolled term is exactly the directed
spectator transfer already isolated in (7.13)--(7.16).  Static atom, killed
label, and source-prefix passports do not sign that term, and later responses
can reactivate all of them.  Consequently recursive exactification rigorously
regenerates the same paid-port/source-reentry waist.  Closing this lane now
requires a genuinely new theorem typing or charging the reset `Y_m -> X_(m+1)`
and the next horizontal source re-entry together; another local fork split
cannot do it.

Suggested Lean-sized declarations are

```text
paidForkThenCapNash_debt_reset_identity
paidForkThenCapNash_absorption_mul_debt_le_excess
paidForkThenCapNash_sum_reset_balance
```

#### 7.18.11 Verdict and formalization boundary

The coherent selection yields the following exact alternative:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{c}
\liminf_n A_n=0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
  \text{terminal approximate Nash profiles and a UE payoff};\\[2mm]
\sum_nA_n<\infty\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
  \text{an exact terminal Nash profile};\\[2mm]
\text{otherwise every consecutive-time paid-row attempt incurs either}\\
\text{a nonsummable negative source-reprojection seam or positive-density}\\
\text{observer switches, with censor and response errors given by
 (7.115)--(7.121).}
\end{array}}
\tag{7.130}
\]

At every large-curl square, the two-coordinate prefix selector adds the
orthogonal exhaustive split (7.127ai).  Its common-prefix arm reaches the
checked post-mark paid-splice consumer.  Its premark arm now reaches
(7.127ba): a fixed literal exit-tail collar crossing or a fixed reached
cap-defect block beginning at the earliest clock disagreement, with the
singleton/literal-work refinement (7.127bc).  Thus the remaining unpaid
object is no longer prefix TV; it is specifically the diffuse Quit-oriented
literal-defect occupation, or the usual paid-port waist after the
Continue-clearing/recipient dispatch.

For a fixed profitable fork extracted from that occupation, the target replay
now has the stronger exact description (7.127bv-a): it is a forward
summable-residual Nash--Bellman chain with the original source prefix and a
uniformly surviving horizontal payoff gap.  Finite intervals reverse into
chronological blocks, but the whole `Nat`-chain does not.  Disjoint reversed
blocks can be joined with summable seams; the resulting spine lies precisely
in the positive-survival summable-hazard port, where no two marginal clocks
persist.  Reusing blocks needs a charge-relative residual estimate, and using
the paid gap needs a cross-track Bellman/projective closing seam.  Formula
(7.127bv-h) identifies the latter as a different-continuation term after first
disagreement.

This does not produce a source-reprojected charged return: the negative seams
are opponent externalities, not gains of their movers, and observer switches
compare different payoff coordinates.  A new theorem would have to turn one
of those exact seam terms into an actual same-player update without losing
the `new_n=old_(n+1)` connector.  Merely stabilizing labels on a subsequence or
counting profile siblings as chronology is invalid.

Suggested ordinary-to-Lean handoff:

```text
pmfTV_include_succ_eq_censored_add_clippedBoundary
consecutiveFiniteTimingNash_boundaryTime_sub_previousTime_ge
coherentAdjacentPaidRows_negativeReprojectionBudget
passiveCensorTriangle
responseReprojection_curl
stoppingLaw_censoredTV_exists_sourcePrefix_absorptionBlock
premarkTVSeam_capDefectBlock_or_exitDebtCollar
```

The first two identities use only the checked finite timing definitions and
Nash inequalities.  The next three are finite telescopes.  The final two use
finite PMF algebra and the checked weighted cap-defect ledger.  No new
equilibrium or charged-relation structure should contain the desired seam
orientation as an input field.

## 7.19 Finite-clock double debt gives two source-prefix hazard blocks with a common exit

The finite-clock co-source theorem in
`CODEX_SPINOZA__FINITE_TESTER_SEPARATION_AND_TWO_ESCAPE_BOUNDARY.md` supplies
an input which is stronger than two unrelated paid ports.  Let `sigma` be one
actual profile whose four stopping laws are supported on

\[
 \{0,\ldots,H-1\}\cup\{\infty\},
\]

and let distinct players `i,j` have debts at least `Gamma>0`.  Their caps are
attained by pure candidates

\[
 q_i,q_j\in\{0,\ldots,H-1,H,\infty\}.               \tag{7.131}
\]

Put `M=quittingRewardBound reward`.  Positive gain implies `M>0`.  The
following one-debtor construction is the useful temporal decoder; applying it
twice retains the common source.

### Theorem 7.19.1 (finite-clock debt to a source-prefix hazard block)

Let player `p` have debt at least `Gamma` at `sigma`, and let `q_p` be an
exact cap-attaining candidate in (7.131).  There are:

* a finite cut `c_p<=H`;
* a finite-clock profile `P_p` which is either `sigma` itself or differs from
  it only in player `p`;
* literal equality of the live-root sequences of `P_p` and `sigma` at every
  date strictly before `c_p`; and
* a finite block from `c_p` through `H` such that

\[
 \Pr_{P_p}(\operatorname{reach}c_p)
      \ge r_0:={\Gamma^2\over16M^2},                 \tag{7.132}
\]

\[
 \sum_{t=c_p}^{H}\sum_k
   P_p(k\hbox{ Quits at }t\mid\operatorname{reach}t)
      \ge \beta_0:={\Gamma\over4M}.                 \tag{7.133}
\]

After the block, the suffix of `P_p` is the literal all-Never profile.

If `q_p` is finite (an internal candidate or the auxiliary Late date `H`),
then `P_p` may be chosen as a profitable common-prefix fork of `sigma`, with

\[
 U_p(P_p)-U_p(\sigma)\ge {\Gamma^2\over8M}.          \tag{7.134}
\]

If `q_p=\infty`, then `P_p=\sigma`; the separately retained pure-Never update
from `sigma` is the exact cap response and has gain at least `Gamma`.

#### Proof

Let `nu` be player `p`'s stopping law at `sigma`, let `v(q)` be the payoff of
the pure-time replacement `q` against `sigma_-p`, and let `C=v(q_p)` be the
cap.  Then

\[
 C-\mathbb E_\nu v\ge\Gamma,
 \qquad |v(q)|,|C|\le M.                             \tag{7.135}
\]

Call a source choice bad when `C-v(q)>=Gamma/2` and let `beta` be its
`nu`-mass.  On the complement the loss is less than `Gamma/2`, while
everywhere it is at most `2M`.  Therefore

\[
 \Gamma\le 2M\beta+\Gamma/2,
 \qquad
 \boxed{\beta\ge\Gamma/(4M)=\beta_0.}               \tag{7.136}
\]

Choose the earliest bad source choice in the temporal order in which Never
is last, and let `c_p` be the earlier of that choice and `q_p`.  The bad set
has no source mass strictly before `c_p`, and the exact maximizer `q_p` is not
bad.  Redirect all bad mass to `q_p`.  This is precisely the stopping-law
transport used by `positiveDebt_exists_commonPrefix_profitableStoppingLawFork`,
now with an exact rather than `Gamma/4`-approximate receiver.  It leaves every
stopping mass, survival denominator, and reconstructed live action unchanged
strictly before `c_p`.  Its payoff gain is

\[
 \sum_{q\ bad}\nu(q)[C-v(q)]
 \ge (\Gamma/2)\beta
 \ge\Gamma^2/(8M),                                  \tag{7.137}
\]

which proves (7.134).

The selected bad source and the receiver differ in pure-time payoff by at
least `Gamma/2`.  The checked first-disagreement bound
`QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow.gain_le_liveMass` gives opponent survival at
their first cut at least `Gamma/(4M)=beta_0`.  Player `p`'s source survival at
that cut is also at least `beta`: all bad source mass lies at or after the
cut.  Hence source joint survival, and therefore fork joint survival, is at
least `beta_0^2=r_0`.  This proves (7.132).

There are now exactly two candidate-type cases.

1. If `q_p` is finite, the transported law has mass at least `beta` at
   `q_p`; take `P_p` to be the forked profile.
2. If `q_p=Never`, Never is not bad because it attains `C`.  All bad mass is
   therefore finite source mass at dates from `c_p` through `H`; take
   `P_p=sigma`.

In both cases player `p` has finite stopping mass at least `beta` inside the
displayed block.  Conditional on its survival to the cut this probability is
at least `beta`, and the sum of its conditional hazards dominates its
finite-stop probability.  This proves (7.133).  Every finite atom of every
coordinate is at most `H`, so the suffix after row `H` is all-Never. QED.

The distinction between internal finite, Late, and Never is therefore fully
accounted for.  Late is the actual finite time `H`, not a compactification
point.  No cap attainment is inferred in this section: it is exactly the
extra finite-clock datum supplied by the co-source theorem.

### Corollary 7.19.2 (two distinct source-prefix blocks, one all-Never exit)

Apply Theorem 7.19.1 to `i` and `j`.  It produces two profiles `P_i,P_j`,
each equal to the same original `sigma` before its own cut, with the uniform
floors `r_0,beta_0` and with distinct debt/response labels.  Both blocks have
the same exit semantic pair:

\[
 X_\infty=\operatorname{Sem}(\hbox{all Never}).       \tag{7.138}
\]

They are two executable source siblings, not successive chronological
states.  This is nevertheless enough to apply the checked positive-minimum
two-cut coercivity theorem to each sibling without losing source ancestry.

To meet the record's strict post-mark indexing also when `c_p=0`, prepend one
deterministic all-Continue row to `sigma` and `P_p`.  Set

\[
 \mathsf{markedRow}=0,\quad
 \mathsf{entryCut}=c_p+1,\quad
 \mathsf{exitCut}=H+2,                               \tag{7.139}
\]

and use `r_0` and `beta_0` as its reach and hazard floors.  The complete root
sequence before the entry is the same silent shift of the original source
prefix.  Entry and exit pairs are literal suffix semantic pairs, and a
separately retained global minimizer supplies the positive-minimum fields.
Thus this is a literal
`QuittingUniformlyReachedPostMarkTwoCutBlock`, not merely an outcome-law or
TV certificate.

For Fin4 put

\[
 K_0=(1-e^{-\beta_0})D_*,                            \tag{7.140}
\]

where `D_*>0` is the attained minimum total terminal debt.  Applying
`finFour_offMinimum_or_exists_paidSplice` to both blocks yields the exhaustive
common-exit alternative

\[
 \boxed{
 D(X_\infty)\ge D_*+{e^{\beta_0}-1\over2}D_*}        \tag{7.141}
\]

or, for **each** label `p in {i,j}`, a literal parent-level paid splice based
on the corresponding `P_p` track, unchanged before its entry cut, with gain

\[
 \boxed{
 U(\mathsf{splice}_p)-U(\mathsf{parent}_p)
   > {\Gamma^2(1-e^{-\Gamma/(4M)})D_*\over256M^2}.} \tag{7.142}
\]

The payer selected by the checked consumer need not equal `p`, and the two
selected payers need not be distinct.  The distinct labels are the two
co-source debt/fork labels and the two temporal blocks carrying their
provenance.

### Exact contraction and nonclaims

This is a genuine chronological strengthening of a bare paid-port packet:
each debt is converted into an actual uniformly reached finite hazard block,
the two blocks preserve one original source, and their exit is the same
explicit all-Never semantic pair.  It also improves the generic common-prefix
fork gain from `Gamma^2/(16M)` to `Gamma^2/(8M)` because the receiver is an
attained exact cap candidate.

It does not yet consume the maintained paid-port waist.  The roots inside the
hazard block are the actual roots of `P_p`; they are not asserted cap-Nash, so
the block is not a path in the punishment-floor admissible charged relation.
The checked two-cut theorem returns an off-minimum profile or another
source-prefix paid splice.  Moreover `P_i` and `P_j` are siblings: treating
their two blocks as consecutive play would lose the common source.  Thus the
new co-source theorem closes all finite/Late/Never and cap-attainment typing,
but the only missing terminal step remains a consumer of either the common
all-Never off-minimum port (7.141) or the pair of source-prefix horizontal
splices (7.142).

Suggested Lean-sized declarations are

finiteClockDebt_exactCap_commonPrefixFork_gain_ge
finiteClockDebt_exists_sourcePrefixHazardBlock
finiteClockDoubleDebt_exists_twoSourcePrefixHazardBlocks_commonNeverExit

## 7.20 Two-fork interpolation has quadratic terminal curvature but can have order-one root defect

The remaining common-cut/reached-two-cut residue suggests independently
mixing a small fraction of each fork into the common source.  This operation
is actual and preserves the common past, but its terminal and rowwise error
scales are different.

Let `sigma` be any actual profile, let `i != j`, and let `f_i,f_j` be two
complete behavioral replacements.  For `a,b in {0,1}`, write `sigma_ab` for
the four literal corners, where `1` installs the indicated full response and
`0` retains the source coordinate.  For every observer `k`, put

\[
 C_k=U_k(\sigma_{11})-U_k(\sigma_{10})
      -U_k(\sigma_{01})+U_k(\sigma_{00}).             \tag{7.143}
\]

For `0<=lambda<=1`, let `sigma_lambda` independently replace the `i`- and
`j`-laws by their complete stopping-law mixtures with weights `lambda` on
`f_i,f_j`.  This is realized by
`quittingStoppingLawMixtureBehaviorStrategy`; it is not a public correlated
coin.  Applying the checked one-coordinate payoff-affinity theorem twice
gives the exact bilinear identity

\[
 U_k(\sigma_\lambda)
 =U_k(\sigma_{00})
  +\lambda[U_k(\sigma_{10})-U_k(\sigma_{00})]
  +\lambda[U_k(\sigma_{01})-U_k(\sigma_{00})]
  +\lambda^2 C_k.                                    \tag{7.144}
\]

If rewards have absolute value at most `M`, then `|C_k|<=4M`.  Suppose the
two base sides satisfy

\[
 U_i(\sigma_{10})-U_i(\sigma_{00})\ge g,
 \qquad
 U_j(\sigma_{01})-U_j(\sigma_{00})\ge g>0.           \tag{7.145}
\]

For `lambda<=g/(8M)`, the full `i` response against the mixed `j`
environment has gain at least `g/2`, since its gain is exactly

\[
 [U_i(\sigma_{10})-U_i(\sigma_{00})]+\lambda C_i.
\]

Mixing only the `lambda` fraction of that response therefore gives the
literal partial side

\[
 U_i(\sigma_\lambda)-U_i(\sigma_{0\lambda})
 \ge {\lambda g\over2},                              \tag{7.146}
\]

and symmetrically for `j`.  The partial four-corner curvature has magnitude
at most

\[
 4M\lambda^2=o(\lambda g).                            \tag{7.147}
\]

There is also an exact universal warning at the final mixed corner.  Replacing
the remaining `(1-lambda)` share of player `i` by its full response gains

\[
 U_i(\sigma_{1\lambda})-U_i(\sigma_\lambda)
 =(1-\lambda)
   \bigl([U_i(\sigma_{10})-U_i(\sigma_{00})]
          +\lambda C_i\bigr).                       \tag{7.147a}
\]

Thus for `lambda<=min(1/2,g/(8M))` the doubly mixed profile still has literal
terminal exploitability at least `g/4`.  Small mixing is therefore not itself
a terminal-approximation device, even before asking for rowwise perfection.

Thus the hoped-for first-order-versus-second-order estimate is correct for
**terminal payoff edges**.  If each fork agrees with the source before its
cut, the mixed law does too; before the earlier cut the doubly mixed profile
retains the literal common source prefix and the same joint reach.

This still supplies no root-Nash estimate.  The following exact regression
shows that (7.147) can coexist with an order-one defect in a positively
reached row.

### Proposition 7.20.1 (conditional-hazard amplification regression)

Use Fin4 labels `i,j,k,l`.  Players `k,l` are passive.  For every nonempty
terminal coalition `S`, define

\[
 r_i(S)=r_j(S)=
 \begin{cases}
 1,&|S\cap\{i,j\}|=1,\\
 0,&|S\cap\{i,j\}|\ne1,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad r_k(S)=r_l(S)=0.                              \tag{7.148}
\]

Let both `i` and `j` use the source stopping law

\[
 \nu={1\over2}\delta_0+{1\over2}\delta_\infty,
\]

and let `k,l` play Never.  The source payoff of each active player is `1/2`:
exactly one of the two active players stops at date zero with probability
`1/2`.  Against the other source law, pure time zero and Never each pay
`1/2`, while every positive finite pure time pays `1`.  Hence

\[
 U_i(\sigma)=U_j(\sigma)={1\over2},\qquad
 B_i(\sigma)=B_j(\sigma)=1,qquad
 d_i(\sigma)=d_j(\sigma)={1\over2}.                  \tag{7.149}
\]

For both players choose the exact cap response `f=delta_1`.  Relative to
`Delta=1/2`, both source atoms are bad, the earliest bad source is date zero,
and the response receiver is the later date one.  Redirecting the whole bad
law gives exactly `f`; both source-attached later-moving forks have gain
`1/2` and common cut zero.

Let `sigma_lambda` independently mix weight `lambda` of `delta_1` into both
active source laws.  Each active stopping law is

\[
 {1-\lambda\over2}\delta_0
 +\lambda\delta_1
 +{1-\lambda\over2}\delta_\infty.                  \tag{7.150}
\]

At the four full-response corners, for either active observer,

\[
 U_{00}={1\over2},\qquad U_{10}=U_{01}=1,
 \qquad U_{11}=0,qquad C=-{3\over2}.                \tag{7.151}
\]

Consequently

\[
 U_i(\sigma_\lambda)=U_j(\sigma_\lambda)
 ={1\over2}+\lambda-{3\over2}\lambda^2,             \tag{7.152}
\]

each partial paid side has exact gain

\[
 {\lambda\over2}(1-3\lambda)>0
 \quad(0<\lambda<1/3),                               \tag{7.153}
\]

and the partial square curvature is `3 lambda^2/2`.  It is therefore
`o(lambda)` exactly as in (7.147).

Now decode (7.150) into its canonical live roots.  Each active player's Quit
hazards at dates zero and one are

\[
 a_\lambda={1-\lambda\over2},
 \qquad
 h_\lambda={2\lambda\over1+\lambda}.                \tag{7.154}
\]

After date one the tail is all Never.  At the date-one root, the pure Quit
and Continue endpoints of either active player are respectively

\[
 Q_\lambda=1-h_\lambda,qquad C_\lambda=h_\lambda.
\]

Thus, for `0<lambda<1/3`, the exact Quit-minus-Continue difference is

\[
 E_\lambda=Q_\lambda-C_\lambda
 ={1-3\lambda\over1+\lambda}.                       \tag{7.155}
\]

Both actions have positive support.  Hence any
`IsQuittingRootSupportApproxNash` tolerance at this row must satisfy

\[
 \delta\ge E_\lambda\longrightarrow1.               \tag{7.156}
\]

The usual endpoint/root-Nash tolerance is also bounded below by the pure-Quit
regret

\[
 \varepsilon_\lambda
 =(1-h_\lambda)E_\lambda
 ={(1-\lambda)(1-3\lambda)\over(1+\lambda)^2}
 \longrightarrow1.                                  \tag{7.157}
\]

The two player-labelled marginal hazard charge newly appearing at date one is

\[
 \chi^{\rm late}_\lambda=2h_\lambda
 ={4\lambda\over1+\lambda}\longrightarrow0.         \tag{7.158}
\]

Therefore

\[
 {\delta\over\chi^{\rm late}_\lambda}
 \ge {1-3\lambda\over4\lambda}\longrightarrow\infty.
                                                               \tag{7.159}
\]

Even if charge is counted over the entire two-date block,

\[
 \chi^{\rm block}_\lambda
 =2a_\lambda+2h_\lambda
 =1-\lambda+{4\lambda\over1+\lambda}\longrightarrow1,
                                                               \tag{7.160}
\]

so both the support defect and the ordinary root-Nash error are asymptotic to
the block charge rather than `o(charge)`.

There is no Bellman error hidden in this computation.  If the continuation
annotation at each row is the actual suffix terminal value of
`sigma_lambda`, then
`quittingRootSequenceTerminalValue_eq_successorPayoff_tailVector` gives the
Bellman identity exactly.  The date-zero endpoint difference is
`-lambda E_lambda`, while the date-one difference is `E_lambda`; the failure
is purely root Nash after conditional reconstruction.

The same obstruction is visible globally.  From `sigma_lambda`, changing one
active player all the way to its full time-one response gains

\[
 (1-\lambda)\left({1\over2}-{3\lambda\over2}\right)
 \longrightarrow {1\over2}.                         \tag{7.161}
\]

Thus the doubly mixed profile is not approaching terminal Nash either.

At the isolated value `lambda=1/3`, both displayed active roots become exact
and the partial response sides become neutral.  This is a useful boundary
check: a non-small finite-game re-equilibration can succeed accidentally, but
it has block charge `5/3` and supplies no vanishing error-to-charge family.

### Exact disposition

Independent small mixing does preserve actuality, the common source prefix,
two distinct paid labels, and the favorable quadratic terminal curvature.
It does **not** give a Nash--Bellman packet with residual `o(charge)`.  The
reason is not the terminal collision probability itself; it is the
conditional-hazard normalization at a newly exposed row.  A small stopping
mass can put both actions into support at a row where their endpoint gap is
fixed.

This regression has semantic minimum zero and is not a hard-residual
counterexample.  It proves the exact local no-go needed here: no theorem using
only the co-source debts, later-moving common-prefix forks, reach, bounded
rewards, and bilinear response-square curvature can justify the desired
charge-relative root-Nash estimate.  A successful consumer must additionally
re-equilibrate the exposed finite timing face, or supply a support-slack sign
which survives conditioning.  Re-equilibration is not a harmless local edit:
it may choose a fixed order-one mixture, a boundary strategy, or an unrelated
root and therefore needs a new source/renewal argument.

## 7.21 Sequential guarded flow is exact, but finite paid guard coverage is impossible

The natural nonlocal repair of Section 7.20 is to concatenate the two finite
fork blocks and choose a fresh continuation value at every boundary.  This is
not a nonlinear timing problem once the two root words have been fixed.

For a finite positively absorbing block `b`, exact Bellman evaluation through
the block is an affine homothety

\[
 T_b(w)=a_b+c_bw=(1-c_b)R_b+c_bw,
 \qquad 0\le c_b<1.                               \tag{7.162}
\]

All internal continuation vectors are affine functions of the exit value
`w`.  Each used-action support inequality is therefore affine in `w`.  If
`P_b(delta)` denotes the set of exit values for which every internal used
action is within `delta` of its best endpoint, then `P_b(delta)` is a compact
polytope.  For two blocks, all cyclic Bellman values lie on the segment
joining `R_0` and `R_1`; in its scalar coordinate the two maps are

\[
 z\longmapsto c_0z,
 \qquad z\longmapsto c_1z+(1-c_1).                \tag{7.163}
\]

Thus sequential re-equilibration is precisely a guarded affine flow.  The
ordinary-mathematics Theorem 9.1 of
`CODEX_SPINOZA__FINITE_TESTER_SEPARATION_AND_TWO_ESCAPE_BOUNDARY.md` proves
the exact alphabet-relative alternative:

1. an infinite guarded schedule gives finite cyclic Nash--Bellman packets at
   arbitrarily small additional error; repeating a positive-absorption cycle
   makes its charge arbitrarily large without accumulating Bellman error; or
2. a finite binary prefix tree, with rational interval/Farkas certificates
   for rational data, excludes every infinite guarded schedule in this
   two-block alphabet.

The closing step is genuinely justified by contraction.  If an open word has
survival product `C<1` and endpoint seam `eta`, its unique cyclic fixed point
is displaced by at most `C eta/(1-C)`.  Continuation stability then enlarges
each root support error only by the corresponding displacement.  This is the
correct sequential alternative; a balanced occupation of unrelated block
endpoints would not suffice.

### Proposition 7.21.1 (no finite source-complete paid tester alphabet)

Fix `gamma>0`.  There is no finite collection `B` with the following three
properties simultaneously:

* every `b in B` has a fixed player `i_b` and a fixed complete behavioral
  replacement `tau_b`;
* whenever `b` is declared applicable at an actual source `sigma`, its first
  paid edge is the literal replacement by `tau_b` and has terminal gain at
  least `gamma`; and
* at every actual profile at least one member of `B` is applicable.

Indeed, for each player `i` collect the finitely many `tau_b` with `i_b=i`.
The checked theorem
`exists_quittingBehaviorProfile_forall_mem_finiteMenu_payoff_le` supplies one
actual profile `sigma_B` at which none of these finitely many complete
replacements improves its owner's terminal payoff.  No member of `B` can be
applicable at `sigma_B`, contradicting coverage.

This statement is deliberately about **gain-retaining source-attached**
blocks.  A finite family of unpaid Nash--Bellman blocks may cover continuation
space, and a parametric block whose actual replacement varies with the source
is not a finite tester family.

The normalized rational exact-scale lower certificate does not evade the
proposition.  Its finite interval tree verifies a lower bound; it is not a
finite list of actual deviations.  Its decoder selects a response after the
source profile is supplied, and the checked two-range theorem forces
nonprecompact late-clock ranges for two identities.  Likewise the
finite-clock double-full-gap theorem produces two blocks at one specially
selected source and one source-dependent clock bound `H`; it does not make
those blocks applicable at a second continuation state.  Treating either
finite verifier as a finite guard cover would silently replace a
source-dependent witness by a fixed actual tester.

### Proposition 7.21.2 (the supplied block guard can be empty on the whole box)

There is also an exact separator showing that free choice of continuation
values does not repair this missing coverage.  Use the rational Fin4 table of
Section 8 of the Spinoza note: players `0,1` receive `-1` whenever they belong
to the terminal coalition, receive `1` when only the other active player
belongs, and the other two players receive zero.  Fix rational `0<p<1`.

At the common-cut charged source root, both active Quit probabilities equal
`p`.  For an arbitrary exit continuation `w in [-1,1]^4`, player zero's Quit
and Continue endpoints are

\[
 Q_0=-1,
 \qquad C_0=p+(1-p)w_0,
\]

and hence

\[
 C_0-Q_0=1+p+(1-p)w_0\ge2p.                       \tag{7.164}
\]

The symmetric inequality holds for player one.  Since both prescribed Quit
actions have positive support, the block guard is empty for every support
tolerance `delta<2p`.  In the ordinary mixed-root Nash convention, deviating
from the prescribed mixture to pure Continue improves by at least `2p^2`, so
the ordinary root-Nash guard is empty for `delta<2p^2` as well.

For the distinct-cut version, player zero quits with probability `p` at the
first row and player one quits with probability `p` at the next row.  Whatever
the final exit value `w_0>=-1`, exact evaluation of the later row gives player
zero continuation at least

\[
 p+(1-p)w_0\ge2p-1.
\]

At the first row player zero's Quit endpoint is `-1`, so its used Quit action
again loses at least `2p`.  Thus this block also has empty support guard for
`delta<2p`, over the **entire** reward box.  Each emptiness proof is a
one-inequality rational Farkas certificate: it combines only the endpoint
formula with the box facet `w_i>=-1`.

This regression has global minimum zero, so it is not a positive-minimum
counterexample.  Its exact scope is nevertheless enough for the producer
question: co-sourced debts, exact cap candidates, common-prefix reach, and two
positive block charges do not imply that either literal block belongs to any
small-error Nash guard after sequential continuation rebalancing.

### Exact disposition of the sequential route

For a **supplied** finite block alphabet, guarded flow gives the desired clean
primal/dual: an approximate periodic Nash--Bellman packet with repeatable
charge, or a finite rational exclusion tree.  What is unavailable is the
global adapter needed to apply that alternative to the positive-minimum
source.  Proposition 7.21.1 rules out a finite adapter made of fixed actual
paid testers, and Proposition 7.21.2 rules out deriving guard membership from
the current two-fork fields alone.

The remaining viable theorem must therefore produce a compact parametric
family of source-dependent blocks whose Nash guards cover the relevant
punishment-floor carrier, or prove a renewable rule which moves from every
failed guard to a new source while spending one bounded global account.  The
finite tree in the negative arm becomes a game-level separating certificate
only after such response completeness is proved.  Without it, it certifies
the failure of one two-letter schedule and nothing about unrestricted
behavioral deviations.

## 7.22 The canonical source-dependent fork grammar is total but not compact

The finite-menu obstruction suggests retaining only a finite **operator
tag**, while allowing its clocks and transported law to depend on the current
source.  Fix a scale `Delta>0`.  At a source `sigma`, the proposed canonical
operator records:

1. the least player label `i` with `d_i(sigma)>=Delta`;
2. the earliest finite source-supported `Delta/2`-bad clock, using Never when
   the bad mass has no finite support;
3. the least pure clock whose payoff is strictly above
   `B_i(sigma)-Delta/4`;
4. the earlier/later orientation of those two clocks; and
5. the stopping-law fork which moves the whole bad set to the selected
   receiver.

The player and orientation tags are finite.  After subtracting the common
first-disagreement date, the remaining clock datum is the relative span in
`Nat union {Never}`.

### Proposition 7.22.1 (pointwise applicability cover)

Suppose Fin4 has positive global minimum total debt `D_*>0`.  Put

\[
 \Delta={D_*\over4}.
\]

At every actual source some player has debt at least `Delta`.  The checked
bad-mass and stopping-selection lemmas, followed by
`positiveDebt_exists_commonPrefix_profitableStoppingLawFork`, therefore make
at least one canonical operator pointwise applicable.  It has a literal
source-prefix fork, opponent reach at its finite cut, and payoff gain bounded
below by

\[
 {\Delta^2\over16M}.                               \tag{7.165}
\]

Thus the **paid applicability guards** cover every hard source as a set.  No
compactness or Nash--Bellman conclusion is used here.  For a finite-clock
source of bound `H`,
`exists_finiteClockCandidate_payoff_eq_continuationBestResponseValue` permits
the receiver to be chosen from the finite internal/Late/Never candidate list.
After installing a Late candidate, however, the next source has bound `H+1`;
iteration does not retain one fixed finite candidate set.

### Proposition 7.22.2 (relative clock translation does not compactify the operator)

The anchored family (7.51)--(7.57) is already a family of canonical operators.
Player zero has the fixed supported bad source time `0`, the unique good
receiver is time `n`, and the gain is `1/2`.  The other active law is

\[
 {1\over2}\delta_n+{1\over2}\delta_\infty .        \tag{7.166}
\]

The selected cut is already zero, so quotienting common delays does nothing.
The operator's relative span is `n`.  In total variation the source laws for
different `n` have distance one, while the receiver laws `delta_n` have
distance two.  Hence neither the source family nor the actual compiler range
is precompact in the strategic metric of the executable-adapter grammar.

In the split Late/Never compactification, `delta_n` tends to the Late point
`omega`, not to Never.  This preserves the limiting pure-time obstacle but
does not produce an actual stopping law.  The sequence carries unit receiver
mass to `omega`, so it violates the tightness/recovery premise of the
executable compact-state theorem.  It also cannot be an `SD` edge: successive
receiver laws have total-variation distance two, so no summable finite-macro
budget decodes them.  Collapsing Late to Never would restore an actual limit
but would change the displayed gain from `1/2` to zero.

This is exactly the operation-facing form of the checked all-depth suffix
compactness no-go.  The finite tag remains constant; the noncompact datum is
the relative source/receiver clock itself.

### Proposition 7.22.3 (earliest-support normalization is nonclosed at fixed horizon)

There is a separate obstruction which does not use an escaping clock.  Fix
`0<alpha<1` and four players `o,j,k,l`.  Give only observer `o` a nonzero
reward, namely

\[
 r_o(S)=\mathbf 1_{\{j\in S\}},                    \tag{7.167}
\]

and let `j` stop surely at time two while `k,l` use Never.  For
`0<=epsilon<=alpha`, give `o` the actual stopping law

\[
 \nu_\epsilon
 =\epsilon\delta_0+(\alpha-\epsilon)\delta_1
    +(1-\alpha)\delta_\infty .                     \tag{7.168}
\]

The prescribed payoff is `1-alpha`, the cap is one, and the debt is exactly
`alpha`.  Pure times zero and one have payoff zero and are bad at scale
`Delta=alpha`; time two is the least cap receiver and has payoff one.  The
fork moves the total bad mass `alpha` to time two and gains exactly `alpha`.

For every `epsilon>0`, the earliest supported bad time is zero and the
normalized receiver span is two.  At `epsilon=0`, the earliest supported bad
time is one and the normalized receiver span is one.  Yet

\[
 \|\nu_\epsilon-\nu_0\|_1=2\epsilon\longrightarrow0.             \tag{7.169}
\]

Consequently the exact canonical graph

\[
 \epsilon\longmapsto
   (\text{earliest supported bad time},
    \text{receiver minus selected cut})              \tag{7.170}
\]

is not closed, even on a fixed two-date source simplex with fixed positive
debt, fixed gain, and unit opponent reach.  Translating by the selected cut
does not repair the graph: it turns the jump of the cut into the jump
`2 -> 1` of the relative receiver.

This is precisely excluded by the proof-relevant adapter grammar.  A unique
trace-visible selected output must have a closed graph on a compact parent
port.  The strict positive-support condition in “earliest supported” is not
closed, and no finite closed `Case` cover can retain the exact canonical
choice at (7.169).  The choice is legal pointwise after `nu_0` has been
reconstructed, but then it cannot be used as an edge in the preceding
projective trace.

The example does not say that every noncanonical bad-mass relation is
noncompact.  On a supplied uniformly tight, bounded-clock port one may weaken
the selector, keep a closed set of quantitative atom witnesses, and retain
overlapping boundary choices.  The present global source theorem supplies no
such uniform clock bound or individual-atom floor.

### Paid coverage is not Nash--Bellman coverage

Proposition 7.22.1 covers sources only by profitable complete-strategy
operations.  A guarded Bellman schedule needs each finite root word to be
approximately Nash against its chosen exit continuation.  Proposition
7.21.2 shows that a canonical paid fork block may have an empty such guard on
the whole reward box.  Hence pointwise totality of the operator relation does
not feed the positive arm of the guarded-flow theorem.

There is a precise but insufficient response to a failed guard.  If an
internal root at absolute reach `R` has ordinary one-root exploitability
`e`, replacing the player's behavior only at that reached live history by a
best endpoint and then following the old suffix changes its terminal payoff
by exactly `R e`.  Thus a lower bound on the **reached** root defect produces
another actual source-attached paid response and, by cap domination, another
debt of the same size at the original source.  An unweighted support defect
does not give this bound when its bad supported action or its internal reach
is small.

Even in the reached version, the new operation returns to the same paid-fork
waist.  Applying it changes the source; later players may replenish the debt
or erase the payoff gained by the earlier mover.  The horizon can increase
from `H` to `H+1`, relative spans can escape as in (7.166), and no current
payoff, total-debt excess, hazard capacity, or finite clock rank is known to
be monotone over the regenerated sequence.

### Exact disposition

The canonical source-dependent grammar has a genuine pointwise cover of all
positive-minimum hard sources, but it fails both requirements needed by the
projective guarded-flow route:

* its proof-relevant operation graph is not compact/closed, already at fixed
  horizon because of earliest-support selection and globally because of
  relative clock escape; and
* applicability of a profitable fork does not imply membership in the
  corresponding Nash--Bellman guard.

The finite failed-guard tree therefore acquires a game-level meaning only if
one adds one of two genuinely new fields: a uniformly tight closed
source-dependent witness correspondence with an actual recovery decoder, or
a renewable reached-defect transition with a bounded global account.  The
positive global minimum supplies pointwise totality but, by itself, supplies
neither field.

## 7.23 A closed soft-fork correspondence isolates essential Late escape

The nonclosed earliest selector is avoidable.  On each fixed finite-clock
simplex one can keep the whole cap-response correspondence and replace the
hard bad-set transport by a continuous soft transport.  This gives a valid
compact-witness edge at every fixed horizon and isolates horizon escape as
the only remaining **topological actuality** loss.  It does not repair the
separate Nash--Bellman guard failure.

### Proposition 7.23.1 (continuous soft fork)

Let `K` be a finite set of pure-time choices, let `nu` be a probability on
`K`, and let `v:K -> [-M,M]`, where `M>0`.  Put

\[
 C=\max_{s\in K}v(s),
 \qquad d_s=C-v(s),
 \qquad C-\mathbb E_\nu v\ge\Delta>0.              \tag{7.171}
\]

Choose **any** maximizer `q`, retaining the full argmax relation rather than
a least member, and define

\[
 \theta_s=(d_s-\Delta/2)_+,
 \qquad \lambda_s={\theta_s\over2M}.               \tag{7.172}
\]

Move the fraction `lambda_s` of the mass at `s` to `q`, leaving the remaining
mass at `s`; call the resulting law `f`.  Since `0<=d_s<=2M`, every
`lambda_s` lies in `[0,1]`.  Also

\[
 d_s\le\Delta/2+\theta_s,
 \qquad \mathbb E_\nu\theta_s\ge\Delta/2.          \tag{7.173}
\]

The total transported mass and exact payoff gain therefore satisfy

\[
 m:=\mathbb E_\nu\lambda_s\ge {\Delta\over4M},     \tag{7.174}
\]

and

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E_fv-\mathbb E_\nu v
 &=\mathbb E_\nu[\lambda_s(C-v(s))]\\
 &\ge {\Delta\over4M}\mathbb E_\nu\theta_s
 \ge {\Delta^2\over8M}.                           \tag{7.175}
 \end{aligned}
\]

Here the first lower bound uses `d_s>=Delta/2` whenever `theta_s>0`.
The construction is continuous in `(nu,v)` on each fixed `q` branch: it uses
only a finite maximum, positive part, products, and finite sums.  The relation

\[
 \{(\nu,v,q):v(q)=\max_sv(s)\}                     \tag{7.176}
\]

is closed and compact.  Thus keeping `q` as a proof-relevant witness gives a
closed set-valued compiler even though choosing its least member need not be
continuous.

### Corollary 7.23.2 (fixed-horizon compact operator cover)

Fix a finite-clock bound `H` and the corresponding product of source
simplices.  On the closed subset where total debt is at least `D_*`, put
`Delta=D_*/4`.  The finite union over player labels of

\[
 \{(\sigma,i,q):d_i(\sigma)\ge\Delta,
                 q\in\operatorname{Argmax}_i(\sigma)\}           \tag{7.177}
\]

is compact and projects onto every source.  The cap equality in (7.177) is
with the unrestricted behavioral cap by
`exists_finiteClockCandidate_payoff_eq_continuationBestResponseValue`.
Applying Proposition 7.23.1 is a continuous actual-law compiler on each
finite discrete `(i,q)` branch.  Hence (7.177), unlike the canonical earliest
selector, is a legitimate `CW` correspondence.

If `q` is finite, the soft fork has mass at least `m` at `q`.  If `q` is
Never, then `lambda_q=0`, so all transported mass came from finite source
times; retaining the source law instead retains finite stopping mass at least
`m`.  Therefore each branch also supplies a charge-retaining actual law with
finite stopping mass at least `Delta/(4M)`.  For the double-debt co-source,
the two soft correspondences give two distinct coordinatewise finite-mass
floors and reproduce the uniformly reached two-cut input without any
earliest-time selector.

The output of the horizon-`H` compiler may place mass at the auxiliary Late
date.  It then belongs to the next finite-clock simplex, not the old one.
Consequently finite-depth compositions form valid compact diagrams

\[
 C_H\longrightarrow C_{H+1}\longrightarrow C_{H+2}\longrightarrow\cdots,
                                                               \tag{7.178}
\]

but their projective limit is actual only under the tightness/recovery
passport of the executable compact-state theorem.  After the soft repair,
loss of actual stopping mass to Late is the only remaining compactness defect
of this operator layer; root-Nash guard coverage remains a separate issue.

### Proposition 7.23.3 (exact removable/essential horizon-escape split)

Let `M_{-i}` be the first opponent stopping time and `A_{-i}` its coalition
when finite.  Against fixed opponents, for every finite pure time `q`, direct
partition by the relative order gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 v_i(q)-v_i(\infty)
={}&\mathbb E[(r_i(A_{-i}\cup\{i\})-r_i(A_{-i}))
                 \mathbf1_{\{M_{-i}=q\}}]\\
 &+\mathbb E[(r_i(\{i\})-r_i(A_{-i}))
                 \mathbf1_{\{q<M_{-i}<\infty\}}]\\
 &+r_i(\{i\})\Pr(M_{-i}=\infty).                 \tag{7.179}
 \end{aligned}
\]

Thus

\[
 |v_i(q)-v_i(\infty)|
 \le2M\Pr(M_{-i}\ge q).                            \tag{7.180}
\]

Consider a sequence of finite cap receivers `q_n -> infinity`.

* If `v_i(q_n)-v_i(infinity)->0`, the escape is **removable**: replacing the
  receiver by actual Never changes the soft-fork gain by at most that amount.
  The approximate response correspondence can be made tight without losing
  a fixed fraction of (7.175).
* If along a subsequence

  \[
   v_i(q_n)-v_i(\infty)\ge\eta>0,                  \tag{7.181}
  \]

  the escape is **essential**.  Equation (7.180) forces

  \[
   \Pr(M_{-i}\ge q_n)\ge {\eta\over2M}.            \tag{7.182}
  \]

  The soft fork has receiver mass at least `m`, so under the literal
  unilateral fork the terminal event at date `q_n` whose coalition contains
  `i` has mass at least

  \[
   m{\eta\over2M}
   \ge {\Delta\eta\over8M^2}.                      \tag{7.183}
  \]

This is the exact failure at infinity.  It is not anonymous loss of
compactness: a nonremovable Late response carries a fixed opponent-tail
cylinder and a fixed actual finite terminal atom at every approximating
source.  In the split compactification (7.181) is precisely the strategic
separation between Late and Never.

### Two escaping co-source ranges

Apply the split to the two distinct debtors supplied at one finite-clock
source.  If an escaping receiver is removable, use Never at an arbitrarily
small loss and retain the finite bad source mass for the charge block.  If it
is essential, (7.182)--(7.183) gives that labelled fork its own fixed late
terminal atom.  Hence two essential escaping ranges give two distinct
**co-sourced sibling** late atoms with explicit floors.

They do not automatically give two persistent labels on one chronology.  If
their receiver times coincide, installing both produces a collision at that
date.  If, after label/order stabilization, one receiver is earlier, its fork
may absorb before the later receiver and destroy the latter's reach.  The
alternative that it retains survival to the later date is exactly the reached
two-fork arm; the alternative that it does not is exactly the intervening
absorption arm.  These are the already checked two-cut/Fable cases, now with
the infinity loss identified by (7.181), not a new Nash--Bellman packet.

Thus softening succeeds architecturally: it removes the artificial canonical
selector discontinuity and turns every fixed horizon into a closed total
operator correspondence.  The genuine residual is the Late recovery
boundary.  At that boundary the two-co-source theorem yields a collision,
reached two-clock fork, or intervening absorption, but the existing consumer
still returns an off-minimum/paid-splice port.  No current theorem preserves
both labels after that consumption or charges the resulting source
reprojection to a bounded global account.

## 7.24 Essential deadline entry does not cancel degree at infinity

The soft correspondence identifies the only strategy-topological boundary:
an essential finite receiver tends to the split `Late` endpoint while keeping
a positive payoff separation from actual Never.  It is tempting to pair the
two essential debtor labels supplied at one co-source and declare their
boundary contribution zero modulo two.  The finite-game entry homotopy shows
that this parity inference is false.

### Proposition 7.24.1 (the local datum is a positive normal sign, not an index)

Use the penalized finite-menu game `G_kappa(A,e)` of Proposition 22.1 in
`CODEX_SPINOZA__POSITIVE_MINIMUM_RESPONSE_COMPACTIFICATION_BOUNDARY.md`.  At a
first-entry point of a spanning equilibrium component, let player `i` be an
entrant whose new action has positive mass immediately after entry.  If `f`
is any supported old action of `i`, penalized indifference gives the exact
hard-payoff identity

```text
U_i(e,x_-i)-U_i(f,x_-i)=kappa_0.                     (7.184)
```

Under the positive-minimum hard source hypotheses, the checked entry estimate
gives

```text
kappa_0 >= D_*/4.                                   (7.185)
```

Thus the entrant-versus-old complementarity coordinate has a strictly
positive normal response sign when the artificial price is removed.  This
does **not** determine the local equilibrium index.  That index also depends
on every tangential active-response derivative and on cross-effects between
simultaneous entrants.  Neither (7.184), the retained terminal atom, nor the
two-co-source debt theorem supplies those signs.

There is an even earlier typing obstruction.  The two essential cap responses
from the double-debt theorem are unilateral deviations from one actual
finite-clock source.  Browder continuation supplies some spanning component
of `Z(A,e)`, but is not anchored at every prescribed old Nash point.  Hence
the two deviations are not automatically endpoints--or even points--of one
equilibrium component.  No component parity can be assigned to their pair
without a new source-anchored continuation theorem.

### Proposition 7.24.2 (a Late collar preserves mod-two degree)

Conditionally suppose that an old equilibrium component is isolated, has a
well-defined finite-game component index, and is followed through a generic
penalized entry homotopy.  The one-dimensional Late/Never complementarity
coordinate is

```text
y=0:       h(x_-i)-kappa <= 0,
0<y<1:     h(x_-i)-kappa  = 0,
y=1:       h(x_-i)-kappa >= 0.                       (7.186)
```

After a regular perturbation, crossing this collar transports the component
index.  Modulo two its normal factor is one.  Adding two independent Late
coordinates multiplies by `1*1=1`, rather than adding two labelled points and
obtaining zero.  Consequently a nonzero-mod-two old component can carry its
entire parity to the horizon boundary.  This is the degree-at-infinity form
of the anchoring obstruction: finite-menu global degree `+1` need not leave
any uncancelled finite root when the Late face itself has odd degree.

This statement uses the ordinary finite-game component-index theorem and its
homotopy invariance, with the normalization recorded in
`CODEX_BOREL__STRICT_RAY_TAIL_NORMALIZATION.md`; it is not a Lean theorem.

### Exact two-player quitting-face falsifier

The lack of cancellation is already visible on a common late date.  Freeze
players `2,3` at Never and let players `i,j` choose between Never (`N`) and
Quit at the common late date (`L`).  Put

```text
a_i=r_i({i}),       b_i=r_i({i,j})-r_i({j}),
a_j=r_j({j}),       b_j=r_j({i,j})-r_j({i}).          (7.187)
```

If `y` is `j`'s Late probability, `i`'s Late-minus-Never difference is

```text
g_i(y)=(1-y)a_i+y b_i,                               (7.188)
```

and symmetrically for `j`.  In both of the following rational tables take
`a_i=a_j=1`, so both labels have the same strictly positive essential
Late-over-Never sign at the all-Never corner and first enter at shadow price
one.

1.  If `b_i=b_j=1`, Late is strictly dominant at zero price.  The bottom
    face has one strict equilibrium `(L,L)`, of index `+1`.
2.  If `b_i=b_j=-1`, then `g_i(y)=1-2y` and
    `g_j(x)=1-2x`.  The bottom face has strict equilibria `(L,N)` and `(N,L)`,
    each of index `+1`, and the regular mixed equilibrium `(1/2,1/2)`, of
    index `-1`.  Its total index is again `+1`, hence odd modulo two.

Both examples are literal quitting reward faces: choose
`r_i({i})=r_j({j})=1`, choose the two collision increments in (7.187) to be
`+1` or `-1`, and set the irrelevant frozen-player coordinates arbitrarily.
They have identical positive entrant signs but different cross-response
geometry.  Most importantly, neither gives the proposed zero mod-two Late
contribution.  These local completions are not claimed to instantiate the
full positive-minimum hard residual; they prove the precise logical point
that essential-range signs and co-source labels alone do not imply boundary
cancellation.  The full hard residual would need an additional theorem
forcing a zero-index tangential face, and the earlier apex-index audit found
no such sign constraint.

Therefore the source-dependent guarded/degree-at-infinity route currently
ends in a **sharp no-go**, not a positive producer: its fixed-horizon graph is
closed and its only escape has a quantitative positive normal sign, but that
sign may carry odd equilibrium degree to infinity.  A viable degree argument
must add either source-anchored component data plus a genuinely cancelling
tangential index, or a theorem excluding the essential Late boundary itself.

## 7.25 Marked-order compactness passes caps but not source reprojection

The counterfactual-scale target asks for one limit retaining all four
player-deleted laws around selected near-cap clocks.  At the purely ordinal
level there is a finite compact object which does exactly the payoff-facing
part of that job.  The same object has an exact suffix-congruence failure,
even when every selected marker is cap attaining.

### Proposition 7.25.1 (common-source marked-order subsequence)

Let `I` be any fixed finite player set.  For each `n`, let

```text
sigma^n=(mu_i^n)_(i in I)
```

be an actual product stopping-law profile on
`N union {infinity}` and choose one deterministic response time `t_i^n` for
every player.  Sample the source clocks `T_i^n` independently.  Record the
decorated total preorder of the `2|I|` labelled objects

```text
{T_i^n : i in I} union {m_i^n : i in I},
```

where `m_i^n` denotes the deterministic marker `t_i^n`, and also record which
objects equal Never.  Let `pi_n` be the law of this decorated preorder.

There are only finitely many such decorated preorders.  Hence a subsequence
has

```text
pi_n -> pi                                             (7.189)
```

in total variation.  The following quantities are fixed linear functionals
of `pi_n` and therefore converge:

1. the prescribed terminal-coalition law and every coordinate of
   `U(sigma^n)`;
2. for each player `i`, the terminal-coalition law and payoff obtained by
   replacing `i` with the pure response `t_i^n`;
3. for each `i`, the source-attached ordinal counterfactual array recording
   the first opponent coalition strictly before `t_i^n`, tied at `t_i^n`, or
   after it, including the opponent-Never event.

All four counterfactual arrays are marginals of the **same** law `pi`; their
compatibility is not imposed after separate subsequence choices.

If the chosen times are `epsilon_n`-best replies, with

```text
0 <= B_i(sigma^n)-V_i^(sigma^n)(t_i^n) <= epsilon_n,
epsilon_n -> 0,                                       (7.190)
```

then, on this subsequence,

```text
B_i(sigma^n) -> V_i^pi(m_i)                           (7.191)
```

for every player.  Thus current payoff and all selected cap limits pass
exactly through one source-attached compact object.

#### Proof

The finite decorated-preorder simplex is compact.  The terminal outcome of
the source clocks is determined by the least finite equivalence class among
the `T` labels, with Never if every `T` label is decorated infinite.  After
replacing `i` by its marker, the terminal outcome is determined by the least
finite class among `m_i` and the opponent `T` labels.  Hence the prescribed
and replaced terminal laws are pushforwards of `pi_n` under maps of one fixed
finite set, and all bounded reward evaluations are linear in `pi_n`.

The before/tie/after array is another marginal: inspect the first finite
opponent class and compare it with `m_i`.  Since all arrays come from one
decorated preorder, their projective identities survive the limit.  Finally,
the two terms in (7.190) differ by at most `epsilon_n`, while the selected
response value already converges as a linear functional.  This proves
(7.191). QED.

This proposition is stronger than four separately compactified Late/Never
values and weaker than the conjectural rooted-scale object.  It retains all
ordinal information relevant to the four selected deviations but not finite
clock distances, unselected response times, or a literal child suffix.

### Proposition 7.25.2 (exact cap markers do not make the state suffix-congruent)

Use the rational four-player table from the checked
`COUNTERFACTUAL_MARKOV_ORDER_AND_SUFFIX_COMPACTNESS_NO_GO`.  Number its players
`1,2,3,4` and put

```text
r(S)=(1, 1_({2,3} subset S), 0, 0)                   (7.192)
```

for every nonempty coalition, with payoff zero at Never.  Fix
`0<a<1`.  In `sigma`, player `1` has stopping law

```text
a delta_0+(1-a)delta_infinity,
```

whereas in `rho` it has

```text
a delta_1+(1-a)delta_infinity;                        (7.193)
```

the other three players are Never in both profiles.

Choose player `1`'s response marker to be `0` at `sigma` and `1` at `rho`.
Both are exact cap responses of value one.  Choose Never as the marker for
players `2,3,4`; their cap is zero, so these are exact cap responses as well.
The two decorated marked-order laws of Proposition 7.25.1 are identical.
Indeed, (7.193) translates player `1`'s finite source atom and its own marker
together, and all other source clocks and markers remain in the common Never
class.  The prescribed payoff and cap vectors are likewise identical:

```text
U(sigma)=U(rho)=(a,0,0,0),
B(sigma)=B(rho)=(1,0,0,0).                            (7.194)
```

Nevertheless their literal suffixes after one all-Continue outcome satisfy

```text
U(S_1 sigma)=0,
U(S_1 rho)=a e_1.                                    (7.195)
```

Both histories have positive reach (`1-a` and `1`).  Thus there is no
well-defined literal one-step suffix operation on the marked-order state,
and no inequality can bound the source-reprojection seam (7.195) by the
distance of those states: that distance is zero while the seam is `a`.

This strengthens the relevance of the checked current-response quotient
regression to the multiscale target.  The failure persists after attaching
one exact cap marker to every player and retaining all four views in a single
joint order law.  It does not refute the existence of a richer scale tree;
it proves that such a tree must remember an alignment with the current
calendar origin or carry the resulting reprojection seam explicitly.

### Corollary 7.25.3 (compactness/source-reprojection trilemma)

There are three distinct levels.

1.  Quotienting by source-relative order gives the compact state in
    Proposition 7.25.1 and passes current payoff and selected caps, but loses
    literal suffixing by Proposition 7.25.2.
2.  Retaining every labelled absolute-depth suffix probe would restore that
    information, but the checked theorem
    `not_isSeqCompact_of_commonAllDepthSpikeState` gives an infinite uniformly
    separated family even when every tested history has a common positive
    reach floor.
3.  A finite program of fixed depths remains continuous, but an adaptively
    moving response/source cut can leave that finite program and recreate
    (7.195) at its new scale.

Therefore the unconditional multiscale compactness theorem stops at
Proposition 7.25.1.  It gives a useful common-source semantic limit, but not
an executable source-reprojection edge.  Any successful nonlocal consumer
must be program-dependent and reveal the next child cut before compact
extraction, or else charge an explicit non-summable alignment seam.  Merely
adding more Late labels or the selected cap values cannot close this waist.

## 7.26 A charge-quantized adaptive holonomy program is compact on one source

The exact datum missing from the date-zero/date-one regression is already
present in the checked boundary-holonomy layer.  A finite source prefix is
not represented merely by its entry payoff and cap.  It carries the operator
which transports the actual exit semantic pair back to that entry, and it
retains the fact that consecutive operators were cut from one common root
word.

### Proposition 7.26.1 (finite actual-prefix transfer state)

Let `P` be a nonempty finite word of product roots and let `tau` be an actual
behavioral tail.  Write

```text
E(tau)=(U(tau),B(tau))
```

for its prescribed terminal payoff and unrestricted behavioral cap.  Let
`H(P)` be the checked `QuittingBoundaryHolonomy` of the word.  Playerwise,

```text
U_i(P*tau) = H(P).prescribed_i.eval(U_i(tau)),
B_i(P*tau) = H(P).bestResponse_i.eval(B_i(tau)).       (7.196)
```

The first map is affine.  The second has the exact form

```text
z |-> max(early_i, tail_i+survival_i*z),              (7.197)
```

where `early_i` is the best pure stopping payoff within `P` and
`survival_i` is opponent-only survival through `P`.  Thus the finite probe

```text
Probe(P,tau)=(E(P*tau), H(P).coordinates, E(tau))     (7.198)
```

separates the input, the executable prefix operation, and its actual exit.

If `P=A*B` is a literal chronological cut of one source word, then

```text
H(P)=H(A)*H(B),                                       (7.199)
```

and the exit semantic pair stored for `A` is literally the entry semantic
pair stored for `B`.  Equation (7.199) is associative.  It is not inferred
from close scalar coefficients; it is certified by equality of the cut
indices in the same actual root family.

These are checked identities.  The relevant declarations are

```text
QuittingAffineSummary.eval_compose,
QuittingMaxAffineSummary.eval_compose,
quittingFiniteBoundaryHolonomy_prescribed_eval,
quittingFiniteBoundaryHolonomy_bestResponse_eval,
quittingFiniteBoundaryHolonomy_concat,
quittingContinuationBestResponseValue_literalRootStack_eq_capFold.
```

The last declaration is the infinite behavioral-tail interchange needed in
(7.196); the finite holonomy theorem by itself asserts only the finite
Bellman envelope.

### Audit on the date-zero/date-one regression

Ask for the literal first-stage cut in Proposition 7.25.2.  At `sigma`, its
exit tail is all Never and has payoff zero.  At `rho`, the first-stage root is
all Continue and its exit tail still carries the hazard-`a` atom, with payoff
`a e_1`.  Hence the exit field of (7.198) separates the two sources exactly.
The affine identity reconstructs their common entry payoff from different
prefix/exit pairs.

If instead an adaptive program selects the moving active atom and translates
the preceding all-Continue calendar padding away, the two probes may agree.
That agreement is legitimate: the selected macros are time-translation
equivalent and have the same terminal and response semantics.  What may not
be done is to identify them and later request the unrecorded fixed one-step
suffix.  Thus (7.198) separates precisely the operations actually declared
by the finite program.

### Proposition 7.26.2 (bounded-capacity finite adaptive program)

Fix a compact annotation carrier `K`, a reward table bounded by `M`, and a
number `C` such that every finite exact Nash--Bellman block in `K` has total
marginal-hazard charge at most `C`.  Fix `gamma>0`.

Consider any source-dependent, set-valued adaptive program which, after
seeing an actual exact block `B`, selects an ordered list of pairwise disjoint
forward subblocks

```text
P_0,...,P_(m-1)                                      (7.200)
```

of `B`.  The selection may depend arbitrarily on all previously exposed
annotations, labels, and holonomy coefficients.  Require only that every
selected subblock has hazard charge at least `gamma`.  Then

```text
m*gamma <= hazardCharge(B) <= C.                     (7.201)
```

In particular `m <= floor(C/gamma)`.  After padding the list to this fixed
length, all tuples consisting of

```text
(entry annotation, exit annotation, holonomy coordinates, finite labels)
```

lie in one compact finite product.  Every finite sequence of such programs
therefore has a subsequence on which the number and finite labels stabilize
and all real probe coordinates converge.

At a limiting coefficient tuple, the affine/max-affine evaluation and
composition identities pass by continuity.  At every approximating finite
program, the common-parent cut certificate makes the chronology literal.
No deterministic earliest-cut selector is required: retaining all ordered
cut lists satisfying (7.200)--(7.201) avoids its lower-hemicontinuity issue.

#### Proof

Hazard charge is a sum of nonnegative stage charges and is additive over
disjoint intervals.  Summing the lower bounds for (7.200) proves (7.201).
The endpoint annotations lie in `K`.  The checked theorem
`quittingFiniteBoundaryHolonomy_coordinates_mem_box` places every player's
five real coefficients in

```text
[-M,M] x [0,1] x [-M,M] x [-M,M] x [0,1].            (7.202)
```

There are uniformly finitely many tuple positions and finite labels, so the
ambient product is compact.  The coordinate composition map is continuous
by `continuous_quittingBoundaryHolonomyCoordinatesCompose`, and literal
composition is (7.199). QED.

In the Fin4 no-uniform-payoff regime, the checked declaration

```text
finFour_hasBoundedFiniteExactNashBellmanHazardCapacity_of_no_uniformPayoff
```

supplies such a finite `C` on the canonical bounded carrier.  Proposition
7.26.2 is therefore not adding a capacity assumption beyond the maintained
hard residual.

### Exact scope boundary

This positive theorem is an **adaptive verifier on one supplied exact
block**, not a producer from an arbitrary paid fork.

1. A sequence of tiny-charge or zero-charge queries is not bounded by
   (7.201).  Such rows must be aggregated into a selected macro or discarded
   as calendar gauge; querying their literal depths re-enters the checked
   all-depth noncompactness obstruction.
2. Compactness in (7.202) is compactness of coefficient/end-point probes.
   The subset realized by arbitrary-length actual blocks need not be closed.
   Retaining the literal natural-valued length destroys compactness by
   `not_isCompact_range_nat_pair_const`.
3. Most importantly, a unilateral paid replacement changes the actual
   source.  Its successor is not a later subblock of `B`, so the exit/entry
   identity in (7.199) is absent.  Starting a new exact block at that source
   invokes the same capacity bound afresh; (7.201) does not telescope across
   the reprojection.

Thus the source-dependent finite-probe question has a positive exact answer
inside one genealogy.  The first missing global datum is now precise: a paid
fork must either preserve the common-parent exact block, or pay a source-
reprojection account which is shared across successive regenerated blocks.
No currently checked field supplies that account.

## 7.27 Co-sourced exact children need not share one capacity genealogy

The capacity theorem in Proposition 7.26.2 becomes useful across regenerated
sources only if the paid children can be put back into one literal exact
Nash--Bellman word.  Here is the self-contained producer question.

### Question 7.27.1 (capacity-shared source reprojection)

Suppose two paid child certificates are produced from one actual source.
Each child consists of

```text
(entry semantic pair, finite root word, exit semantic pair),              (7.203)
```

has hazard charge at least `gamma`, and retains its common-source prefix
ancestry.  Can one produce a finite exact Nash--Bellman parent word and two
pairwise disjoint chronological intervals in that word such that

1. the roots and entry/exit annotations on each interval are literally those
   of the corresponding child;
2. the source-prefix maps into the same parent genealogy rather than merely
   into two unilateral siblings; and
3. every gap between the two intervals is itself an exact Nash--Bellman
   connector with matching endpoint annotations?

If so, hazard additivity gives

```text
2*gamma <= hazardCharge(parent),                                          (7.204)
```

and repeated descendants spend one common capacity account.  The finite-
clock double-gap/common-prefix construction does not supply this conclusion.
Its two positive blocks have a common right endpoint at the all-Never suffix,
so as originally cut they overlap.  Reordering or copying one block requires
exactly the new connector and endpoint compatibility in item 3.  Moreover,
the construction does not in general assert root-Nash or Bellman typing for
the child roots.  The following example shows that adding that typing still
does not repair the connector.

### Proposition 7.27.2 (exact co-source sibling regression)

There is a rational four-player quitting table, one actual source, and two
co-sourced paid children with all of the following properties:

- each child is a one-row exact Nash--Bellman block;
- each has hazard charge one and is reached with probability one;
- the two children have the same literal all-Never exit tail; and
- no finite exact Nash--Bellman parent contains both children as
  endpoint-preserving disjoint subblocks.

#### Construction

Use players `0,1,2,3`.  The all-Never payoff is zero.  Define the nonempty-
coalition rewards by

```text
r({0})   = (1,2,1,1),
r({1})   = (2,1,1,1),
r({0,1}) = (0,0,1,1),
r(S)     = (1,1,1,1) for every other nonempty S.                           (7.205)
```

Let `sigma` be all Never.  Against `sigma_{-0}`, player `0`'s pure clock zero
has value one, and against `sigma_{-1}`, player `1`'s pure clock zero has
value one.  Thus both players have debt one at the same source.

Let `C_0` be the one-row profile in which player `0` Quits surely and every
other player Continues, followed by the all-Never exit.  Its entry annotation
and exit annotation are

```text
v^0=(1,2,1,1),       z=(0,0,0,0).                                         (7.206)
```

This row is exact Nash against `z`: player `0` compares Quit payoff `1` with
Continue value `0`; player `1` compares prescribed Continue payoff `2` with
the joining payoff `0`; and players `2,3` compare `1` with `1`.  Its Bellman
successor is exactly `v^0`.  Define `C_1` symmetrically.  It is also exact,
with

```text
v^1=(2,1,1,1),       z=(0,0,0,0).                                         (7.207)
```

Both rows absorb surely, so each has hazard charge one and actual reach one.
They are precisely the two pure finite-clock replacements of the common
all-Never source, and they share its literal all-Never exit.

#### No shared exact genealogy

Assume first that an endpoint-preserving copy of `C_0` occurs before one of
`C_1` in a finite exact Nash--Bellman parent.  The intervening word would have
entry annotation `z=0` and terminal annotation `v^1`.  But player `2` receives
reward one at **every** nonempty quitting coalition in (7.205), and
`v^1_2=1`.  For any product root `q`, if `s(q)` is its joint Continue
probability, the Bellman update in coordinate `2` is

```text
F(q,w)_2 = (1-s(q))*1 + s(q)*w_2.                                         (7.208)
```

Starting from terminal value `w_2=1`, (7.208) remains exactly one at every
preceding row.  Hence every finite Bellman word terminating at `v^1` has
entry coordinate `1`, never `0`.  No connector from `z` to `v^1` exists.
The reverse order gives the same contradiction with terminal value `v^0`.
Since two disjoint intervals in one linear word must occur in one of these
orders, the desired parent does not exist.

Even the most direct tail substitution already displays the strategic seam.
If `C_0` is placed immediately before `C_1`, player `0` at the first row
compares Quit payoff `1` with Continue value `v^1_0=2`; its prescribed Quit
action is no longer Nash.  The reverse order fails symmetrically.  Putting
both Quit actions at the same date produces coalition `{0,1}` and is neither
a pair of disjoint blocks nor either child's terminal semantics.

### Consequence and exact scope

The existing co-source/common-prefix fields do not imply a shared-capacity
embedding, even after separately strengthening both children to exact
Nash--Bellman blocks.  The first missing executable datum is one of the
following genuinely chronological objects:

1. an exact Nash--Bellman connector from one child's exit to the other's
   entry, together with a declared order in one common parent root family; or
2. a tail-insensitivity theorem which permits reannotating the earlier child
   without changing its root-Nash inequalities, again with literal ancestry.

Common source, common exit, positive reach, positive charge, and separate
exactness do not imply either object.  This is a producer-level no-go rather
than another abstract verifier: (7.205)--(7.208) are the complete finite
counterexample.

The table also has exact terminal Nash profiles `C_0` and `C_1`, so its global
minimum debt is zero.  It therefore does **not** refute a theorem which uses
the full positive-minimum hard residual to produce the missing connector.  It
does prove that positive minimum would have to enter that production
essentially; it cannot be appended as a passive label to the current
