co-source two-response output.  Until such a theorem exists, the two children
start two capacity accounts rather than spending disjoint portions of one.

## 7.28 Positive minimum forces an off-minimum profile or a signed response seam

The failure of a same-parent connector has no sign by itself.  The global
minimum does, however, control what happens when one exactifies a child by an
actual near-cap response.  This gives the strongest unconditional dichotomy
available from the scalar debt identity.

### Proposition 7.28.1 (minimum-fibre response-seam dichotomy)

Let

```text
D_* = inf_x D(x) > 0,
D(x) = sum_l (B_l(x)-U_l(x)),                           (7.209)
```

where `x` ranges over actual behavioral profiles of a four-player quitting
game.  Fix any actual profile `x`.  Then at least one of the following holds.

1. `D(x) >= D_*+D_*/16`.
2. There are a player `k` and a pure-time-or-Never response producing the
   actual profile `y=x[k<-rho]` such that

   ```text
   U_k(y)-U_k(x) >= 3D_*/16,                            (7.210)
   ```

   and `D(y) >= D_*+D_*/16`.
3. There are such `k,y` and some coordinate `l` for which either

   ```text
   B_l(y)-B_l(x) > D_*/56,                              (7.211a)
   ```

   or `l!=k` and

   ```text
   U_l(x)-U_l(y) > D_*/56.                              (7.211b)
   ```

Thus the third branch is a definite directed semantic seam on the same
literal unilateral-response edge.  It is not inferred from the failed
connector between two different children.

#### Proof

Suppose branch 1 fails.  Choose `k` with maximal debt at `x`.  Since there are
four players,

```text
d_k(x) >= D(x)/4 >= D_*/4.                              (7.212)
```

Pure-time/Never extremality of the unrestricted behavioral cap gives a
response `rho` within `D_*/16` of `B_k(x)`.  For `y=x[k<-rho]`, its actual
gain `g` therefore satisfies

```text
g=U_k(y)-U_k(x) >= d_k(x)-D_*/16 >= 3D_*/16.            (7.213)
```

If branch 2 fails as well, global minimality and the failure of branches 1--2
give

```text
D(y)-D(x) > -D_*/16.                                    (7.214)
```

Put `Delta B_l=B_l(y)-B_l(x)` and
`Delta U_l=U_l(y)-U_l(x)`.  Expanding the definition of total debt gives the
exact transfer identity

```text
sum_l Delta B_l - sum_(l!=k) Delta U_l
  = g + D(y)-D(x)
  > D_*/8.                                              (7.215)
```

The left side is a sum of the seven signed quantities

```text
Delta B_0,...,Delta B_3,  -Delta U_l (l!=k).             (7.216)
```

At least one exceeds `(D_*/8)/7=D_*/56`, which is exactly
(7.211a) or (7.211b). QED.

No cap attainment is used.  Replacing `D_*/16` by any smaller response error
only improves the constants.

### Corollary 7.28.2 (two co-sourced children)

Apply Proposition 7.28.1 separately to the two actual child profiles supplied
by the finite-clock double-full-gap/common-prefix construction.  Either one
of the two children or one of their two selected response profiles has total
debt at least `17D_*/16`, or **both** child-response edges carry a signed
component of magnitude greater than `D_*/56` in the orientation
(7.211a)--(7.211b).

This is genuinely source attached: each response starts from the actual
child to which it is assigned.  It is also genuinely global: (7.214) is the
only place the positive minimum is used.  But it is not a capacity-shared
consumer.  The two applications live on sibling sources, their selected
players and signed coordinates may coincide, and neither (7.215) nor failure
of the old connector puts them into one exact parent word.  Iteration can
therefore replenish the same cap coordinate after every reprojection.

### Sharp abstract semantic regression

The scalar minimum identity cannot force the off-minimum branches in
Proposition 7.28.1.  To see the precise limitation, retain the Bellman layer
of (7.205)--(7.208), hence the two exact children and the no-connector
invariant.  On the semantic layer attach to every declared actual node the
same debt vector

```text
d=(1,1,0,0),        D=2=D_*.                            (7.217)
```

At the first child declare a player-`0` cap-response edge with payoff gain
one and increase only `B_0` by one; at the second declare the symmetric
player-`1` edge.  Keep (7.217) at the response nodes by increasing the mover's
prescribed payoff by the same amount.  On each edge,

```text
D(y)-D(x)=0,
sum_l Delta B_l-sum_(l!=k)Delta U_l=1,                  (7.218)
```

and the entire signed account is carried by the mover's cap rise.  Every node
remains exactly on the positive minimum fibre, while the constant-spectator
argument (7.208) still forbids the common-parent connector.

This is an abstract semantic/Bellman regression, not a claimed realization
by one quitting reward table: its purpose is to test only the inference from
positive total debt, the exact transfer identity, and the existing co-source
fields.  It shows that these fields allow permanent on-minimum cap transport.
Consequently a stronger theorem must use an additional hard-residual
constraint to convert the signed seam into chronological charge or to stop
its renewal.  Connector failure plus `D_*>0` alone does not do so.

## 7.29 The hard-residual fields return the signed seam to existing ports

This section performs the promised stopping audit.  It tests the two terms in
(7.211) against punishment normality, the singleton minimum margin, and the
retained positive terminal atom.  The result is a precise duplicate boundary,
not a new consumer.

### 7.29.1 The paid row is already present before the signed split

At every non-off-minimum child `x` in Proposition 7.28.1, the maximal debtor
`k` satisfies `d_k(x)>=D_*/4`.  Applying

```text
positiveDebt_exists_actualJointReach_paidFirstDisagreementRow
```

with `Delta=D_*/4` produces on that same actual source a literal
`QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow` with

```text
mover gain >= D_*/16,
joint reach >= D_*^2/(512*M^2).                         (7.219)
```

Indeed the checked division-free inequality is

```text
Delta^2 <= 32*M^2*jointReach.                           (7.220)
```

The strengthened

```text
positiveDebt_exists_actualJointReach_paidRow_withSupport
```

adds that the source witness is either a finite clock with positive mass in
the mover's actual stopping law or Never with positive Never mass.  Thus
alternative (i) in the present audit is already exactly the maintained
quantitative paid-port producer.  It does not use the `D_*/56` seam,
punishment normality, or the retained coalition atom.

For a sequence of children, Fin4 pigeonhole freezes `k`.  It also freezes the
source/receiver order type after passing to the usual finite-versus-Late-
versus-Never subsequence.  This does not make the rows consecutive roots of
one chronology.

### 7.29.2 What one signed coordinate adds

Write

```text
a=D_*/56
```

and freeze, along a subsequence, the mover `k`, signed coordinate `l`, and
whether (7.211a) or (7.211b) holds.  Own-cap invariance under a unilateral
change gives

```text
B_k(y)=B_k(x),                                          (7.221)
```

so the cap-rise coordinate in (7.211a) is automatically distinct from `k`.
There are only finitely many such labels and orientations.

If the cap-rise arm holds, put `e=U_l(y)-U_l(x)`.

- If `e<=a/2`, then

  ```text
  d_l(y)-d_l(x)=(B_l(y)-B_l(x))-e>a/2.                 (7.222)
  ```

  Hence `d_l(y)>D_*/112`, and the same checked theorem at the literal target
  `y` produces a distinct-`l` paid row with gain `D_*/448` and joint reach at
  least `D_*^2/(401408*M^2)`.
- If `e>a/2`, the original complete `k`-response has a positive
  `l`-externality of size `a/2`.  The role-swapped actual-reach theorem
  localizes it to a `k`-controlled first-disagreement row.  It is an
  externality row, not a profitable `l` row.

If the payoff-drop arm holds, put

```text
h=U_l(x)-U_l(y)>a.
```

- If `B_l(y)-B_l(x)>=-a/2`, then

  ```text
  d_l(y)-d_l(x)=(B_l(y)-B_l(x))+h>a/2,                 (7.223)
  ```

  and the same distinct-`l` paid-row conclusion follows.
- Otherwise `l`'s cap drops by more than `a/2`.  An old-source near-cap
  response for `l`, installed at both endpoints, witnesses the corresponding
  response-square gain in the reverse orientation.  This is the cap-reset
  seam, not a forward exact connector.

The payoff-loss localization itself is already
`CODEX_DESCENDANT__SIGNED_EXTERNALITY_ACTUAL_REACH_AND_RESPONSE_CYCLE.md`,
using the two `Fable` declarations named above after copying payoff coordinate
`l` into controller `k`'s auxiliary reward coordinate.  The cap-rise/payoff-
loss split and its canonical bad-mass source-supported version are Sections
2--4 of
`CODEX_BOX_PORT__MACROSCOPIC_SIGNED_SEAM_AND_BOUNDARY_STALL.md`.
Equations (7.222)--(7.223) merely restate their first finite split at the
constants of Proposition 7.28.1.

### 7.29.3 Why this is not two persistent clocks

In the debt-rise subarm there are two distinct player labels, but only two
behavioral response ports.  The first paid row in (7.219) is a localized
comparison between two pure clocks inside the complete `k`-response.  Its
literal replacement profile need not be the complete target `y` at which the
`l` row is selected.  In the complementary subarms, both localized rows can
be controlled by `k` anyway.

In neither case are the rows exact product Nash against declared successor
annotations, and no Bellman endpoint equality joins them.  Consequently they
do not give two nonsummable marginal hazards on one exact spine.  This is
exactly the typing boundary recorded in Sections 4--5 of
`CODEX_ROOT__FOUR_CLOCK_GUARD_RENEWABLE_SCREENING_BOUNDARY.md`: behavioral
gain is not the absorption charge of a punishment-floor admissible exact
edge.

The retained positive terminal atom does not repair the type.  Unless the
complete response is proved to occur after its marked causal cut, it may
preempt or change that atom.  If the response is after the mark, the atom is
retained but supplies no Nash--Bellman conditions for either paid row.  If it
is before the mark, earliest-disagreement localization gives the already
known premark charged block/paid-port dispatch.  Thus no new instance of the
two-persistent-clock consumer is produced.

### 7.29.4 Punishment floor and support descent

At an actual global minimum, the checked singleton margin and punishment
normality give

```text
Pun_l <= singleton_l <= U_l                              (7.224)
```

for every player; if the limiting debt support is a singleton, the vanishing
stopping-law mixture repairs its only weak coordinate.  Therefore a
source-attached minimum sequence carrying (7.219) enters the existing

```text
QuittingPaidRowFloorSafeSource
```

and its checked

```text
exists_markedExactOrbit_alternative_of_witness
```

alternative.  This is precisely the minimum-floor paid entrance proved in
`SOCIAL_WEIGHT_REVIEW__MINIMUM_FLOOR_REPAIR_TO_EXACT_PORT_AND_STRICT_CURL_NOGO.md`:
uniform payoff or a source-matched summable all-Continue semantic port with
positive suffix reach.  It is the existing exact-port waist, not a terminal
consumption of the signed seam.  At a merely off-minimum or collar profile,
(7.224) is not supplied.

Finally, on a finite-clock source an exact cap response kills its mover's
current debt.  This is not a finite support descent.  Other previously zero
debt coordinates may become positive, and the killed coordinate may reactivate
after the next response.  Likewise moving stopping-law mass from a supported
source clock to a receiver may delete one support point and add another, so
cardinality need not fall.  The genuine rank described in Section 9.3 of
`CODEX_SOURCE_GATE__SATURATION_FACE_UE_REGRESSION_AND_MARKED_TWO_PORT_AUDIT.md`
requires preservation of **every** old zero debt plus creation of one new
zero.  The checked `QuittingFixedLawResetDispatch` lacks exactly that compound
field.

Hence alternatives (ii)--(iii) do not follow.  After finite-label freezing,
the hard-residual data yield only:

```text
existing actual paid port
  | existing role-swapped/cap-reset port
  | existing minimum-floor exact-port alternative
  | nonrenewable killed-debt coordinate.                              (7.225)
```

This lane should stop here; continuing would rederive the already maintained
cap-leak/paid-port theorems without adding a common ancestry or shared
capacity account.

## 7.30 Full-debt response minimax ends at a correlated law or balanced occupation

The full-debt chamber permits a genuinely global finite calculation which is
different from local paid-row extraction.  It uses all four cap-response
endpoints at once.

### 7.30.1 Response-hull trichotomy

Let `x` be one actual full-debt source, with prescribed payoff `U` and four
attained full responses `y^i=x[i<-rho_i]`.  Put

```text
g^i=U(y^i)-U(x).                                         (7.226)
```

Exact cap attainment gives

```text
g^i_i=d_i(x)>0.                                          (7.227)
```

Let `C=conv{g^0,g^1,g^2,g^3}`.  Exactly one of the following priority-ordered
alternatives holds.

1. **Pareto law:** `C` contains a nonzero vector `g>=0`.
2. **Balanced occupation:** alternative 1 fails and `0 in C`.
3. **Strict response separator:** `C` is disjoint from the nonnegative
   orthant, and there is `alpha>>0` such that

   ```text
   alpha dot g^i < 0       for every i.                  (7.228)
   ```

#### Proof

Intersect the compact convex set `C` with the closed nonnegative orthant.  A
nonzero intersection is alternative 1.  If the intersection is exactly
`{0}`, alternative 2 holds.  If the intersection is empty, strict separation
gives a nonzero `alpha>=0` with

```text
max_(g in C) alpha dot g < 0.                            (7.229)
```

The normal is nonnegative because the other separated set is the entire
unbounded nonnegative orthant.  Since there are only four strict inequalities,
adding a sufficiently small positive multiple of the all-ones vector makes
every coordinate of `alpha` positive while preserving (7.229).  This proves
(7.228). QED.

If exact cap attainment is unavailable, choose simultaneous `epsilon_n`-cap
responses for all four players on one actual source sequence and pass to a
common terminal-law/payoff subsequence.  The limiting vectors satisfy
`g^i_i=d_i>0`, and the same finite trichotomy applies.  In the finite-clock
co-source setting the pure cap candidates are attained; the double-full-gap
theorem is therefore the two-column projection of this four-column chamber
calculation.

### 7.30.2 The genuine endpoint in the Pareto arm

Let `mu` be the source terminal law and `mu_i` the response-endpoint laws.  If

```text
g=sum_i lambda_i g^i >=0,      g!=0,                    (7.230)
```

then

```text
nu=sum_i lambda_i mu_i
```

is a genuine probability law on terminal outcomes, with payoff `U+g`.
Because every source debt is strictly positive, choose `t>0` so small that

```text
t*g_i <= d_i=B_i-U_i       for every i.                 (7.231)
```

The correlated terminal law

```text
nu_t=(1-t)mu+t*nu                                           (7.232)
```

has payoff

```text
W=U+t*g,       U<=W<=B,       W!=U.                      (7.233)
```

Thus full debt converts response-hull approachability into a strict Pareto
endpoint inside the natural debt box.  This is a real terminal-outcome law,
not merely a formal vector.

It is not, however, an actual behavioral semantic pair.  The mixture in
(7.232) uses a public choice among complete response profiles.  Independent
behavioral strategies produce the whole response rectangle, including
uncontrolled multiple-response vertices.  Moreover the full-debt box theorem
says that every exact product root against every `W in [U,B]` is all
Continue.  Hence the correlated improvement cannot be installed as an
absorbing stationary root.  A direct consumer would need either a product-
law realization of `nu_t` whose unrestricted caps are controlled, or a
global carrier comparison proving that this correlated law forces a lower-
debt actual profile.

### 7.30.3 What the other two arms mean

In alternative 2 there are weights `lambda_i` such that

```text
sum_i lambda_i U(y^i)=U.                                 (7.234)
```

Every used mover gains strictly in its own coordinate, so at least two
response vectors occur; their cross-player externalities cancel exactly.
Equation (7.234) is an honest finite occupation balance and the corresponding
mixture of endpoint terminal laws has the source payoff.  It is not a product
stopping law and does not order the responses chronologically.

In alternative 3, `alpha` is only a normal to the four selected response
directions.  If it additionally satisfies the global terminal-row conditions

```text
0 <= alpha dot U,
alpha dot r(S) <= alpha dot U for every nonempty S,       (7.235)
```

then the zero bias gives the checked positive weighted-welfare **cap**.  A UE
conclusion further requires the separately typed one-sided security
certificates at `U` used by

```text
isUniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_oneSidedGuarantees_of_positiveWeightedWelfareBias.
```

Punishment normality gives punishment floors below `U`; it is not such a
one-sided security certificate at `U`.  If (7.235) fails, the failure is the
explicit live-row certificate `alpha dot U<0` or a terminal coalition `S`
with `alpha dot r(S)>alpha dot U`.  Thus a response-local minimax separator
does not become a terminal welfare separator automatically.

### 7.30.4 Exact rational balanced-occupation regression

The balanced arm is compatible with every local field of the full-debt
packet, including the whole frozen debt box.  Let the source profile have
players `0,2` Quit surely at one common date `n>=1`, while players `1,3` play
Never.  Define the complete reward table by

```text
S         r(S)
{0}       (-3, 3, 1,-1)
{1}       (-3,-3, 0,-3)
{2}       ( 1,-1,-3, 3)
{3}       ( 0,-3,-3,-3)
{0,1}     (-4, 2, 4, 0)
{0,2}     ( 0, 0, 0, 0)
{0,3}     (-1, 0, 0,-2)
{1,2}     ( 0,-2,-1, 0)
{1,3}     ( 0,-4, 0,-4)
{2,3}     ( 4, 0,-4, 2)
{0,1,2}   (-1, 1, 3,-3)
{0,1,3}   (-1,-1, 0,-1)
{0,2,3}   ( 3,-3,-1, 1)
{1,2,3}   ( 0,-1,-1,-1)
{0,1,2,3} (-1,-4,-1,-4).                               (7.236)
```

The source payoff is `U=0`.  The four pure cap responses are, respectively,

```text
0 leaves after n:   g^0=r({2})     =( 1,-1,-3, 3),
1 joins at n:       g^1=r({0,1,2})=(-1, 1, 3,-3),
2 leaves after n:   g^2=r({0})     =(-3, 3, 1,-1),
3 joins at n:       g^3=r({0,2,3})=( 3,-3,-1, 1).       (7.237)
```

For every player, quitting earlier gives its singleton reward `-3`, following
the source gives `0`, and the displayed response gives `1`.  Hence

```text
B=(1,1,1,1),       d=(1,1,1,1),       D=4.              (7.238)
```

The source atom `{0,2}` has probability one and every response cut is reached
with probability one.  The singleton moat is exact:

```text
B_i-r_i({i})=4=D       for every i.                     (7.239)
```

Punishment normality also holds.  Opponents can force `{1}` against player
`0`, `{3}` against players `1,2`, and `{1}` against player `3`; the
Continue/Quit pairs are `(-3,-4)` in each case.

Against every continuation `v in [0,1]^4`, players `0,2` strictly Continue:
their Quit-minus-Continue differences are negative at every opponent pure
coalition.  Once they Continue, player `1` strictly Continues regardless of
player `3`, and then player `3` strictly Continues.  Therefore all Continue
is the unique exact product root throughout the entire displayed box
`[U,B]`.

Yet

```text
g^1=-g^0,       g^3=-g^2.                              (7.240)
```

More explicitly, every response-hull vector has the form

```text
(x,-x,z,-z).                                            (7.241)
```

It is coordinatewise nonnegative only when it is zero.  Uniform occupation
of the four response endpoints has payoff exactly `U=0`, while every mover's
own gain is one.  Thus there is no nonzero Pareto response mixture and no
strict social potential along all four response directions.  The all-ones
weight annihilates every `g^i`; it is not a global terminal normal because

```text
sum_j r_j({0,1})=2>0=sum_j U_j.                         (7.242)
```

This is a complete ordinary quitting-game regression for the minimax step.
It is not a counterexample to uniform equilibrium: all Never is an exact
terminal Nash profile because every singleton own reward is `-3`, so the
table's true global minimum debt is zero.  Its force is exact and narrower:

```text
full displayed debt + four co-sourced attained responses
+ singleton moat + punishment normality + positive atom
+ unique all-Continue root on the whole debt box
does not exclude a zero-drift response occupation.                       (7.243)
```

Therefore the only full hard-residual input absent from (7.236) is the
comparison with **all actual behavioral profiles** asserting `D_*>0` and
global minimality of this source.  Any global full-debt consumer must use
that comparison to exclude the balanced occupation or to realize its
correlated law as a product stopping profile.  Co-source response gains,
social-surplus separation, and finite occupation balance alone do not do so.

## 7.31 Public mixing preserves debt; calendar mixing needs new guards

The balanced arm of Proposition 7.30.1 cannot be consumed merely by calling
its convex coefficients a temporal schedule.  There are three distinct
implementations, and each has a different obstruction.

### 7.31.1 A revealed public lottery makes caps affine

First consider an *augmented* quitting game in which a finite public signal
`a in A` is drawn with law `lambda` before play, observed by every player,
and phase `a` prescribes an actual behavioral profile `sigma^a`.  Write
`U_i^a` and `B_i^a` for its payoff and unrestricted cap.  Then the public-plan
payoff and cap satisfy the exact identities

```text
U_i^pub = sum_a lambda_a U_i^a,
B_i^pub = sum_a lambda_a B_i^a.                         (7.244)
```

The first identity is linearity of expectation.  For the second, every
phase-contingent deviation has payoff at most the displayed average of the
phase caps.  Conversely, for each phase choose an arbitrarily near-cap
behavioral deviation and use it only after observing that phase.  Finiteness
of `A` lets the approximation errors be summed and then sent to zero.  Cap
attainment is not required.  Consequently

```text
D^pub = sum_a lambda_a D(sigma^a) >= D_*.               (7.245)
```

In particular, if `x` is a global-minimum source and the balanced response
occupation has average prescribed payoff `U(x)`, then

```text
sum_i B_i^pub >= sum_i U_i(x) + D_*
              = sum_i B_i(x).                          (7.246)
```

Thus even an exogenous revealed lottery does not Nashify the balanced
occupation.  It reproduces the average of the phase debts.  If every used
phase is itself a minimum-debt profile, equality holds in (7.245); any
positive-weight off-minimum phase makes the appropriate averaged excess
strict.

More generally every actual candidate `zeta` obeys

```text
sum_i (B_i(zeta)-B_i(x))
  >= sum_i (U_i(zeta)-U_i(x)).                          (7.247)
```

This is just `D(zeta)>=D_*` with `D(x)=D_*`, but it is the exact global
cap-control condition missing from the response-hull argument.  If
`U(zeta)=U(x)`, total cap cannot fall.  If every coordinate debt of `zeta`
were at most `epsilon`, then `D_* <= 4 epsilon`; hence no such construction
can yield terminal approximants for `epsilon<D_*/4`.

### 7.31.2 The original game has no nonabsorbing public selector

The revealed signal used above is not endogenous to the quitting game.
Conditional on no absorption, the public history at every date is the unique
word consisting entirely of all-Continue actions.  A common random variable
measurable from that history is therefore constant.  There are only two
native substitutes:

1. private randomization, whose independent player coins generate a product
   rectangle including simultaneous-response cross vertices; or
2. a publicly observed Quit action, which reveals a phase only by ending the
   game before the selected continuation profile can run.

The distinction is already visible in the exact table (7.236).  Applying the
displayed responses of players `0` and `1` simultaneously changes the source
coalition `{0,2}` to `{1,2}`, whose reward is

```text
r({1,2})=(0,-2,-1,0).                                  (7.248)
```

This cross vertex is not a public convex mixture of the two one-player
response endpoints.  Hence the balanced response law is correlated data,
not an actual behavioral profile supplied by the original information
structure.

### 7.31.3 A deterministic phase calendar does not preserve the convex law

A finite or periodic calendar is public, but it does not choose one complete
profile at time zero.  If phase `p` has an unnormalised terminal contribution
`eta_p` and Continue mass `c_p`, the terminal law of the schedule is weighted
chronologically:

```text
eta_0 + c_0 eta_1 + c_0 c_1 eta_2 + ... .              (7.249)
```

It is not the arithmetic mixture appearing in the balance equation
`sum_i lambda_i g^i=0`.  Moreover an unrestricted deviator observes the
calendar and survival event and may optimize separately at every phase.
Root regrets are nonnegative phasewise quantities, so signed cancellation of
the endpoint payoff vectors does not cancel those regrets.  A punishment tail
controls only deviations that reach it; it cannot punish a profitable
deviation which absorbs during the current phase.

There is also an exact box obstruction at a full-debt minimum source.  On the
whole semantic box `[U(x),B(x)]`, the checked minimum-debt homotopy theorem
isolates all Continue as the exact root.  Therefore any exact positive-
absorption phase in a periodic Nash--Bellman schedule must take its successor
continuation outside this box.  Equivalently, for every fixed absorption
floor `a>0`, compactness of the root simplex times the box gives a positive
minimum root-Nash defect among roots of absorption at least `a`.  The missing
operation is a semantic collar crossing or connector, not the selection of
better convex weights.

### 7.31.4 Exact repair datum and status

The smallest noncircular datum which repairs the calendar construction is a
literal finite guarded Nash--Bellman cycle:

```text
(V_p,q_p)_{p mod m},
q_p exact root Nash against V_{p+1},
V_p = F(q_p,V_{p+1}),
sum_p absorption(q_p)>0,                               (7.250)
```

together with the source ancestry and punishment-floor fields required by
the existing periodic compiler.  These equations control phase-contingent
unrestricted deviations and give the chronological weights (7.249).  They
are strictly stronger than a balanced identity among four terminal response
vectors.  A same-parent exact connector producing these guards would also
repair the capacity-shared genealogy gap of Proposition 7.27.2.

The alternative datum, a sequence of actual product schedules whose payoffs
converge to `U(x)` and whose unrestricted debts converge to zero, is already
the desired terminal approximation and cannot be used as a producer without
circularity.  Thus endogenous temporal/public mixing supplies no new
consumer from the full-debt co-source theorem alone.  The exact trichotomy is:

```text
revealed public choice  -> caps and debts average;
hidden private choice   -> unwanted product cross vertices;
absorbing public choice -> phase is revealed only after termination.     (7.251)
```

Positive minimum is essential in (7.245)--(7.247): rather than excluding
the obstruction, it proves that a revealed balanced occupation retains a
macroscopic aggregate cap.  The smallest genuinely new global hypothesis is
therefore not another paid response, but a guarded chronological connector
whose Bellman and root-Nash equalities survive all behavioral deviations.

## 7.32 Chattering has a first-order singleton-moat Nash cost

The remaining nearby proposal is to implement the balanced occupation by a
rapid cycle of small hazards.  Cross events then have probability `O(h^2)`,
so the signed endpoint drift can indeed cancel to first order.  The local
Nash errors do not cancel.

A narrow duplicate search found that the linear estimate below is precisely
the full-debt specialization of Proposition 1 and Corollary 2 in the reviewed
ordinary-mathematics note
`CODEX_RAMSEY__STRICT_ALLCONTINUE_BASIN_LINEAR_DEFECT.md`.  Equations
(7.252)--(7.257) record the application rather than claim a new basin
theorem.  The new calculation here is the survival-normalized periodic
consequence (7.259)--(7.261) for the proposed balanced chatter.

Let `x=(U,B)` be a full-debt global minimum, put

```text
d_i=B_i-U_i>0,       s_i=r({i})_i,
m_i=U_i-s_i,         m=min_i m_i.                      (7.252)
```

The checked singleton margin gives

```text
B_i-s_i >= D_*,
m_i >= D_*-d_i = sum_{j != i} d_j > 0.                 (7.253)
```

Hence `m>0`.  Consider any finite phase family of product roots `q_p^h` and
successor continuation vectors `V_p^h` such that

```text
max_p sum_i q_{p,i}^h -> 0,
max_p dist_infinity(V_p^h,[U,B]) -> 0.                 (7.254)
```

Write `Q_i(q_-i)` and `C_i(q_-i,V)` for the current Quit and Continue
payoffs.  Uniformly over the finite phase family,

```text
C_i(q_p^h,V_p^h)-Q_i(q_p^h)
  = V_{p,i}^h-s_i + O(M sum_{j != i}q_{p,j}^h)
  >= m/2                                                    (7.255)
```

for all sufficiently small `h`.  The last constant `1/2` is inessential.
It follows from continuity; an explicit reward-bound estimate gives the
same conclusion after shrinking the neighborhood in (7.254).

Let `e_{p,i}^h` be the coordinate root-Nash defect.  Since Continue is
strictly better in (7.255), the exact binary-root defect identity gives

```text
e_{p,i}^h
  = q_{p,i}^h (C_i-Q_i)
  >= (m/2)q_{p,i}^h.                                  (7.256)
```

If `a_p^h=1-product_i(1-q_{p,i}^h)` is the phase absorption, then

```text
sum_{p,i} e_{p,i}^h
  >= (m/2) sum_{p,i}q_{p,i}^h
  >= (m/2) sum_p a_p^h.                               (7.257)
```

Thus the aggregate local root error is not `o(total absorption)`.  First-
order cancellation of the Bellman drift and second-order control of product
cross terms do not affect (7.257), because Nash defects are nonnegative.

There is an equivalent exact-root statement.  If `q_{p,i}^h>0` is supported
by an exact root, player `i` cannot have the strict Continue advantage in
(7.255).  An interior exactification requires

```text
V_{p,i}^h = s_i + O(M sum_{j != i}q_{p,j}^h).          (7.258)
```

But every point of `[U,B]` has coordinate `i` at least `m_i` above `s_i`.
Therefore a corrector of size `O(h)` cannot exactify a positive `O(h)` Quit
hazard.  The continuation must move a definite distance, asymptotically at
least `m_i`, through the semantic collar.  This is the infinitesimal version
of the box obstruction in Subsection 7.31.3.

### 7.32.1 Repetition leaves an order-one unrestricted cap defect

The periodic denominator makes the obstruction stronger, not weaker.
Suppose a fixed finite phase shape satisfies

```text
q_{p,i}^h = h a_{p,i}+o(h),
lambda_i := sum_p a_{p,i}>0,
Lambda := sum_i lambda_i.                              (7.259)
```

Assume first that the phase Bellman equations are exact.  Against the
repeated cycle, let player `i` deviate to Continue at every date.  The exact
one-shot telescoping formula weights (7.256) by opponent-survival prefixes.
Those prefixes tend to one over a single small cycle, while the opponent
contraction denominator is

```text
1-product_p product_{j != i}(1-q_{p,j}^h)
  = h(Lambda-lambda_i)+o(h).                           (7.260)
```

Consequently the terminal improvement satisfies

```text
liminf_{h->0} improvement_i(h)
  >= m_i lambda_i/(Lambda-lambda_i) > 0.               (7.261)
```

The same conclusion holds when the aggregate Bellman error per small cycle
is `o(h)`: after division by (7.260) it contributes only `o(1)`.  In
particular a four-label balanced occupation with every `lambda_i>0` cannot
become terminal Nash through chattering.  Its per-row errors tend to zero,
but their survival-normalized sum stays order one, exactly as the checked
periodic residual-charge formula predicts.

If the limiting action gap had the opposite sign, mixing a Quit probability
`O(h)` would leave Continue mass near one on the suboptimal action and the
root defect would already be order one.  Hence the only way any chattering
scheme can have root error `o(absorption)` is the genuine first-order
complementarity condition

```text
V_{p,i}^0=s_i
```

for every coordinate carrying positive limiting hazard.  The full-debt
singleton moat excludes precisely that condition throughout `[U,B]`.

This is a sharp no-go for the proposed local repair, not for schedules which
make an order-one collar excursion.  Such an excursion again requires the
source-preserving guarded connector isolated in Section 7.31; balanced drift,
cross-term suppression, and `O(h)` cap re-equilibration alone cannot supply
it.

## 7.34 One counterfactual threat date improves E3, but the sure gate is not recursive

The active-witness pattern admits a genuine one-date improvement.  Use four
conditional roots

```text
q_0=(0,41/52,16/45,4/5),
q_1=(17/60,0,0,0),
q_2=(4/15,4/5,0,1),

```

The corresponding marginal laws are

```text
player 1: (41/52,0,11/65,11/260; Never 0),
player 2: (16/45,0,0,29/45; Never 0),
player 3: (4/5,0,1/5,0; Never 0).                     (7.272)
```

Exact evaluation in the unnormalised E3 table gives

```text
U=(69042689/52650000,
   12348659/17550000,
   36460199/3290625,
   37241303/52650000),
B=(6451/4875,161/225,721/65,421/585),                 (7.273)

B-U=(628111/52650000,
     209341/17550000,
     40426/3290625,
     648697/52650000).
```

Therefore

```text
max_i(B_i-U_i)=648697/52650000 < 1/80.                (7.274)
```

The pure-time cap witnesses are

```text
player 0: date 3 / after support / Never (a three-way tie),
player 1: date 0,
player 2: date 0,
player 3: date 0.                                     (7.275)
```

For exact reproducibility, after normalising rewards by `17` the verifier
stores exploitability `648697/895050000 < 1/1360`.  The temporary certificate
is `/tmp/E3_LOW_DEBT_PROFILE_CLOCK4_CERTIFICATE.json`, its SHA-256 is

```text
c73850ccd69249674276baf907ad65611fa427f6e72731b712ef033dd9851665,
```

and the exact verification command is

```text
python3 ../Experiments/fin4_exact_search/run.py verify \
  /tmp/E3_LOW_DEBT_PROFILE_CLOCK4_CERTIFICATE.json.   (7.276)
```

This improvement identifies an exact structural operation.  At date `2`,
player `3` Quits surely.  Hence date `3` is never reached under the prescribed
profile.  It is also never reached after a unilateral deviation by any
player `i != 3`, because player `3` still Quits at date `2`.  Only a deviation
by player `3` can expose the suffix.  Consequently appending or changing the
date-`3` law preserves `U` and every cap `B_i` for `i != 3`, while it may
change `B_3`.  The row `(0,1,1,0)` is therefore a literal one-coordinate
counterfactual cap corrector, not a convex or on-path perturbation.

The same observation gives the obstruction to naive recursion.  At the
corrector row, the nonempty coalition `{1,2}` Quits surely.  If player `3`
has deviated past the first sure gate, that coalition absorbs before any
deeper suffix.  If another player deviates, the earlier sure Quit of player
`3` already absorbs.  Thus no unilateral deviation can see a fifth date.
All deeper dates are semantically invisible to both prescribed payoff and
all four caps.

More generally, after a sure-player gate owned by `j`, a suffix can correct
only player `j`'s cap.  Once that suffix contains a sure nonempty opponent
coalition, the counterfactual branch terminates and cannot be iterated.  A
genuinely recursive E3 construction must therefore avoid a sure gate on its
live branch, or replace it by a positive-survival gate and pay the resulting
actual reach in the other three debt coordinates.  The exact one-date
improvement (7.271)--(7.274) is evidence for this counterfactual-tail route,
but it does not yet give `eta(E3)=0`.

## 7.35 Softening the sure gate has the wrong first-order sign

The most direct attempted recursion can be decided exactly.  Keep the first
two roots in (7.271), keep the corrector `q_3=(0,1,1,0)`, and replace the
sure player-`3` gate by

```text
q_2(h)=(4/15,4/5,0,1-h),       0 <= h <= 1.          (7.277)
```

Thus `h` is precisely the newly opened probability of surviving date `2`
conditional on reaching it.  The prescribed payoffs are affine in `h`; their
right derivatives at zero, in the unnormalised E3 table, are

```text
U'(0)=(13717/506250,
       -13717/810000,
       -397793/2106000,
       -27434/3290625).                              (7.278)
```

For player `0`, the cap at `h=0` is tied between date `3`, the dates after
the support, and Never.  The derivatives of those pure-time tester values
are respectively

```text
1276/14625, 638/14625, 638/14625.                    (7.279)
```

Hence the right derivative of `B_0` is `1276/14625`.  The active cap
witnesses of players `1,2,3` are the strict earlier witnesses in (7.275), so
their cap derivatives are zero.  Consequently every debt has strictly
positive right derivative:

```text
(B-U)'(0+)=(395879/6581250,
            13717/810000,
            397793/2106000,
            27434/3290625) > 0 coordinatewise.       (7.280)
```

In particular the maximal debt increases for all sufficiently small
positive `h`.  No fifth-date row repairs this calculation: after player `3`
is removed by a deviation, the pair `{1,2}` still Quits surely at date `3`,
whereas every other unilateral deviation leaves the earlier player-`3` Quit
in place.  Thus every date-`4` law has exactly zero effect on `U` and on all
four caps, for every `h`, not merely to first order.

### 7.35.1 The smallest deterministic branching chain also fails

To make a fifth date visible, the sure pair at date `3` must be reduced.  Two
singleton replacements preserve the old player-`3` threat at `h=0`:
`{0}` and `{1}`.  Indeed, against either singleton player `3` obtains value
`1` by its best Quit/Continue choice, the same value supplied by `{1,2}`.

First take the singleton `{1}` and put the strongest later punishment for
player `1`, the sure pair `{0,2}`, at date `4`:

```text
q_3=(0,1,0,0),        q_4=(1,0,1,0).                 (7.281)
```

At `h=0` the complete semantic pair is still exactly (7.273): prescribed
play never reaches date `3`, deviations by players other than `3` are stopped
at date `2`, and the player-`3` cap threat remains `1`.  On opening the gate,
exact enumeration gives

```text
U'(0)=(507529/13162500,
       -13717/810000,
       -630982/3290625,
       -589831/52650000),

B'(0+)=(319/4875,0,0,0),

(B-U)'(0+)=(353771/13162500,
            13717/810000,
            630982/3290625,
            589831/52650000)>0.                      (7.282)
```

The putative date-`4` correction of player `1` misses its target: player
`1`'s cap is uniquely attained at date `0`, so every later tester remains
inactive near `h=0`.  The punishment can lower later player-`1` tester
values, but it cannot change `B_1`; its only first-order semantic effect is
through prescribed payoff.

The only player with a late active cap is player `0`.  The analogous best
deterministic branch is

```text
q_3=(1,0,0,0),        q_4=(0,1,1,1),                 (7.283)
```

where the date-`4` triple gives player `0` best-response value `0`.  Again
the semantic pair at `h=0` is exactly (7.273).  The exact right derivatives
are now

```text
U'(0)=(315491/10530000,
       -96019/17550000,
       -630982/3290625,
       -589831/52650000),

B'(0+)=(319/6500,0,0,0),

(B-U)'(0+)=(201289/10530000,
            96019/17550000,
            630982/3290625,
            589831/52650000)>0.                      (7.284)
```

Here the later punishment does reach the player with the late cap, but it is
one date too late.  Once player `0` is the sole Quitter at date `3`, its pure
deviation *at date `3`* exposes the singleton payoff before the date-`4`
punishment.  This tester has derivative `319/6500` and becomes the active
cap witness.  No choice at date `4` can lower it.

These examples are instances of a general triangular visibility fact.  If a
finite profile is perturbed only from date `t` onward, every pure-time tester
strictly before `t` is unchanged.  Hence a cap uniquely attained before `t`
is locally insensitive to the suffix.  Conversely, if a sure singleton
owned by `j` is installed at date `t` in order that a deviation by `j` expose
date `t+1`, the cap of `j` also contains the pure tester which Quits at date
`t`; a later punishment cannot alter that tester.  A deterministic
successive-owner correction can work only after a macroscopic active-face
switch which makes a late tester binding while avoiding a profitable owned
singleton at the exposing date.

Thus neither the one-parameter opening (7.277) nor the smallest deterministic
branching chain contracts the E3 debt.  The exact remaining option is a
genuinely probabilistic live branch, in which no exposed correction row is a
sure singleton and prescribed play pays positive mass into the deeper tail.
That is not a local continuation of (7.271): it must rebalance an active cap
face and its on-path payoff simultaneously.

## 7.36 Exact logit scaling produces a tropical singleton-refusal certificate

The endogenous soft-cycle producer admits a sharper analysis than its
uniform row bound.  This analysis is ordinary mathematics.  A narrow
duplicate search found the aggregate returned-block obstruction in
`CODEX_GAUSS__CURL_FREE_TOGGLE_POTENTIAL.md`, Propositions 47--49, and the
checked algebraic identity
`QuittingNormalizedSingletonSourcePacket.refusal_sub_mixture_eq_mass_div_mul_surplus`
in `Quitting/Classification/SingletonPacketRefusal.lean`.  The result below
identifies the positive relative-regret arm left between them.

Take a sequence of exact-return soft cycles of periods `H_n` and temperatures
`theta_n`, with roots `x_(n,t,i)` and values `v_(n,t)`.  Assume

```text
H_n theta_n -> 0,
S_n:=sum_(t,i)x_(n,t,i) -> 0,
v_(n,0) -> v.                                         (7.285)
```

The first condition is exactly the producer's `H_n epsilon_n -> 0`, up to
the fixed factor `log 2`.  Put

```text
lambda_(n,i)=sum_t x_(n,t,i),
alpha_(n,i)=lambda_(n,i)/S_n.
```

After a subsequence, `alpha_n -> alpha` in the player simplex.  Write
`s_i=r_i({i})` and

```text
m_i=v_i-s_i,             m=min_i m_i.                 (7.286)
```

### Theorem 7.36.1 (zero-temperature cyclic classification)

Under (7.285),

```text
v=sum_i alpha_i r({i}),
m_i>=0,
alpha_i>0  ==>  m_i=m.                                (7.287)
```

Thus `alpha` solves the positive-level singleton complementarity system

```text
(M alpha)_i>=m,
alpha_i>0 ==> (M alpha)_i=m,                           (7.288)
```

where `M_(i,j)=r_i({j})-s_i`.  Moreover, if `d_(n,i)` denotes the complete
terminal debt of the repeated soft word, then for every `alpha_i<1`,

```text
d_(n,i) -> alpha_i*m/(1-alpha_i).                     (7.289)
```

In particular, if `alpha` has at least two support points, then either

```text
m=0 and max_i d_(n,i)->0,                             (7.290)
```

or `m>0` and precisely the positive-mass owners retain the explicit positive
refusal debts in (7.289).  If `alpha=e_i`, the terminal coalition law instead
converges to the singleton `{i}` and the owner's debt remains the unclassified
player-deleted normalization.

#### Proof

At a soft root the exact endpoint gap and exact two-action regret are

```text
g_(n,t,i):=C_(n,t,i)-Q_(n,t,i)
          =theta_n log((1-x_(n,t,i))/x_(n,t,i)),

e_(n,t,i)
 =theta_n min{x_(n,t,i),1-x_(n,t,i)}
       * |log(x_(n,t,i)/(1-x_(n,t,i)))|.              (7.291)
```

Since every root hazard is at most `S_n`, eventually it is below `1/2` and

```text
e_(n,t,i)=x_(n,t,i) g_(n,t,i).                        (7.292)
```

One Bellman step changes a value coordinate by at most a reward-bound
constant times the row hazard.  Summing around the word therefore gives

```text
max_t |v_(n,t)-v_(n,0)| -> 0.                         (7.293)
```

The first-order singleton expansion of the exact Bellman equation has a
remainder bounded by a constant times the square of the row hazard.  The
remainders over the whole word are `O(S_n^2)`, because
`sum_t q_(n,t)^2 <= S_n^2`.  The cyclic value increments telescope exactly.
After division by `S_n`, this proves the mixture identity in (7.287).

Uniformly in the moving phase index, forced Quit tends to `s_i` and forced
Continue tends to `v_i`.  Hence

```text
g_(n,t,i) -> m_i.                                     (7.294)
```

Equation (7.291), together with `x_(n,t,i)->0`, first gives `m_i>=0`.
If `m_i>m_k`, then for some fixed positive `eta` and every phase eventually,

```text
x_(n,t,i)/(1-x_(n,t,i))
 <= exp(-eta/theta_n)
       x_(n,t,k)/(1-x_(n,t,k)).                       (7.295)
```

Summing over the common phase set shows
`lambda_(n,i)/lambda_(n,k)->0`.  Therefore only minimum-gap labels can carry
positive limiting hazard share, completing (7.287)--(7.288).

For the debt statement, unroll the scalar cap recurrence with the actual
row errors rather than their common upper bound.  If

```text
c_(n,t,i)=product_(j!=i)(1-x_(n,t,j)),
A_(n,i)^-=1-product_t c_(n,t,i),
```

then

```text
d_(n,i)
 <= [sum_t (product_(u<t)c_(n,u,i)) e_(n,t,i)]/A_(n,i)^-.   (7.296)
```

All prefix weights tend uniformly to one.  Equations (7.292), (7.294), and
the finite product expansion give

```text
numerator/S_n -> alpha_i m_i,
A_(n,i)^-/S_n -> 1-alpha_i.                           (7.297)
```

This proves the upper bound in (7.289).  For the matching lower bound, let
player `i` choose Never.  When `alpha_i<1`, the opponents' repeated terminal
law converges to the singleton mixture with weights
`alpha_j/(1-alpha_i)`, `j!=i`; all collisions have period mass `O(S_n^2)`.
Its payoff to `i` therefore tends to

```text
N_i=[sum_(j!=i)alpha_j r_i({j})]/(1-alpha_i).
```

Using the first identity in (7.287),

```text
N_i-v_i=alpha_i(v_i-s_i)/(1-alpha_i)
       =alpha_i m/(1-alpha_i)                         (7.298)
```

when `alpha_i>0`, and it is zero when `alpha_i=0`.
Never is an allowed complete deviation, so (7.298) matches the cap upper
bound and proves (7.289).  If `alpha=e_i`, the same first-order terminal-law
calculation gives convergence to `delta_{\{i\}}`, but (7.297) has vanishing
denominator in the owner coordinate.  This is exactly the isolated-owner
exception.  QED.

### 7.36.2 Relation to the checked hard residual

The aggregate endpoint regret has the exact limit

```text
[sum_(t,i)e_(n,t,i)]/S_n -> m.                        (7.299)
```

Thus the checked returned-block gap does not force terminal debt to vanish.
In the Fin4 no-uniform branch its positive relative-error alternative forces
`m>0`; it is precisely the positive-level system (7.288).  Under punishment
normality, `(alpha,s)` is a normalized singleton source packet, because its
singleton mixture is `v>=s` and every positive-mass target coordinate is the
solo coordinate.  The checked refusal identity then reproduces (7.298)
exactly.  This is a finite algebraic certificate, but not a terminal
consumer.

The positive level is compatible with the maintained Fin4 matrix signs.  For
the checked paired singleton matrix,

```text
M=[ 0  3 -1 -1
    3  0 -1 -1
   -1 -1  0  3
   -1 -1  3  0 ].                                    (7.300)
```

The exact full-support solution

```text
alpha=(1,1,1,1)/4,             m=1/4                  (7.301)
```

satisfies `M alpha=m*(1,1,1,1)`.  This matrix is checked
full-normal-core and homogeneous-LCP-infeasible.  Hence punishment normality,
full normal core, and failure of the
zero-level homogeneous system do not contradict the tropical output; they
select its strictly positive level.

If the first hazard tier is a singleton, (7.295) orders the remaining three
labels by their finitely many limiting gaps `r_j({i})-s_j`.  Conditioning on
the dominant owner Never exposes the next tier.  This is a finite tropical
refusal ordering, but the moving-period collision regression in the reviewed
soft-cycle note shows that punishment normality alone does not consume it.
The checked singleton-tight atomic handoff would apply after global
minimum-fibre anchoring; the Brouwer-selected carrier point does not supply
that field.  Therefore the entropy-weighted strengthening ends at an exact
finite refusal certificate, rather than at another paid port or a proof of
uniform equilibrium.

### 7.36.3 Exact comparison with the existing normalized packets

The conclusion is not a new packet producer in the no-uniform branch.  The
reviewed normal-terminal-gap lift already constructs, directly from the
terminal gap and punishment normality, a **full-support** normalized
singleton packet.  It is stronger in production and support: (7.287) is
conditional on a vanishing-hazard soft-cycle sequence, and its `alpha` may
have proper support.  Likewise the ballistic normalized-flow omega-chain has
actual strict-ray source provenance and retains collision/renewal data which
(7.287) forgets.

What is genuinely stronger than those finite packet fields is the semantic
identification along this particular actual profile sequence:

```text
all occupied singleton surpluses equal the same m,
aggregate endpoint regret / hazard -> m,
actual terminal debt_i -> alpha_i*m/(1-alpha_i),       (7.302)
```

and the lower bound in the last formula is implemented by the literal Never
deviation against the same periodic opponents.  A normalized packet alone
records a mixture inequality and an abstract refusal value; it does not say
that one actual terminal sequence attains that refusal cap, nor does it give
the cap limit.  Thus Theorem 7.36.1 sharpens the **consumer interface** of an
endogenously produced soft escape, but does not supersede either the
full-support producer or the source-attached ballistic relation.

## 7.37 The soft fixed points have one high-to-zero-temperature component

There is a useful global continuation theorem, but it ends at the same
positive-level certificate.  Fix a finite period `H`.  Use inverse
temperature `beta>=0` and define the soft response by

```text
L_i^beta(Q,C)=exp(beta Q)/(exp(beta Q)+exp(beta C)),
L_i^0=1/2.                                             (7.303)
```

Let `T_beta` be the simultaneous map on the root/payoff cube whose root
coordinates are (7.303) and whose payoff coordinates are the cyclic Bellman
map.  This subsection uses the finite-dimensional Browder continuation
theorem in the exact form recorded in
`CODEX_SPINOZA__POSITIVE_MINIMUM_RESPONSE_COMPACTIFICATION_BOUNDARY.md`:
the fixed set of a continuous cube map over a compact parameter interval has
a connected component meeting both parameter faces.  This is ordinary
finite-dimensional topology, not a checked Lean declaration.

### Theorem 7.37.1 (anchored unbounded soft component)

The full fixed set

```text
Z_H={(beta,x,v): beta>=0, T_beta(x,v)=(x,v)}           (7.304)
```

has one connected component containing the `beta=0` fixed point and meeting
every slice `beta=B`.  Hence that component is unbounded in inverse
temperature.

At `beta=0`, every root coordinate is `1/2`.  With this root fixed, one turn
of the Bellman word is an affine contraction with survival factor
`(1/16)^H`; consequently its cyclic value is unique.  Thus the zero-parameter
fixed point is unique.  Apply Browder on `[0,B]`.  Its spanning component must
contain this unique point, and therefore lies in the same component of the
full set (7.304).  Since `B` is arbitrary, that component is unbounded.

For any sequence on this component with `beta_n->infinity`, compactness of
the state cube gives a limiting cyclic state.  Bellman closure passes by
continuity.  The log-odds equation implies the exact root complementarity
conditions: a limiting interior hazard has `Q=C`, a zero hazard has `Q<=C`,
and a sure hazard has `Q>=C`.  Thus every such endpoint is an exact cyclic
Nash--Bellman root.

If at least two distinct player labels have positive hazard somewhere in the
limiting word, every player-deleted period contracts.  The exact cyclic
terminal compiler then makes the repeated word a terminal Nash profile and
gives a uniform-equilibrium payoff.  Therefore, under the hypothetical
no-uniform branch, every zero-temperature endpoint of the anchored component
is either

```text
an isolated-owner exact cycle, or an all-Continue cycle. (7.305)
```

Along an all-Continue endpoint, Theorem 7.36.1 applies to the approaching
component sequence.  If its first tropical tier has at least two players and
level `m=0`, the actual terminal debts tend to zero and again give a uniform
payoff.  The only remaining ends are therefore a positive-level refusal
certificate `m>0` or a singleton first tier, which is the relative isolated-
owner normalization.

This is stronger than applying Brouwer independently at each temperature:
the same connected component is anchored at the unique high-temperature
state and reaches arbitrarily low temperature.  It still does not force a
second, zero-level end of that component.  Component connectedness supplies
neither an orientation at infinity nor a sign distinguishing positive-level
refusal faces.

### 7.37.2 A quitting-specific positive-level end

The missing topological sign is real even with the checked paired singleton
matrix.  Let `pi` swap `0<->1` and `2<->3`.  Put

```text
r_i({i})=0,
r_i({pi(i)})=3,
r_i({j})=-1 for the other two singleton owners j,
r_i(S)=0 whenever |S|>=2.                              (7.306)
```

Its normalized singleton matrix is exactly (7.300), so it has the checked
full normal core and no homogeneous zero-level solution.  The table is also
punishment-normal: all opponents Continuing holds a player to its solo/Never
value zero.

For period one, take the symmetric stationary hazard `q` for all four
players.  The Quit endpoint is zero.  The Continue absorbing contribution,
using the row sum `3-1-1=1`, is

```text
D(q)=q(1-q)^2.
```

The exact Bellman value and Continue endpoint are

```text
v(q)=q(1-q)^3/[1-(1-q)^4],
C(q)=v(q)/(1-q).                                      (7.307)
```

For `0<q<=1/2`, set

```text
beta(q)=log((1-q)/q)/C(q).                            (7.308)
```

Equations (7.307)--(7.308) are exactly the Bellman and logit fixed-point
equations.  This gives a connected symmetric branch from the unique
`beta=0,q=1/2` state to `beta->infinity,q->0`.  At its zero-temperature end,

```text
alpha=(1,1,1,1)/4,      v_i->1/4,      m=1/4.         (7.309)
```

Thus the anchored component itself can carry a positive-level refusal end;
degree and the full-normal/no-homogeneous singleton signs do not turn it into
a zero-level endpoint.  This table has the all-Never terminal equilibrium,
so it is not a counterexample to Fin4.  Its exact role is narrower: a proof
that the soft component has another end must use the global positive-gap or
minimum-fibre data, not continuation topology plus the local hard matrix
signs alone.

## 7.38 Global debt minimization over soft fixed points need not find the minimum fibre

The most favorable selection rule does not repair the missing anchoring by
compactness alone.  Here is a Fin4 obstruction which applies simultaneously
to every finite period and every positive temperature.

Let the no-absorption payoff be zero and define, for every nonempty quitting
coalition `S`,

```text
r_i(S)= 1  if i is not in S,
r_i(S)=-1  if i is in S.                              (7.310)
```

The all-Never profile has prescribed payoff zero.  Against three Never
opponents, player `i` gets `-1` from every finite Quit time and zero from
Never.  Hence its complete cap is zero, all four debts vanish, and the global
minimum total semantic debt is

```text
D_*=0.                                                (7.311)
```

Now take **any** finite-period soft-cycle fixed point at any positive
temperature, and repeat its word forever.  Every root coordinate is strictly
between zero and one.  Consequently, after player `i` switches to Never, at
least one opponent Quits almost surely.  The resulting coalition excludes
`i`, so the literal Never payoff is exactly `1`.  Therefore the complete cap
of every player satisfies

```text
B_i>=1.                                               (7.312)
```

The prescribed repeated word also absorbs almost surely.  If `mu` is its
first-quitting-coalition law, then

```text
U_i=sum_S mu(S)(1-2*1_(i in S)),
sum_i U_i=sum_S mu(S)(4-2|S|)<=2.                    (7.313)
```

Combining (7.312)--(7.313) gives the exact, uniform separation

```text
D=sum_i(B_i-U_i)>=4-sum_i U_i>=2.                    (7.314)
```

This holds for every soft fixed point, not only for the component anchored at
infinite temperature.  Thus a point minimizing total terminal debt over the
whole soft fixed set still has debt at least two for every `H` and every
positive temperature.  Because semantic debt is continuous on the compact
terminal carrier, these semantic pairs stay a positive distance from the
entire zero-debt minimum fibre.

The obstruction is the noncommuting joint-infinity diagram

```text
strictly interior periodic roots --repeat--> almost-sure absorption
              | roots -> all Continue             | no terminal continuity
              v                                    v
          all-Continue roots -----------> all-Never payoff zero.            (7.315)
```

Finite-clock density and upper semicontinuity cannot change (7.314): the
soft fixed-point constraint remains inside the almost-surely absorbing
stratum, whereas the minimizing semantic pair lies on the joint-Never
stratum.  Equivalently, if `F_(H,theta)` denotes the semantic image of the
soft fixed set and `K_*` the minimum fibre, compactness yields anchoring only
after one separately proves the recovery property

```text
inf {D(z): z in F_(H,theta)} -> D_*,
```

or, more strongly, `dist(F_(H,theta),K_*) -> 0`; neither conclusion follows
from density of unrestricted finite-clock profiles.

This table has a uniform equilibrium and `D_*=0`, so it does not refute a
theorem that uses the **positive** minimum essentially.  It does prove the
sharp methodological no-go requested here: selecting the least-debt soft
fixed point, even globally across all components and with `H->infinity`, is
not by itself a source-preserving retraction to the carrier minimum.  Any
positive-minimum version must contribute a new comparison linking soft
fixed points to the supplied minimum source; continuity and selection do not
supply that link.

## 7.39 The two-Never escape contracts exactly to off-minimum or a paid splice

The reviewed period-one two-Never theorem can be passed through one checked
global consumer, but the result is exactly the maintained paid-port waist.
Let `q_n` be its stationary soft root, put

```text
h_n=sum_i q_(n,i)>0,
L_n=ceil(1/h_n),
roots_n(t)=q_n.                                        (7.316)
```

For all large `n`, `h_n<=1/2`.  Form a
`QuittingUniformlyReachedPostMarkTwoCutBlock` using

```text
markedRow=0,  entryCut=1,  exitCut=1+L_n,
hazardFloor=1,  reachFloor=1/2,                        (7.317)
```

and the attained positive global minimum semantic pair.  Every field is
literal:

```text
L_n h_n>=1,
jointReach(0,1)=product_i(1-q_(n,i))>=1-h_n>=1/2,
entryPair=exitPair=the stationary soft semantic pair. (7.318)
```

The last identity holds because the root stream is constant.  Therefore
`QuittingUniformlyReachedPostMarkTwoCutBlock.finFour_offMinimum_or_exists_paidSplice`
gives the exhaustive alternative

```text
D(soft_n)>=D_*+((exp(1)-1)/2)D_*,                     (7.319)
```

or one literal unilateral splice which preserves the source root at date
zero, has suffix gain greater than

```text
(1-exp(-1))D_*/16,
```

and has parent-profile gain greater than

```text
(1-exp(-1))D_*/32.                                    (7.320)
```

The updated payer debt is at most `(1-exp(-1))D_*/16`.  This is a genuine
source-prefix adapter, and it uses the global minimum essentially.  It does
not use the special two-Never identities: periodicity, positive hazard, and
the minimum pair already suffice.  The minimum-selection obstruction in
Section 7.38 explains why (7.319) cannot currently be excluded.

There is also an exact reason the new two-Never data do not directly feed the
checked two-persistent-label uniform-payoff consumer.  Repeating any one soft
word forever gives every player a divergent marginal hazard stream, hence in
particular `HasTwoPersistentQuittingMarginals`.  But its endpoint Nash error
per period is `E_n`, and Theorem 7.36.1 gives

```text
E_n/h_n -> kappa>0.                                   (7.321)
```

Thus the repeated word has an infinite Nash-error ledger.  More generally,
concatenate whole soft periods with multiplicities `a_n`.  Eventually
`E_n>=(kappa/2)h_n`, so

```text
sum_n a_n h_n=infinity
  ==> sum_n a_n E_n=infinity.                         (7.322)
```

Any such schedule carrying divergent total hazard therefore fails the
summable-residual hypothesis.  If two fixed support labels are persistent,
their limiting positive shares already force the left side of (7.322).
Consequently the checked persistent-label theorem supplies its survival
fields but not a `QuittingSummableResidualNashBellmanSpine`.

For `H=1`, the two positive Never limits do yield two same-source cap
replacements after one common root: against stationary opponents every pure
time lies between Quit-now and Never, and the positive limiting refusal gap
makes Never the exact cap for each selected owner for all large `n`.  These
two replacements are siblings.  Applying the first changes the opponent
refusal law seen by the second, and the LCP relations give no sign for that
changed gain.  Hence they do not become successive chronological edges.
The strongest presently checked consequence is exactly (7.319)--(7.320),
not a terminal Nash profile or a renewable two-label spine.

## 7.40 Support two does not lift through the two-player theorem

The zero-share debt conclusion is attached to the original diffuse law and
does not survive the checked two-player existence theorem.  The following
rational regression is stronger than exhibiting one badly chosen reduced
equilibrium: it forces the actual headline proof into its solo-rate branch,
and either possible named payoff target has a fixed outsider obstruction.

Use players `a,b,c,d`.  All unlisted reward coordinates are zero.  On the
support pair put

```text
r_a({a})=r_b({b})=1,
r_a({b})=r_b({a})=3,
r_a({a,b})=r_b({a,b})=0.                              (7.323)
```

For the two outsiders put

```text
r_c({a})=3,     r_c({b})=-1/2,
r_d({a})=-1/2,  r_d({b})=3,                           (7.324)
```

and keep every reward to `c` or `d` on a coalition containing that outsider
equal to zero.  In particular `s_c=s_d=0`.

Let `a,b` independently use stationary hazard `h in (0,1)`, and let `c,d`
play literal Never.  This is an actual Fin4 behavioral profile.  Its
normalized hazard and common-clearance data satisfy

```text
lambda=(1/2,1/2,0,0),
A lambda=(1,1,5/4,5/4),
kappa=1.                                               (7.325)
```

For either outsider `e`, its prescribed payoff equals its literal Never
payoff.  Explicitly,

```text
U_c=U_d=(5/2)(1-h)/(2-h)>0.                            (7.326)
```

If `e` Quits at a reached date, its payoff on every possible coalition at
that date is zero.  A pure Quit time therefore pays a convex combination of
zero and (7.326), while Never pays (7.326).  Hence the complete unrestricted
cap is attained by Never and

```text
debt_c=debt_d=0                                        (7.327)
```

for every `h`, not merely in the limit.  Pure-time extremality includes all
behavioral deviations.

The source also has the correct returned-block asymptotics.  For either
support player,

```text
U_a=U_b=4(1-h)/(2-h),
Q_a=Q_b=1-h,
C_i-Q_i=(2+h)/(2-h),                                  (7.328)
```

so exact stationary Bellman closure holds, total hazard is `2h`, and
aggregate endpoint regret divided by total hazard tends to `1=kappa`.
The support Never debts tend to one.  Thus this is a literal actual-profile
model of the support-two tropical refusal data, including the outsiders'
unrestricted zero debts.

Now restrict the table to `{a,b}`.  Its solo values are positive, so the
zero-solo branch of
`quittingGame_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_twoPlayer` is impossible.  For
either choice of owner and every `p in (0,1]`, the solo-rate inequality is

```text
(1-p)*1+p*0 <= 3.                                     (7.329)
```

Consequently the actual headline proof takes the solo-rate branch and names
either `r({a})=(1,3)` or `r({b})=(3,1)` as its two-player uniform payoff.

This target determines the restricted terminal law.  If `x,y` are the
probabilities of singleton `{a}` and singleton `{b}`, respectively, then

```text
U_a=x+3y,
U_b=3x+y.                                              (7.330)
```

All other reduced outcomes have payoff `(0,0)`.  Hence convergence to
`(1,3)` forces `x->1,y->0`, and convergence to `(3,1)` forces
`x->0,y->1`.  This applies to any terminal approximants used to realize the
named two-player payoff, not only to a displayed stationary equilibrium.

Lift those approximants by making `c,d` Never.  In the first case the lifted
prescribed payoff of `d` tends to `-1/2`; in the second, the lifted prescribed
payoff of `c` tends to `-1/2`.  The bad outsider can Quit at date zero and
receive exactly zero, regardless of the support players' simultaneous
actions, because all coalitions containing it pay it zero.  Therefore

```text
liminf outsider debt >=1/2.                            (7.331)
```

This verifies the obstruction with actual Fin4 profiles and complete
behavioral caps.  The checked deletion naturality preserves the two surviving
coordinates but has no theorem for (7.331); the deleted player's cap is
precisely the missing datum.

The regression has a uniform equilibrium and does not satisfy positive
global minimum debt.  Its conclusion is the exact logical boundary needed
here: the support-two tropical packet plus the outsiders' source-wise zero
debts cannot be combined with the unconditional two-player headline to make
a Fin4 terminal approximation.  A successful cardinal reduction would need
a source-preserving small-player construction or an independent cap bound
for every deleted player at the **lifted** profiles.  Since support two does
not close, no appeal to the support-three theorem is warranted by this route.

## 7.41 Common clearance gives a chronological Never descent, but not a renewable rank

The two-Never output has one stronger source-preserving consequence than the
sibling statement in Section 7.39. It uses only the actual stationary source
and the common-clearance equations, and it recomputes the second response
after the first update.

Let `K=supp(lambda)` and, for every nonempty `A subset K`, let
`sigma_n^A` retain the soft source strategy of each player in `A` and of each
zero-share outsider, while replacing every player in `K\A` by literal Never.
Because `A` is nonempty, these are actual almost-surely absorbing stationary
profiles. Put

```text
Lambda_A=sum_(k in A) lambda_k,
V_i(A)=sum_(k in A) lambda_k r_i({k})/Lambda_A.       (7.332)
```

The singleton-law calculation is unchanged after the finitely many Never
updates, and gives

```text
U_i(sigma_n^A) -> V_i(A).                             (7.333)
```

If `j in A` and `A\{j}` is nonempty, literal Never changes this limit by

```text
V_j(A\{j})-V_j(A)
 = lambda_j * sum_(k in A\{j}) lambda_k A_(j,k)
   / (Lambda_A * (Lambda_A-lambda_j)).                (7.334)
```

This identity is exact algebra: write `V_j(A)` as the convex combination of
the solo value `s_j` and `V_j(A\{j})`, then subtract. Against stationary
opponents every deterministic quitting time lies in the exact envelope whose
endpoints are Quit-now and Never. Hence, whenever the numerator in (7.334)
is positive, literal Never is not merely profitable but is the exact complete
behavioral cap for all sufficiently large `n`.

### Proposition 7.41.1 (at most two chronological cap updates reduce the leading support)

For the period-one tropical source with `kappa>0`:

1. if `|K|=2`, one literal exact-cap Never update reduces the leading hazard
   support to one player;
2. if `|K|=3`, two successive literal exact-cap Never updates reduce the
   leading support to one player; and
3. if `|K|=4`, two successive literal exact-cap Never updates reduce the
   leading support to two players.

The two updates in the last two cases are chronological descendants of the
same source, not sibling profiles.

Indeed, fix any `j in K`. The clearance relation says

```text
sum_(k in K\{j}) lambda_k A_(j,k)=kappa.              (7.335)
```

When `|K|>=3`, not every summand on the left can be at least `kappa`.
Choose `i!=j` with

```text
lambda_i A_(j,i)<kappa.                               (7.336)
```

First replace `i` by Never. Its source gain converges to

```text
lambda_i*kappa/(1-lambda_i)>0,                        (7.337)
```

and Never is its exact source cap for large `n`. At the resulting actual
child, replace `j` by Never. Equations (7.334)--(7.336) show that this second
gain converges to

```text
lambda_j*(kappa-lambda_i A_(j,i))
 / ((1-lambda_i)*(1-lambda_i-lambda_j)) > 0.          (7.338)
```

The same stationary envelope makes the second Never update an exact cap at
that child. This proves the two-step claims. For `|K|=2`, (7.337) alone
proves the one-step claim.

Thus common clearance really does repair the first sibling-to-chronology
type mismatch: it selects an order and proves the second response against the
updated opponents. It does **not** yet make the support descent renewable,
because the first removed player can become profitable again after the
second update.

### Proposition 7.41.2 (hard-matrix-compatible exact reactivation)

The failure of renewal is compatible with every maintained singleton-matrix
condition. Use the checked four-player `duplicatedCyclicMatrix` on
`Option (Fin 3)` and let

```text
K={some 0,some 1,some 2},
lambda_(some k)=1/3,  lambda_none=0,
kappa=1/3.                                            (7.339)
```

On `K` the matrix is

```text
    [ 0 -1  2 ]
A = [ 2  0 -1 ].                                     (7.340)
    [-1  2  0 ]
```

The `none` row and column duplicate coordinate zero. The checked
declarations `duplicatedCyclicMatrix_standardQ`,
`duplicatedCyclicMatrix_noHomogeneous`, and
`normalCore_duplicatedCyclicMatrix_eq_univ` prove respectively standard Q,
homogeneous infeasibility, and full recursive normal core.

Realize this matrix by taking every solo payoff to be one, every singleton
payoff to be

```text
r_i({k})=1+A_(i,k),                                   (7.341)
```

and every nonsingleton payoff to be zero. Let the three players in `K` use
stationary hazard `h`, let `none` use hazard `h^2`, and send `h` to zero.
These are literal actual stationary profiles with exact Bellman returns. The
terminal law tends to the uniform lottery on `K`, every coordinate payoff
tends to `4/3`, each support owner's Never cap tends to `3/2`, and the
zero-share outsider's complete debt tends to zero. Moreover

```text
endpointRegret/totalHazard -> 1/3=kappa.              (7.342)
```

Thus the profiles carry the full tropical semantic and cap output, not only
an abstract matrix solution. For sufficiently small `h`, each support
owner's source cap is literal Never.

Now make any first support owner `i` Never. Of the two remaining support
owners, exactly one `j` sees the last owner `k` through the positive entry
`A_(j,k)=2`; this is the unique profitable second Never direction in
(7.334). But the cyclic sign pattern then forces

```text
A_(i,k)=-1.                                           (7.343)
```

After the second Never update, the terminal law is asymptotically singleton
`k`. The first removed player receives `1+A_(i,k)=0`, whereas Quit at date
zero tends to its solo payoff one. Its reactivation gain therefore tends to
one. In particular, no pair of the three leading owners can be removed by
this two-step cap rule while leaving both removed labels solved.

This regression has a uniform equilibrium and does not have positive global
minimum debt. It therefore does not refute a renewal theorem using that
global field essentially. It proves the narrower exact no-go: common
clearance, two source-coherent Never caps, unrestricted cap formulas, full
normal core, standard Q, and homogeneous infeasibility do not make leading
support cardinality a monotone response rank. The positive theorem is the
finite chronological descent (7.337)--(7.338); the remaining missing datum is
a positive-minimum mechanism that charges or forbids the order-one
reactivation (7.343).

## 8. What this proves and what it does not

The aggregate lemma closes one specific loss in the paid orientation:

\[
\text{diffuse supported source clocks}
\not\Rightarrow
\text{vanishing total causal mass}.
\]

The complete source law or the near-cap response law always carries a fixed
finite atom, and exact cap descent preserves its debt-normalized mass.

It does **not** show that the cluster in (3.4) lies at the global minimum,
that the killed coordinate survives a later horizontal player response, or
that a positive finite terminal-law atom occurs at one uniformly bounded
stage.  It therefore supplies neither a terminal Nash profile nor a complete
renewable rank.  The exact new residual is narrower.  The prescribed arm is
a same-law reset-rigid neutral source.  The response arm is either the same
kind of reset-rigid source or a killed-owner singleton/Never law.  In every
branch the positive atom has a nonresetting normalized passport.

The later compactness calculations also do not refute an enriched
hierarchical boundary which separates finite ties escaping to infinity from
joint Never.  They prove that the ordinary stopping-law compactification and
a one-anchor common-delay quotient are insufficient, and that any enrichment
making all pure-time counterfactuals jointly continuous cannot remain a
sequentially compact metric state without a new, nonuniform decoder.

The capacity-shared genealogy calculation adds a separate finite obstruction.
Even exact, positively charged, commonly sourced children with the same exit
need not admit a common exact parent.  Its constant-spectator Bellman
invariant is exact, but the example has minimum debt zero.  Hence it isolates
the source-ancestry/connector hypothesis which a positive-minimum theorem
would have to produce; it is not a counterexample to such a theorem.

The full-debt minimax calculation is global across the four response
coordinates but still stops before an actual profile.  Its Pareto arm gives
a genuine correlated terminal law strictly inside the frozen debt box; its
balanced arm gives an exact response occupation; and its separator arm gives
only a response-local social normal.  None supplies the product-law/cap
component of a terminal semantic pair.  The rational regression in (7.236)
proves that every listed local full-debt field allows exact occupation
cancellation.  Its zero true minimum is essential to the scope.

The temporal-mixing calculation then uses the missing global minimum
comparison directly.  A revealed public lottery preserves average
unrestricted debt, while the original quitting game cannot reveal a
nonabsorbing public phase.  Private randomization creates product cross
vertices, and deterministic calendars replace convex weights by
survival-weighted chronological products with phasewise caps.  Consequently
the balanced occupation is not a terminal consumer.  What would suffice is
a supplied guarded periodic Nash--Bellman cycle with a positive absorption
budget and source ancestry; producing that cycle remains open.

The chattering calculation rules out the infinitesimal version of the same
repair.  At a full-debt minimum, every coordinate has a strictly positive
Continue-over-singleton moat throughout `[U,B]`.  A small positive Quit
hazard therefore incurs root-Nash error proportional to its absorption, and
periodic repetition turns this into an order-one unrestricted improvement.
An `O(h)` continuation correction cannot change that first-order coefficient;
exact root re-equilibration requires an order-one semantic collar excursion.
